## Story & Purpose Auditor

### efigure1.png

---
**MAIN CLAIM:** At a fixed design bandwidth ($W=4/N$), all five smoothing kernels have essentially the same main-lobe width, but their out-of-band skirts differ by tens of dB — so bandwidth alone does not characterize a smoother's leakage.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the visualization carries the shape comparison, but several load-bearing pieces live only in the caption or an external table:
- "then box" in the legend is unexplained; a reader cannot tell that these are periodograms subsequently box-smoothed, nor that this is the matched-bandwidth construction.
- $W$ is never marked on the axes; the dotted vertical lines in (b) at $\pm4$ are the box edges, but without the caption they read as unexplained guides (the box's "roof" at 0 dB is invisible, so the legend entry "ideal box" maps to two bare vertical lines).
- The equivalent degrees of freedom ($\nu$) — arguably the *payoff* of the comparison — appear only in the caption and Table~\ref{tab:dof}, not in the figure or legend.
- The two panels use different $x$-ranges ($\pm10$ vs $\pm24$) with no visual cue; a reader scanning left-to-right will assume they are co-registered.
- The headline "40 dB" is asserted in prose but not annotated anywhere on (b).

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — overlaid response curves vs. frequency offset is the canonical and correct display for comparing filter/kernel leakage, and pairing a linear main-lobe view with a dB skirt view is standard. One refinement: panel (a)'s job (main lobes coincide) is a small, local fact that would be served equally well by an inset on panel (b) or by a zoomed dB view, freeing the figure of a full second panel.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Enormous dead whitespace between (a) and (b) (roughly a third of the canvas), which visually implies a missing third panel.
- Panel (a)'s $y$-axis extends to 0 with most of the informative structure compressed into the top 15% of the range; the tick set (0.00–0.10) conveys little.
- The Bohman curve in (b) is clipped at the $-100$ dB floor and vanishes off-axis, leaving a broken trace that looks like a plotting artifact rather than a statement about dynamic range.
- Fine multitaper ripple in (a) is visually busy without supporting the claim (the ripple's real consequence is visible in (b)).
- The legend spans the full figure width in two rows with inconsistent column ordering (dotted box, multitaper | untapered, Hann | Bohman), making curve→label matching slow.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — panel (a) is largely contained within the central region of panel (b) (all methods flat at 0 dB across the passband), and the $\nu$ values in the caption duplicate Table~\ref{tab:dof}. If the manuscript has a main-text figure making the same bandwidth-vs-leakage tradeoff, this whole figure is a natural supplement item (as its "efigure" name suggests it already is).

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Bohman curve is clipped at the axis floor and disappears; either raise the floor, annotate the clipping, or state the dynamic-range limit so the gap is not read as missing data.
- [BLOCKING] "then box" is undefined in the figure; the legend must be self-describing (e.g., "Hann periodogram + box smoother") or the construction indicated with a small schematic.
- [ADVISORY] Dark blue (multitaper) and light blue (Bohman) are the two curves whose skirts most need distinguishing in (b); they are the most confusable pair in the palette. Recast one as a distinct hue or use dash patterns.
- [ADVISORY] $\pm W$ is not labelled on either axis, so the "same design bandwidth" premise is not visible.
- [ADVISORY] The $\nu$ values, which quantify the variance cost of the leakage advantage, are absent from the graphic.
- [ADVISORY] Panel $x$-ranges differ without indication; no shaded region or bracket links (a)'s span to (b).

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Close the inter-panel gap and move the legend into panel (b)'s empty upper-left region, or into a single compact column at right.
- Annotate the claim directly: a vertical double-headed arrow with "≈40 dB" spanning the untapered and Bohman skirts at a representative offset (e.g., $10/N$).
- Append $\nu$ to each legend entry (e.g., "multitaper, $K=7$ ($\nu=14.0$)"), so the leakage/variance tradeoff is readable without the table.
- Mark $\pm W$ with light vertical rules and an axis tick label, and shade the passband in both panels so the matched-bandwidth premise is visible at a glance.
- Either raise panel (b)'s floor to $-90$ dB so all curves remain on-axis, or add a "floor" annotation where Bohman exits.
- Consider demoting panel (a) to an inset in (b) (linear main lobe, $\pm6/N$) and reclaiming the width; this makes the figure one panel, halves the reader's eye travel, and removes the differing-$x$-range hazard.
- Clip panel (a)'s $y$-axis to the region of interest (e.g., 0.11–0.14) with a broken-axis indicator, or plot the main lobe normalized to the box height so the deviations are legible.

### efigure2.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Smoothing an untapered (or fully-tapered-set) spectral estimate imports strong leakage bias into the high-frequency floor, and this bias is largely removed by discarding the leakiest (7th) Slepian taper or by pre-tapering with a Hann window.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the figure's headline comparison is not visually resolvable. The dashed blue "K = 6" trace is completely hidden under the solid blue "K = 7" trace in both panels, so the reader cannot see the effect announced in the title ("Dropping the leakiest taper") or the caption's claim that dropping the 7th taper "halves" the bias. Several other quantities the caption leans on (ν values, the box-smoother bandwidth, what "then box" means) exist only in the caption. Panel (b)'s claimed floor levels (20 dB, 10 dB, 5 dB) must be read off a noisy oscillating band rather than from any annotation.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate (with reservations) — overlaid log-power spectra plus a difference panel is the right idiom for "establishing a bias relationship across frequency." The reservation is that the estimates retain so much realization-to-realization jitter (±7 dB swings in blue and orange) that the *systematic* bias, which is the actual claim, is buried in variance. Consider (i) plotting the expected value / average over many realizations, or (ii) overlaying a bold running median on each light raw trace, or (iii) an inset showing the difference between K = 7 and K = 6 alone.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Grey raw periodogram in (a): consumes the most ink, extends the y-range by ~20 dB, and plays no role in the claim (it is not a smoothed estimate). Either drop it or draw it at low alpha behind everything.
- Full vertical extent of the noisy blue/orange bands in (b): the jitter is not the message; only the level is.
- Panel (b) y-axis extending to 30 dB when no trace exceeds ~19 dB.
- The dashed-blue legend entry, which currently promises a curve the reader cannot find.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — panels (a) and (b) are the same data in two encodings; (b) alone supports the claim, and (a) functions mainly as orientation. Also likely overlaps a main-text figure showing the same AR(4) realization and estimator set; as an extended-data figure the only *new* content is the K = 6 vs K = 7 contrast, which is precisely the element currently invisible.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The K = 6 trace is occluded by K = 7, so the figure does not show the phenomenon named in its own title/caption. As drawn, the figure cannot support "dropping the seventh taper halves that."
- [BLOCKING] Quantitative claims in the caption (20 dB, 10 dB, floors "halved") are not readable from the axes; the reader must trust the text. Either annotate the floor levels or restate them as approximate.
- [ADVISORY] "then box" and the ν values are undefined within the figure; a reader scanning figures out of order will not know the smoother bandwidth or the equivalent degrees of freedom trade-off being invoked.
- [ADVISORY] Green trace ends at ~18.5 dB, not 20 dB; caption wording should match the plot or say "≈20 dB".
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) omits the green untapered-box curve at low frequency behavior detail and the black true PSD is heavy enough to hide the well-matched estimates near the peaks, making the "they all agree where there is no leakage" half of the story hard to verify.
- [ADVISORY] Legend is a 3×2 block detached from both panels; with six entries and two near-identical blue styles, matching is slow.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Give K = 6 a distinct hue (or plot K = 7 and K = 6 in separate stacked sub-panels of (b)); alternatively add a third panel plotting only (K = 7 bias) − (K = 6 bias) so the "halving" is a single curve.
- In (b), draw raw traces at ~25% alpha and overlay a bold running median per estimator; add faint horizontal guides at 0, 5, 10, 20 dB and direct-label each curve at its right-hand terminus instead of using the legend.
- Delete or heavily de-emphasize the grey periodogram in (a); tighten (b)'s y-limits to about −10 to 20 dB.
- Annotate the box-smoother bandwidth (and ν for each curve) directly on the panels as small inline labels so the variance/bias trade-off is visible without the caption.
- Rewrite the caption's second sentence to describe what the eye can confirm (ordering of floor levels) and move the precise dB figures to a small table or to text, or add the annotations that make them readable.
- If space is tight, retain only panel (b) in the main figure and move panel (a) to the supplement, since (b) carries the claim.

### efigure3.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** When methods are compared at *equal* measured resolution, no estimator dominates: multitaper variants buy high equivalent degrees of freedom (low variance) at the cost of orders-of-magnitude more spectral leakage and much higher side-lobe/skirt levels than tapered-periodogram-plus-box smoothing, while Welch/Gaussian smoothing sit at the extreme high-variance-reduction, high-leakage corner.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No —
- The three y-axis labels for panels (b) and (c) are **clipped and/or overprinted onto the neighbouring panel's axis region** ("kernel power beyond 2× half-power half-width" and "peak beyond 1.5× half-power half-width (dB)" collide with panels (a) and (b)). This is the single largest readability defect.
- Only panel (a) tells the reader which direction is good ("higher = less variance"). Panels (b) and (c) have no polarity cue, so a reader scanning the figure cannot tell that *down* is better there — which is exactly what makes the trade-off story visible.
- The grey dotted vertical line is unlabelled; its meaning (the 8/N operating point used in the main table) exists only in the caption.
- Curves terminate at different x-values (Hann→Gaussian stops near 10; Welch extends to ~25; others to ~16) with no visual explanation, which reads as missing data rather than as parameter-range limits.
- The caveat that the Gaussian and Welch entries in (c) measure a main-lobe skirt, not a true side lobe, is caption-only, yet it changes how those two curves should be interpreted.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate, with a reservation — Parameter-swept line plots against measured half-power width are the right choice for "establishing a relationship at matched resolution," and small multiples sharing the x-axis are well suited. However, the *headline* message is a trade-off between two of the plotted quantities, and the reader must currently perform that cross-panel comparison mentally. Consider adding (or promoting) a fourth panel plotting leakage (b) against degrees of freedom (a) directly — a trade-off curve per method, with resolution as the along-curve parameter. That single panel would state the main claim without eye-movement between axes.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Eight series × three panels is at the upper limit of legibility; the two Slepian variants (K = 2NW−1 vs 2NW−2) differ only marginally in (a) and could be collapsed to one, with the other moved to the supplement or shown as a shaded band.
- "multitaper, K = 2NW−2" and "Bohman periodogram, then box" use visually similar blues (dark-blue square vs light-blue square) — the shared square marker adds confusion rather than information.
- x-axis range extends to 25 although only one series reaches beyond ~16, leaving ~35% of each panel's width nearly empty.
- Nine decades of y-range in (b) with minor tick marks; the minor ticks contribute ink without being read.
- Repeated identical x-axis label three times; one shared label would suffice.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — Panel (a) at the dotted 8/N line reproduces the degrees-of-freedom column of Table \ref{tab:dof}; the figure's added value is the *sweep*, not the single operating point. Fine to keep, but the caption should say explicitly that the table is one vertical slice of this figure, and the table should not repeat leakage/side-lobe numbers that are readable here.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] y-axis labels of panels (b) and (c) are truncated/overlapping adjacent axes; panel (b)'s label text is cut at the left edge. Figure is not publication-legible as rendered.
- [BLOCKING] No polarity cue on (b) and (c); the reader cannot infer the trade-off direction without the text.
- [ADVISORY] Unlabelled dotted vertical reference line in all three panels.
- [ADVISORY] Unequal curve extents with no explanation; may be misread as missing or failed computations.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (c) mixes two physically different quantities (side-lobe peak vs main-lobe skirt) on one axis with no in-figure distinction.
- [ADVISORY] Colour/marker collision between the two square-marker blue series.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Increase inter-panel spacing (or use `constrained_layout`/`tight_layout`) and shorten y-labels to two lines, e.g. "kernel power beyond\n2× half-power half-width" and "side-lobe / skirt peak (dB)\nbeyond 1.5× half-width"; verify no text is clipped at the figure boundary.
- Append a polarity hint to each y-label: "(higher = less variance)", "(lower = less leakage)", "(lower = better rejection)". Optionally add a small arrow annotation "better ↓".
- Label the dotted line in-panel once, e.g. "8/N (Table 1)", rather than only in the caption.
- Clip the x-axis to the populated range (≈1–17) and either show Welch's extension in a break/inset or note "Welch continues to 25/N" as an in-panel annotation.
- Distinguish the two square-marker blues: give Bohman a distinct marker (e.g. open diamond) or a clearly different hue.
- In panel (c), mark the Gaussian and Welch curves as skirt measurements — e.g. plot them with open markers and add a legend note "open markers: main-lobe skirt, no side lobe" — so the caveat lives in the figure.
- Consider adding a trade-off panel (equivalent dof on x, leakage on y, one parametric curve per method) to carry the main claim directly; if space is tight, this could replace one of the two near-identical multitaper series.
- Replace three identical x-axis labels with a single shared label beneath the row.

### efigure4.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Four superficially different spectral estimators (Welch averaging, periodogram smoothing, sliding-sinc, Thomson multitaper) are all expressible as the same quadratic form, and once diagonalized they produce nearly identical Slepian-like eigen-tapers — differing only in how weight is distributed across the ladder, which in turn sets the effective degrees of freedom ν, the kernel width, and the out-of-band leakage.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the visual architecture (4 methods × 4 diagnostics) reads cleanly, and the panel titles carry most of the argument, but three symbols essential to the claim are never defined in the figure: **Q** (rows 2, appearing as "eigen-tapers of Q"), **ν** (row 3 titles), and **W** (row 4, "beyond 2W", plus the shaded band). A reader who lands on this figure first cannot tell what Q is, what ν measures, or what the blue shading means. The row-to-row logical flow (bank → eigen-tapers → weights → kernel) is also implicit; nothing on the figure states that rows 2–4 are *derived from* row 1.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — this is a comparison-across-methods problem solved with a small-multiples matrix, which is the right instrument. Offset line plots for taper shapes, bars for the weight ladder, and dB-log kernels are each the conventional and correct choice. Shared y-limits within each row (notably 0–0.4 in i–l and −80–0 dB in m–p) make the cross-column comparison legitimate. One caveat: the offset line stacks in rows 1–2 have no y scale, so they communicate *shape* only; that is acceptable for this claim but should be acknowledged.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Panel letters (a)–(d) collide with the third line of the column titles; in (c) and (d) the letter is printed over the word "bank". Same overlap risk for (m)–(p) against the two-line kernel titles.
- Legend box in (b) sits on top of the k = 0 / f′ = 0/N trace; in (d) the "k = 1" label is overwritten by a curve.
- Legends repeated four times in row 2 (e–h) when one legend, or a single shared legend outside the grid, suffices. Row 1 also repeats a legend in (b) and (d).
- "sample t" repeated 8 times; "taper index k" 4 times; "frequency offset (units of 1/N)" 4 times. Bottom-row-only labelling would recover space.
- Roughly 40% of the plot area in (i)–(l) is empty (x-axis runs to ~12, bars stop at 7 in two panels; y-axis to 0.4 when the max bar is 0.30).
- Duplicated y-axis tick labels on inner columns of rows 3–4.
- The word "eigen-tapers of Q, k = 0..3" repeated verbatim in (e), (f), (g).

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — the caption self-identifies this as "Fig. banks in full", i.e. it is already the expanded companion to a main-text figure, which is the correct place for it. The genuine redundancy is *internal*: panels (g) and (h) are, by the paper's own argument, the same functions, so showing both duplicates ink to make a point that would land harder as an annotation ("identical to (g)") or an overlay. Additionally, the six scalars in the titles of rows 3–4 (ν, kernel width, % beyond 2W, ×4 columns) are tabular data currently scattered across eight panel titles; a compact 4-row summary table would let a reader compare them without eye-scanning.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Q, ν, and W are load-bearing for the claim and appear nowhere in the figure or caption as definitions. Add at minimum a one-clause gloss in the caption ("Q, the quadratic-form kernel matrix of the estimator; ν, effective degrees of freedom; W, the analysis half-bandwidth").
- [BLOCKING] Panel letters (a)–(d) overlap column titles, and legends in (b) and (d) overlap data traces. This is a production defect that will be flagged at proof stage.
- [ADVISORY] No row labels. The four diagnostics are the figure's argument structure and should be named once on the left margin rather than re-announced in every panel title.
- [ADVISORY] The colour scheme switches meaning between rows: rows 1–2 encode taper index k, rows 3–4 encode method/column. Nothing signals the switch.
- [ADVISORY] Panels (e)–(h) look nearly identical at print size, which *is* the result, but the figure does not help the reader see it. The convergence is asserted by juxtaposition rather than demonstrated.
- [ADVISORY] Row 1 panels (b) and (d) do not obviously show "a bank" in the same sense as (a) — (a) shows spatially separated windows, (b) shows modulated tapers. The visual analogy across the row is weaker than the titles imply.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Move panel letters outside the axes (upper-left of the axes box, above the title block) and relocate legends to a single shared legend per row, placed outside the grid.
- Add left-margin row labels: "the bank", "eigen-tapers of Q", "eigen-weights", "kernel". Then strip the repeated boilerplate from panel titles, keeping only the column-specific numbers (ν, width, % beyond 2W).
- Define Q, ν, and W explicitly in the caption's first sentence, and state in-figure what the blue shading is (e.g. a small "|f| ≤ W" text label inside one shaded band).
- Tighten (i)–(l): set x-limits to the actual support (0–12 is fine if fixed, but drop to 0–11 and set y-limit to 0.32) so the bar-height differences — the real signal — occupy more ink.
- To make the convergence claim visible rather than merely implied: overplot the Slepian eigen-tapers as thin dashed grey curves behind (e), (f), (g), so the reader *sees* three methods collapsing onto one basis. Then (h) can be dropped or reduced, and the near-identity of (g) and (h) becomes an annotation instead of a duplicated panel.
- Label bottom-row x-axes and left-column y-axes only; remove the 24 redundant axis labels.
- Consider promoting the six summary scalars into a small table adjacent to the figure (or as the final column), so ν, width, and leakage can be read as a ranking rather than hunted across titles.

### figure1.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Three superficially different constructions — eigenvalue-weighted all-taper multitaper, box-smoothed untapered periodogram, and the average of sliding sinc-window periodograms — yield the *same* PSD estimate, exactly in the first case and to a $1/L$-vanishing error in the second.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the visual logic (overlay → residual → convergence) reads cleanly, but three things cannot be recovered without the caption: (i) in (a) the green and dashed-purple traces are hidden under the thick grey band, so the reader cannot verify agreement, only infer it from absence of visible deviation; (ii) in (b) the reader has no way to know the green trace at $10^{-14}$ dB is floating-point roundoff rather than a small real discrepancy — this is the crux of the "exactly equal" claim and it is invisible; (iii) in (c) neither the anchoring of the $1/L$ guide nor the fact that the last point corresponds to the pink trace in (b) is indicated.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — the panel sequence matches the argument type (establishing equivalence, then quantifying residual, then characterizing its scaling). Line overlay for (a), residual-on-log-axis for (b), and log–log convergence for (c) are the right choices. One structural note: (a) is a "nothing to see" panel by design; its persuasive work is done entirely by (b). If space is tight, (a) could shrink to a small inset of (b).

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Three-line prose legend under (a) — this is caption text placed inside the figure; the entries are sentences, not labels.
- Full 0–40 Hz spectral detail in (a) (the alpha peak, harmonics, the rolloff) is irrelevant to the equivalence claim; it serves only to show the input is real EEG.
- Panel (b)'s decade ticks span 15 decades, most of which are empty white space between the two traces.
- Duplicate legends in (b) and (c) restate series already named in (a)'s legend.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Low–Medium — panel (c)'s rightmost point is, by construction, the maximum of panel (b)'s pink trace, so (b) and (c) share an anchor point; and (b)'s two summary numbers ($3\times10^{-13}$, $0.06$ dB) are the only quantities a reader will retain from the panel, which a one-line table entry could carry. The figure as a whole is not redundant — the equivalence is the paper's point — but (a) is the weakest-earning panel.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel (c)'s y-axis label is clipped/collides with the "(c)" panel tag at the top-left; the label text and panel identifier overlap. Fix before submission.
- [BLOCKING] The $1/L$ guide line does not match the data slope: the pink series falls by roughly a factor of 24 over a 16-fold increase in $L$, i.e. steeper than $1/L$. Either the guide is mis-anchored, or the caption's "$1/L$" claim overstates what the panel shows. As drawn, the figure argues against its own caption.
- [ADVISORY] The green trace in (b) sits at the double-precision noise floor. Without an annotated "machine precision" line or shaded band, a skeptical reader cannot distinguish "algebraically identical" from "very small bias."
- [ADVISORY] Green vs. pink/purple is a risky pair under deuteranomaly; in (a) the distinction is additionally carried only by linestyle under a thick grey band.
- [ADVISORY] "power (dB)" in (a) is dB relative to an unstated reference; the absolute level is uninterpretable.
- [ADVISORY] Only five points in (c), the minimum for a convincing scaling claim; no indication whether $L/N$ values are the only ones tested.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Move the (a) legend content into short on-curve annotations ("multitaper", "smoothed", "sliding avg, $L=16N$") and relegate the descriptive sentences to the caption; delete the (b) and (c) legends and label the two traces in (b) directly.
- In (b), add a horizontal dotted line labelled "double precision ($\sim10^{-14}$ dB)" behind the green trace, and clip the y-axis to roughly $10^{-15}$–$10^{-1}$ to remove empty decades.
- Re-fit or re-anchor the guide in (c): plot the fitted slope with its exponent annotated (e.g. "slope $=-1.1$") alongside the $1/L$ reference, or revise the caption to state the observed scaling is at least as fast as $1/L$.
- Fix the (c) axis-label/panel-tag collision; consider a rotated label on two lines ("max |average −\\ multitaper| (dB)").
- Shrink (a) to roughly half its current width (its only job is "the curves lie on top of each other") and give the freed space to (c), which carries the quantitative claim.
- Add the reference level to the (a) y-axis label, e.g. "power (dB re 1 $\mu$V$^2$/Hz)".

### figure2.png

---
**MAIN CLAIM:** Every spectral estimate is the true spectrum convolved with the estimator's kernel plus noise, so taper choice controls leakage (kernel skirts) and smoothing width trades variance against resolution — with an intermediate box width ($2W\approx$ peak width) being "just right."

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the small-multiple layout, the informative column titles ("leaky and noisy" → "still noisy" → "just right" → "too smooth"), and the top-row overlay of estimate on truth do carry most of the story unaided. But several encodings are undecodable without the caption:
- The short blue bar above the peaks in (c)/(d) is unlabeled, and it is *absent* in (a)/(b) — the reader cannot tell whether that means "no kernel width" or "not drawn," which undercuts the cross-column comparison the figure exists to make.
- $\nu$ appears in every top title with no gloss on the figure; "equivalent degrees of freedom" lives only in the caption, yet it is the quantitative carrier of the variance half of the argument.
- "beyond 2W" in (e)–(h) titles presumes $W$ is defined; for (e) and (f) no box smoothing exists, so $W$ is silently something else (half-power half-width). This is the one genuinely confusing conceptual gap.
- The (a) legend lists "peak width 7/N" and "separation 30/N" as grey swatches, but no corresponding marks are legible in the panel; the legend promises annotations the reader cannot find.
- In (e), kernel (grey line/fill) and true spectrum (grey fill) are the same hue, so the two most important objects in the panel are hard to separate — whereas (f)–(h) get the orange/grey contrast.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — this is simultaneously a comparison across four estimators and a mechanism explanation (kernel → estimate). Small multiples with a fixed y-scale, paired rows, and the truth redrawn in every panel is the right construction; the vertical pairing of estimate above its causal kernel is the figure's strongest design decision. No alternative would do better.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Four repetitions of "frequency (cycles per sample)" and four of "frequency offset (units of 1/N)"; one label per row (or a centered label) suffices.
- Repeated y tick labels are fine, but the duplicated axis *titles* are pure ink.
- The heavy grey fill under the Fejér kernel in (e) down to −80 dB adds mass without information; a line (as in f–h) would match the other panels and reduce ink.
- Panel letters are inconsistently placed and in (g) the letter collides with the title text.
- The legend in (e) ("true spectrum (shape only)") could be replaced by a single direct label in one panel.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Low — (b)/(f) is not redundant with (c)/(g); it isolates the taper effect from the smoothing effect, which the argument requires. The only tabulable content is the pair of scalars per column ($\nu$, % beyond $2W$), and putting them in titles is more effective than a table would be. Keep all eight panels in the main text.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The blue kernel-width bar appears only in (c)/(d). The figure's comparative claim requires the equivalent mark in (a) and (b); its absence reads as missing data.
- [BLOCKING] "$2W$" is used in (e)/(f) where no smoothing box exists and $W$ has not been defined for those estimators. Define $W$ on-figure or relabel (e)/(f)'s metric.
- [BLOCKING] Legend entries in (a) for "peak width 7/N" / "separation 30/N" have no visible counterpart in the panel.
- [ADVISORY] Panel label (g) overlaps its title; label placement is inconsistent across the eight panels.
- [ADVISORY] (e) uses grey for both kernel and true spectrum, breaking the colour logic established in (f)–(h).
- [ADVISORY] $\nu$ is unglossed on the figure.
- [ADVISORY] Top row shows a single realization, so "plus noise" is asserted by one draw; the $\nu$ values carry the real evidence but are easy to overlook.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Draw the half-power-width bar in all four top panels (grey in a/b to match their colour coding), and add a one-word in-panel label such as "kernel width" beside the bar in (a) only.
- Add "$\nu$ = equiv. DOF" once, e.g. under the (a) title or in the (a) legend.
- Replace "beyond $2W$" in (e)/(f) with an explicit definition of $W$ for untapered/tapered cases, or annotate $W$ with a tick on the (e)/(f) x-axes so the quantity is visible, not inferred.
- Either draw the peak-width and separation bars visibly in (a) (e.g. two horizontal grey bars just under the peaks with the values as text) or delete those legend entries.
- Keep one x-axis title per row; delete the three duplicates in each row.
- Render the (e) kernel as an unfilled line and keep grey fill exclusively for "true spectrum," so the grey = truth / coloured = kernel convention holds in all four lower panels.
- Move all panel letters to a uniform outside-upper-left position to clear the titles.

### figure3.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: The box-smoothing half-width $W$ must be chosen to balance falling noise against rising kernel bias, so total RMS error has a broad minimum whose location shifts to smaller $W$ when only the spectral peak region is scored (panel b); panel (a) makes a separate, weaker point about what Hann tapering does to the record.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — Panel (b) is close to self-reading thanks to the "too noisy / just right / too smooth" annotations and the two dotted reference lines, but three items block a caption-free read: (i) the symbol $\nu$ (equivalent degrees of freedom) appears nowhere in axes, legend, or even the caption; (ii) the orange circles are unlabeled cross-references to another figure, so the reader cannot tell what they mark; (iii) "RMS error vs true PSD (dB)" is ambiguous — is it error *of* the estimate *relative to* the true PSD, in dB units of the PSD, or a ratio? Panel (a) is legible but its relevance to "choosing the bandwidth" is not evident from the visualization alone — nothing in (a) refers to $W$.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate (b) / Mismatch in framing (a) — Panel (b) is a textbook relationship/decomposition plot and the line-with-markers choice is right; overlaying the two error components with their sum is exactly the bias–variance story. Panel (a) is a time-series illustration of a *process step* (tapering), not of bandwidth choice; it belongs with the taper discussion (or with Fig. motif, whose record it reuses) rather than in the bandwidth figure. If (a) must stay, retitle it so the two panels share one claim, e.g. frame (a) as "what the taper costs: effective record length is shortened," which would then motivate the $W$ axis in (b).

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- The dashed Hann envelope in (a) is drawn as both $+$ and $-$ branches and "scaled to the record's peak amplitude"; an arbitrary vertical scaling adds ink without adding information. One branch, or a shaded envelope, suffices.
- The grey untapered record and the orange tapered record overlap heavily in the middle, where the two are nearly identical; the grey trace mostly adds density.
- Long legend text in (a) ("Hann taper, scaled to the record's peak amplitude") could be a short in-axes label.
- Legend entries in (b) restate the annotations ("noise: standard deviation of the estimate", "bias: blur by the kernel") that could be direct in-line labels on the blue and orange curves, freeing the legend block entirely.
- Marker dots on the two black curves add little where the curves are dense; they matter only at the circled points.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — Panel (a) plots the same record as Fig. motif, so the grey trace is a repeat; only the taper overlay is new. Panel (b) is unique and load-bearing. The dashed "peak region" curve is a second scoring rule of the same experiment and could be relegated to the supplement if the narrative does not turn on it.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] $\nu = 2, 9, 50$ is used as the label for the three circled operating points but is never defined in the figure or the caption; the reader cannot connect $\nu$ to $W$ or to the smoothing.
  - [BLOCKING] The y-axis label "RMS error vs true PSD (dB)" does not state what is averaged over what; specify that it is the RMS over frequency of $10\log_{10}(\hat S/S)$ (or equivalent), averaged over 300 realizations.
  - [BLOCKING] The orange circles are unexplained inside the figure; the cross-reference ("panels b, c, d of Fig. motif") lives only in the caption, and a reader scanning figures will not resolve it.
  - [ADVISORY] The figure carries two claims (what tapering does; how to choose $W$), which weakens the title "Choosing the bandwidth."
  - [ADVISORY] The caption's "no error bars are shown because the curves are ensemble averages" is a non-reason — ensemble averages have a standard error. Either report the Monte-Carlo scatter or say it is smaller than the line width.
  - [ADVISORY] The x-axis unit "box half-width $W$ (units of $1/N$)" would read more naturally as "$NW$" or "$W$ in Rayleigh units", and the connection to the two dotted lines (peak width, peak separation) should be stated as $2W$ consistently on both.
  - [ADVISORY] Blue (noise) and black (total) are indistinguishable for $W \lesssim 4$, hiding the fact that noise dominates there; that overlap is actually part of the claim and should be made visible.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Move panel (a) into the taper/motif figure (or the supplement) and let this figure be the single-panel bias–variance plot; retitle it "the bandwidth trade-off."
  - Add a short in-figure gloss for $\nu$, e.g. "$\nu$ = equivalent degrees of freedom" as a small note, and add the caption sentence defining $\nu$ in terms of $NW$.
  - Label the three circles with the panel letters they point to (e.g. "Fig. 1b") directly in the figure, so the cross-reference survives without the caption.
  - Replace the (b) legend with direct curve labels: "noise" on the blue curve, "bias" on the orange, "total" near the black solid curve, "total, peak region only" near the dashed curve.
  - Make the shared-region distinguishable: slightly offset or dash the blue curve where it coincides with black, or annotate "noise-dominated" / "bias-dominated" on either side of the minimum.
  - Tighten the y-axis label to something unambiguous, e.g. "RMS log-spectrum error (dB)", and state the averaging in the caption.
  - Shade the broad minimum (e.g. the $W$ range within 0.1 dB of the minimum) to make the "broad" claim in the caption visible rather than asserted.
  - If (a) is retained, drop the grey untapered trace or plot it in a thin light line behind a shaded taper envelope, and remove the arbitrary amplitude scaling note from the legend.

### figure4.png

---
**MAIN CLAIM:** Summing the eigenvalue-weighted Slepian spectral windows reproduces the ideal box of half-width $W$ exactly for all $N$ tapers (and approximately for the standard $K=2NW-1$), which is why the multitaper estimator has a box-like smoothing kernel — with panel (d) justifying the $K\approx 2NW$ cutoff.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the visual logic is mostly legible (a: tapers → b: their windows tile the band → c: their sum fills the box), but three things require the caption: (i) that panel (c) is the *exact* identity, not an approximation; (ii) that $K=7$ is the practically used truncation (nothing in (c) marks which curve is the "recommended" one); (iii) the $N=256$, $W=4/N$ parameters that make the "units of $1/N$" axis and the value 8 in (d) interpretable. Additionally, overlapping text (below) blocks reading of panels (a) and (b) without external help.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — (a)–(c) are functions of a continuous variable shown as line plots, which is correct; the vertical-offset stack in (a) is the right idiom for shape comparison; (d) is a decay-over-index curve on a log axis, correct for spanning $10^{-11}$ to $10^{-1}$. The figure is explanatory (a process/derivation), and the left-to-right, top-to-bottom build matches that purpose.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Duplicate discrete legends in (a) and (b) for the same $k$ colour map; one shared colourbar or direct end-of-curve labels would replace ~14 legend entries.
- Panel (a)'s y-axis label "taper (offset)" plus an unticked axis — the axis line itself carries no information and could be dropped.
- Panel (b) is largely subsumed by (c): the "filling the gaps" message is visible in the running sum. It is pedagogically nice but is the weakest earner of space.
- Panel (c)'s six-entry legend where only three curves matter to the claim ($K=1$, $K=7$, all $N$).
- Panel (d) plots up to $k=16$ although the curve is flat after $k\approx 9$; the flat tail is dead ink.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — (b) and (c) tell overlapping stories (individual windows vs. their cumulative sum), and (d) states a single scalar fact ("$1-\lambda_k$ becomes $O(0.1)$ at $k=2NW$") that could live in the text or a small inset. Panel (a) is standard textbook material likely shown elsewhere in the manuscript or assumed known.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Text collisions: in (a) the x-label "sample t" is overprinted by the legend; in (b) the x-label "frequency offset (units of 1/N)" is overprinted by the legend and the y-label "(unit-norm tapers)" collides with the panel title. These must be fixed before submission.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (c) does not visually distinguish the operationally relevant case $K=2NW-1=7$ from the other partial sums; the caption's main quantitative point ("rounded shoulders and small ripple") has no visual anchor.
- [ADVISORY] Inconsistent taper range across panels: (a) shows $k=0\ldots5$, (b) shows $k=0\ldots7$, (c) steps 1,3,5,7,9, (d) goes to 16. A reader cannot tell whether these are the same set.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) has no vertical scale reference, so the reader cannot see that tapers are unit-norm (a fact panel (b) depends on).
- [ADVISORY] In (d), the orange dashed line at $2NW=8$ and grey dotted at $K=7$ are one sample apart and hard to separate at print size; the legend order also lists $2NW$ before $K$, opposite to their left-to-right position.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (c) y-axis "kernel (unit area)" peaks near 0.125 = $1/(2NW)$ in these units; this connection is invisible.
- [ADVISORY] Large white gap between the top and bottom rows wastes ~15% of the figure area.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Fix the layout: move legends outside the axes or replace them with a single shared $k$ colourbar spanning (a)–(b); reserve space for x-labels.
- In (c), draw the $K=7$ curve in a heavier stroke with an in-panel annotation ("standard choice $K=2NW-1$") and label the black curve "all $N$ tapers = exact box"; consider adding a small inset showing the shoulder/ripple region magnified, since that is the quantitative claim.
- Harmonise the taper index range: show the same $k=0\ldots7$ set in (a) and (b), and truncate (d) at $k\approx 12$.
- Add an in-panel annotation to (b) such as "windows tile the band $|f|\le W$" and to (a) a scale bar or "unit norm" note, so each panel states its own message.
- If space is tight, demote (a) and (b) to a single combined panel (tapers as small multiples with their windows beneath) or move (a) to the supplement, keeping (c) as the figure's centre of gravity with (d) beside it.
- Consider annotating (d) directly with the two vertical lines' meanings instead of a legend, and shading the region $k\ge 2NW$ as "leakage only".

### figure5.png

---
**MAIN CLAIM:** Welch averaging, periodogram smoothing, and sinc-window sliding are all implicitly taper banks like Thomson's multitaper, and the flatness of each method's eigen-weight ladder (bottom row) is what sets its effective degrees of freedom ν — with the equally weighted Slepian bank being the flattest/most efficient reference.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the top row is readable as "different taper banks," and the bottom row as "different weight distributions," but three things cannot be recovered without the caption: (i) that ν is computed *from* the ladder (the causal link between rows is the whole point and is nowhere stated on the figure); (ii) that panels (b)–(d) show only 4 of a larger/continuous set while (a) shows all 7 members (the reader will read the differing curve counts as data); (iii) what the vertical offsets in the top row mean, since the y-axis has no ticks and is labeled only "amplitude (curves offset)". Panel letters (c) and (d) physically collide with their titles, and the legends in (b)–(d) overprint the topmost curve.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — small multiples of line plots for "how the bank is constructed" and bar charts for the discrete weight ladder is the right pairing for a comparison-of-constructions figure. One caveat: the four ν values are currently the punchline but live as text in four separate subplot titles; a single small fifth panel (or inset bar) comparing ν across the four methods would convert the reader's mental arithmetic into a direct visual comparison.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- "eigen-weight ladder:" repeated verbatim in four titles (e–h) — state once as a row label.
- Four different arbitrary bar colors (pink/orange/green/blue) that encode nothing and do not match the viridis curve colors above them.
- Empty x-axis span to k≈12 in (e) and (h) where only 7 bars exist; unlabeled, so the reader must infer that emptiness = K.
- Repeated "sample t" / "taper index k" x-labels on all four columns.
- Top y-axis tick marks with no values (ink without information).
- Legend boxes duplicated across (b), (c), (d) where (c) and (d) use the same k = 0..3 keys.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — the caption states that a companion figure (`fig:banksfull`) "adds the eigen-tapers and kernels," i.e., this figure is a strict subset of another one. Either this is the main-text digest and the full version belongs in the supplement (state that explicitly), or the two should be merged; as drafted, a reader encounters the same four banks twice.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel labels (c) and (d) overlap their titles, and legends in (b)–(d) overlap the k=0 / f'=0 curve. Both are production-quality defects.
- [BLOCKING] The row-to-row logic (ladder flatness → ν) is not expressed graphically or textually inside the figure; without the caption the two rows read as unrelated.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (c)'s top curves are legended "k = 0..3" — the same key as the Slepian panel (d) — while the title says "a sinc window at every offset." A reader cannot tell whether (c) shows sinc windows at four offsets or four eigen-tapers, and the caption's remark that (c)'s eigen-tapers *are* Slepians makes the ambiguity worse.
- [ADVISORY] Inconsistent number of curves shown per panel (7 in a, 4 in b–d) with no on-figure note that b–d are truncated samples of a continuum/larger set.
- [ADVISORY] ν values are reported to 1 decimal in titles but the differences (11.1 vs 17.2) are the quantitative claim; no uncertainty or normalization context (e.g., ν for the ideal K=7 flat bank = 14.0) is flagged as the reference.
- [ADVISORY] Bottom-row x-limits differ in effective content (7, 12, 10, 7 bars) but are drawn on the same axis range — good — yet K is never annotated.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Move panel letters to a dedicated left-aligned position above each axes (or inside the top-left corner of the axes) so they cannot collide with multi-line titles; shorten titles to two lines by moving the parenthetical parameters into the caption.
- Relocate legends to the lower-right of each top-row axes, or replace them with direct end-of-curve labels ("k=0", "f'=1/N") at the right edge; a single shared legend for the viridis order would suffice if the color→index mapping is made uniform.
- Make the color mapping consistent and meaningful: use the same viridis color for taper/offset index k across all top panels, and color each bottom-row bar set to match its column rather than using four arbitrary hues.
- Add an explicit link between rows: a short row label on the left such as "bank members (4 of K shown)" and "weights c_k → ν = 2(Σc)²/Σc²", plus an arrow or bracket annotation, so the reader sees that flat ladder = large ν without the caption.
- Annotate K on each bottom panel (e.g., "K = 7 nonzero") and, if the empty axis space is meant to convey the differing bank sizes, add a light guide or explicitly note it.
- Add a compact fifth element — a single horizontal bar or dot plot of ν for the four methods, with the flat-Slepian value marked as reference — to make the quantitative punchline visible at a glance.
- Disambiguate (c): state in the title/legend what the four drawn curves are (e.g., "sinc window at offsets t₀ = ...") and drop the k index there, or show the sinc window at offsets in the top row and reserve k for the eigen-taper figure.
- Decide the division of labor with `fig:banksfull`: if that figure is a superset, move it to the supplement and say so in this caption; if both are main text, merge them into one figure with an extra row.
- Add value ticks (or a scale bar) to the top-row y-axis, or drop the ticks entirely and label the axis "amplitude (arb. units, curves vertically offset)".

### figure6.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** Among four common spectral estimators matched to the same resolution bandwidth, the shape of the effective smoothing kernel — specifically how much energy leaks beyond $\pm W$ — determines whether the estimate tracks the true PSD or floors tens of dB too high, with Hann-based kernels (c, g) winning and the untapered box (a, e) failing worst.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the architecture is right (method-per-column, estimate on top, kernel below, matched colors), but three of the load-bearing quantities live only in the caption or are unlabeled.

- The small dark-blue square sitting above the peak in every top panel is never explained; it also appears wedged into the (a) legend next to "true PSD," which reads as a rendering glitch rather than a data element. A reader cannot tell if it marks $W$, the half-power width, or a stray marker.
- $\nu$ appears in all four top titles as the implicit "fairness" variable but is never expanded (equivalent degrees of freedom). Since the whole comparison rests on matching resolution/variance, this symbol must be legible without the text.
- The median absolute errors (11.6, 4.6, 1.3, 0.8 dB) are the single cleanest quantitative summary of the claim and they are buried in prose. Put them in the panel titles alongside $\nu$.
- In the bottom row, the gray "true spectrum (shape only)" is drawn on a *frequency offset* axis while the spectrum itself is a function of absolute frequency. What frequency is offset zero? The gray shape implies the kernel is centered somewhere specific, but the reader cannot tell where, and the second gray peak near $+30/N$ suggests a particular placement. This is the most confusing element in the figure.
- Titles mix parameterizations: (a) and (c) state $W=4/N$, (b) states $NW=4$, (d) states neither. These are the same bandwidth but a reader must infer that.

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — this is a mechanism-plus-comparison figure, and the paired small-multiples layout (outcome above, cause below) is exactly the right structure; the column-wise color binding does real work. One caveat: the top row is "one realization," which makes the outcome evidence anecdotal for a claim about estimator bias. Consider overlaying the ensemble mean (or a shaded 10–90% envelope over realizations) on top of the single realization, so the reader sees that the 20 dB floor in (a) is systematic bias rather than one unlucky draw.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Four repetitions of "frequency (cycles per sample)" and four of "frequency offset (units of 1/N)"; one label per row suffices with shared axes.
- Redundant y-tick labels on columns b, c, d in both rows (axes are shared).
- Top-row y-range extends to $-40$ dB though no curve goes below $\approx-25$; bottom-row extends to $-80$ though panel (e)'s kernel never falls below $-36$. Tightening reclaims ink for the region where the methods actually differ.
- The dense sidelobe hash in (f) is at risk of moiré/aliasing at print size; it reads as a texture rather than a measurement.
- The in-axes legends in (a) and (e) compete with the curves near the peak; a single shared legend outside the grid would be cleaner.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — the caption says "(e--h) the kernels, as in Fig.~\ref{fig:motif}," and panel (a) is described as "the exact estimate \eqref{eq:identity}," both of which indicate this figure is partly re-presenting material established earlier. The four MAE numbers plus four $\nu$ values plus four leakage percentages are also a 4×3 table trying to escape; if the kernels have already been shown in the motif figure, one option is to keep the top row (the payoff) in the main text with the numbers annotated, and move (e--h) to the supplement. My recommendation is to keep both rows — the cause/effect pairing is the argument — but then trim the motif figure's kernel panel instead, so the kernels are introduced once and exploited once.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The dark-blue square marker above each peak in (a--d) is undefined and appears corrupted in the (a) legend.
- [BLOCKING] $\nu$ is used in all four top titles without definition anywhere in the graphic.
- [BLOCKING] Offset zero and the placement of the gray "true spectrum (shape only)" in (e--h) are unspecified, so the kernel-versus-spectrum comparison cannot be read.
- [ADVISORY] The headline quantitative result (median absolute error per method) is caption-only.
- [ADVISORY] "One realization" undercuts a bias claim; no ensemble information is shown.
- [ADVISORY] Caption says "purple"; the plotted color reads pink/magenta. Also confirm the four hues survive grayscale printing and common color-vision deficiencies — green (a/e) versus orange (c/g) is the risky pair.
- [ADVISORY] Bandwidth stated three different ways across the four titles.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (g)'s kernel exits the bottom axis at roughly $\pm18/N$ with no indication that it continues below; a clipped-curve arrow or an axis break would prevent misreading it as truncated data.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Either delete the blue square markers or add an explicit annotation, e.g. a horizontal bar under the peak labeled "$2W$," which would directly connect the top row to the shaded $|f|\le W$ band below.
- Rewrite top titles as a single consistent template: "untapered + box, $NW=4$, $\nu=17.2$, MAE $=11.6$ dB". Same template for all four columns.
- In (e--h), mark offset zero with a light vertical line and state in the y-label or a corner annotation that the kernel is centered at the spectral peak (or wherever it is centered); consider annotating the gray shape once in (e) only.
- Collapse repeated axis labels to one per row; drop duplicate tick labels on shared axes; crop top row to $[-30, 50]$ dB and bottom row to $[-70, 5]$ dB.
- Add a faint ensemble envelope behind the single realization in (a--d), with the legend entry "10–90% over 200 realizations," so the 20 dB floor is visibly systematic.
- Add a horizontal dashed guide at the true-PSD floor level in (a) and (b) to make "floors 20 dB high" and "floors 10 dB high" readable straight off the plot.
- Coordinate with the motif figure so the kernels are introduced in exactly one place.

### figure7.png

---
**MAIN CLAIM:** The three everyday spectral estimators (Thomson multitaper, smoothed Hann periodogram, Welch averaging), matched in resolution and degrees of freedom, produce the same EEG spectrum to within each estimator's own sampling noise, with the residual disagreement concentrated in the inter-harmonic valleys.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the overlay in (a) and the difference trace in (b) read well, and the legends carry the parameters and median |diff| values, so a reader gets "three methods, nearly identical." But the load-bearing part of the claim — *within their own noise* — is nowhere on the graphic. Panel (b) shows differences of ±5–12 dB at some frequencies with no reference for how large a difference is expected by chance, so an unguided reader is as likely to conclude "these methods disagree by up to 10 dB" as the intended conclusion. The explanation for the valley spikes (seventh taper side lobes) is also caption-only. Additionally the panel (b) y-axis label is clipped at the top of the canvas ("difference from multitaper (dB" with the closing paren cut).

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — overlaid spectra plus a difference panel is essentially a Bland–Altman/Tukey mean-difference treatment, which is the right idiom for "do these estimators agree?" Panel (b) is what does the work; (a) provides the context that all three track the same harmonic structure. No change of type needed, only annotation.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Full method specifications inside the legends ("K = 7 Slepian tapers, equal weights", "9 Hann segments of 1.5 s, step 0.81 s") — three lines of dense text competing with the data; short labels (multitaper, smoothed, Welch) with ν values suffice, details belong in the caption.
- Median |diff| values embedded in the panel (b) legend duplicate the caption text; they would be better as small in-panel annotations next to the relevant band or left in the caption only.
- The blue multitaper curve is largely hidden beneath the pink Welch curve in (a); the third curve adds ink without adding discriminable information at this line width.
- The heavy black zero line in (b) draws more attention than the data; a thin gray line is enough.
- Axis range to 40 Hz where all three curves and both differences are visually indistinguishable — the last ~10 Hz is low-information but harmless; keep if the flatness there is part of the argument.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Low–Medium — panel (b) is a derived view of panel (a), so the two panels overlap in content, but the derivation is the point and the differences are invisible in (a). Risk is mainly that the numeric summary statistics appear three times (legend, caption, presumably body text). If the manuscript already shows a similar three-method overlay elsewhere (e.g., the abstract figure referenced in the caption), panel (a) could shrink to an inset or move to the supplement, leaving (b) as the main-text figure.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The central claim ("agree to within their own noise") is not testable from the graphic. Panel (b) lacks any representation of the expected noise level (the 1.6 / 2.1 / 1.5 dB figures from the caption), so the reader cannot judge whether ±5 dB excursions are expected or anomalous.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (b) y-axis label is clipped by the figure boundary; also the (b) label collides with it.
- [ADVISORY] Caption calls the Welch curve "purple" but it renders as pink/magenta; the two difference curves in (b) reuse the (a) colors correctly, but the color-name mismatch will confuse.
- [ADVISORY] The interesting structure — differences concentrated in the valleys between harmonics — is asserted in the caption but not marked on the figure; nothing directs the eye to the correspondence between (a)'s troughs and (b)'s spikes.
- [ADVISORY] Legends sit below the panels and are wider than the data area, pushing the effective data-ink fraction down; in (a) the legend block is roughly as tall as a third of the plot.
- [ADVISORY] Blue is occluded by pink across much of (a), so "three estimates" reads visually as two.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Add a shaded band in (b) at ±the expected difference noise (e.g. ±1.6 dB for smoothed-minus-multitaper, ±1.5 dB for Welch-minus-multitaper, or a single band at the combined $4.3\sqrt{2/\nu}$ value) with a short in-panel label such as "expected sampling noise." This single change makes the title claim readable without the caption.
- Fix the clipping: increase top/left margins so the (b) y-label and the "(dB)" close paren are fully visible, and offset the "(b)" panel letter.
- Shorten legends to "multitaper (ν = 14.0)", "smoothed (ν = 8.9)", "Welch (ν = 17.6)" and move tapers/segments/step into the caption; place the legend inside the upper-right white space of (a) if it fits.
- Mark two or three inter-harmonic valleys with light vertical guides or small arrows spanning both panels, so the valley→spike correspondence is visible; or add a faint gray copy of the (a) spectrum behind (b) on a secondary axis.
- Increase the multitaper line width or draw it last/dashed so all three curves are distinguishable in (a); alternatively plot (a) with a small vertical offset per curve and say so in the caption.
- Correct "purple" to "pink" (or recolor to actual purple) in the caption.
- Consider replacing the paired difference traces with a single panel showing both differences plus the noise band, and demoting panel (a) to a narrow inset, if space in the main text is tight.
---

### figure8.png

---

**MAIN CLAIM:** For a real seizure recording, multitaper estimation and simple post-hoc box-smoothing of the Hann periodogram produce essentially interchangeable spectrograms (median absolute difference ≈1.5 dB), both far less noisy than the raw periodogram.

**SELF-SUFFICIENT:** No — the architecture is readable (raw trace → three estimators → spectral slices → difference distribution), but several links must be supplied by the caption:
- Panel (h)'s x-axis label is **cut off** ("multitaper minus Hann-then-box (d"), so the reader cannot tell the units or that it is a per-pixel difference between (e) and (g).
- The blue and orange dashed lines in (a) are never keyed in-figure; the reader must infer that blue = 247 s (d) and orange = 318 s (f). The white dashed lines in (c,e,g) are likewise unlabelled.
- Panel (d) has no legend; the four-curve key exists only in (f). A reader scanning top-down hits (d) first and sees four anonymous coloured traces.
- The single colourbar sits beside (e) with no visual cue that it governs (c) and (g) too.
- ν (degrees of freedom) appears in five titles but is never defined on the figure; without it, the numbers 2 / 4.8 / 6 / 7.7 are opaque and the whole variance-reduction argument is invisible.
- Orange is doing double duty: the ictal-marker/waveform colour in (a,b) and the "Hann, then box" estimator colour in (d,f).

**CHART TYPE:** Appropriate — time series for the raw signal, spectrograms for time–frequency change, overlaid line spectra for estimator comparison, histogram for the difference distribution. Two caveats: (i) the 10–45 dB colour scale **saturates** the ictal band to flat yellow in all three spectrograms, i.e. exactly where the estimators are being compared, so (c/e/g) cannot support the comparison visually and the burden falls entirely on (d,f,h); (ii) (h) collapses all spatial structure — a difference *image* (multitaper − Hann-then-box, diverging colour map) would show *where* the two disagree, with the histogram as an inset or a single sentence in text.

**NOISE ELEMENTS:**
- Panel (b): the 6-s ictal waveform is atmospheric context, not evidence for any estimator claim; it is a candidate for removal or for the supplement.
- Repeated "frequency (Hz)" axis labels on (c,e,g) and repeated 0/10/20/30 ticks — could be labelled once per column.
- Three near-identical spectrograms occupy ~50% of the figure area yet, under the saturating colour scale, differ only in fine-grain speckle; much of that ink is not carrying the claim.
- The "median |diff| 1.5 dB" panel title carries the key quantitative result but reads as a caption fragment rather than a panel title.

**REDUNDANCY RISK:** Medium — (c), (e), (g) are visually almost indistinguishable at this reproduction size, and (h) plus the (f) overlay already establish their equivalence numerically; two of the three spectrograms could move to the supplement, or the trio could be replaced by one spectrogram plus one difference map. The caption also states the colour scale is shared with a second EEG figure, raising the possibility that the same spectrograms recur there.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel (h) x-axis label is truncated; the figure's headline quantitative axis is unreadable.
- [BLOCKING] Colour scale saturates (clips at 45 dB) across the entire ictal region, so the three spectrograms cannot visually support the equivalence claim they are meant to demonstrate.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (d) has no legend and the dashed time markers in (a) and (c,e,g) are unkeyed; the cross-panel correspondences are caption-dependent.
- [ADVISORY] ν is used as the organising quantity throughout but never defined in-figure.
- [ADVISORY] Orange encodes both "ictal epoch/waveform" and "Hann-then-box estimator" — recolour one.
- [ADVISORY] Single colourbar's scope over three panels is not signalled.
- [ADVISORY] The histogram discards the spatial location of estimator disagreement.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) does not contribute to the main claim.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Fix the clipped (h) label; make it read e.g. "multitaper − Hann-then-box (dB)" and annotate the median with a vertical line plus text rather than in the title.
- Either raise the colour ceiling (or use a percentile/robust scale) so the ictal band is not flat yellow, or add explicit "clipped" hatching/annotation and state that the comparison rests on (d,f,h).
- Replace one of (c,e,g) with a diverging difference map (multitaper − Hann-then-box) on a symmetric ±8 dB scale; demote the third spectrogram to the supplement. Keep (h) as a small inset on that map.
- Move the four-curve legend to (d) (first occurrence) or place one shared legend outside the panel column; recolour "Hann, then box" so orange is reserved for the ictal marker.
- Add small in-figure labels to the dashed lines ("pre-ictal 247 s", "ictal 318 s") in (a) and in one spectrogram, and place the colourbar centred on the spectrogram column with a bracket or explicit "(c,e,g)" note.
- Define ν once on the figure (e.g. "ν = equivalent degrees of freedom; larger ν = smoother, lower-variance estimate") so the variance-reduction ordering is legible without the text.
- Consider dropping (b), or shrinking it to an inset on (a), to free space for the difference map.

---

### figure9.png

---
MAIN CLAIM: With degrees of freedom held roughly comparable, multitaper and box-smoothed-periodogram spectrograms render each seizure's descending harmonic stack essentially identically and far more legibly than the raw Hann periodogram, whose variance buries the pattern in noise.

SELF-SUFFICIENT: No — The panel titles carry most of the load well (estimator, window, ν all on-figure), and panel (a)'s title explains the shading, which is good practice. But three things stop a text-free read: (1) the seizure shading in (a) is not carried down into (b–d), so the reader must eyeball vertical correspondence to know which spectrogram features are the seizures; (2) "ν" is never expanded on-figure — a reader who does not already know it means equivalent degrees of freedom cannot grasp that the comparison is variance-matched, which *is* the claim; (3) the ±500 µV clipping in (a) is invisible (the trace visibly flat-tops near the limits but nothing says so). Also, the descending 20→3 Hz harmonic stack — the feature the whole figure is about — is never marked; the caption asks the reader to find it unaided.

CHART TYPE: Appropriate — The purpose is a like-for-like comparison of estimators on the same time-frequency data, and vertically stacked, time-aligned heatmaps with one shared colour scale is the correct instrument. The raw trace on top is well justified as ground truth. One caveat: the shared scale is saturating — large regions of (c) and (d) below ~5 Hz and inside the seizures are flat yellow at the 45 dB ceiling, so the two "identical" estimators may look identical partly because both are clipped. Consider raising the ceiling, or adding a difference panel (c)−(d) in dB to substantiate "render it identically" quantitatively rather than by visual assertion.

NOISE ELEMENTS:
- Panel (a) is drawn on a wider x-extent (~0–620 s) than panels (b–d) (0–600 s) and its axes box does not align with theirs; the apparent alignment is therefore slightly false and the extra width is pure decoration.
- Tick labels on the top three panels' time axes are suppressed (good), but panel (a) retains a full axis box/spine that adds nothing.
- The colourbar is vertically centred across (b)–(c) only, leaving it visually orphaned from (d) despite applying to all three; the large blank margin to its right is wasted space.
- 30 Hz upper limit with ticks only at 0/10/20/30 is fine, but the dense 5-dB colourbar ticks are finer than anything the reader can judge by eye.

REDUNDANCY RISK: Medium — The caption says "Spectrograms as in Fig. X," so this is the same three-estimator comparison applied to a second clip. If the earlier figure already established that smoothing beats the raw periodogram, this figure's only new content is "and it also works when there are two seizures with a descending harmonic stack." That is a legitimate but modest increment. Either (i) sharpen the unique payload — the harmonic stack and its descent — with annotation so the figure makes a claim the first figure cannot, or (ii) move it to the supplement as a replication and keep one worked example in the main text.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Seizure intervals are shaded only in (a). Add the same shading (or bracketing bars along the top of (b)) to the spectrogram panels so the reader can locate the events without cross-panel eyeballing.
  - [BLOCKING] The claimed feature — the harmonic stack descending 20→3 Hz — is unmarked. Without a thin arrow or dashed guide along the fundamental in one panel, the figure cannot deliver its own claim.
  - [BLOCKING] Colour saturation at the 45 dB ceiling in (c) and (d) confounds the "renders it identically" claim; identical clipping is not identical estimation.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (a) x-extent and axis box do not match (b–d); fix to a common time axis so vertical reading is exact.
  - [ADVISORY] "ν" is undefined on-figure; a one-word gloss ("ν = 2 dof") in the panel titles or colourbar area would make the variance-matching argument self-evident.
  - [ADVISORY] The ±500 µV clip in (a) is undisclosed on-figure.
  - [ADVISORY] Colourbar is not aligned to the full span of the panels it governs.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Propagate the seizure shading into (b–d) as translucent overlays or as two solid bars in a thin strip above panel (b).
  - Annotate the first seizure in panel (c) or (d) with a dashed curve or a pair of arrows tracing the fundamental from ~20 Hz to ~3 Hz, labelled "harmonic stack, descending"; leave the second seizure unmarked so the reader can verify the pattern themselves.
  - Raise the colour ceiling until (c)/(d) are unsaturated inside the seizures, or switch to per-panel-identical limits chosen from the 99.5th percentile of all three panels, and state the clipping fraction if any remains.
  - Force a shared x-axis across all four panels (matplotlib `sharex`) and remove panel (a)'s redundant right spine and extra width.
  - Add a small fourth spectrogram panel showing (d)−(c) in dB on a diverging scale; this converts "render it identically" from an assertion into a visible result and would make the figure clearly non-redundant with the earlier example.
  - Extend the colourbar to span (b) through (d), or place it as a short horizontal bar beneath the stack.
  - Gloss ν on-figure, e.g. "Hann periodogram, 2-s windows: ν = 2 dof".
  - Note "trace clipped at ±500 µV" directly in panel (a), near the y-axis label.

## Composition & Layout Inspector

### efigure1.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Good — two-spine (L-shaped) axes, no frame, no gridlines, no fills or shadows, no 3D. The dotted ±W verticals in (b) are genuine data ink (reference marks), not junk. Only real waste is the large unmarked canvas between the two panels.
READING FLOW:     Problematic — the eye goes (a) → long empty gap → (b) → then must travel back down-and-left to the legend, which is centered under the void rather than near either panel. The legend's 3-column layout also reads across the gap, so the mapping from line style to curve requires two long saccades per series.
CHARTJUNK:
  - None ornamental; but the detached 5-entry legend functions as lookup overhead rather than information.
  - Redundant duplicated x-axis label text ("frequency offset (units of 1/N)") appears twice; unavoidable with separate scales, but note the axis ranges differ (±10 vs ±24), which readers may mistake for the same window.
WHITE SPACE:      Excessive — roughly 35–40 % of the 2172 px width is empty canvas between panels; each panel is compressed to ~25 % of the figure width while a time-series-like trace would benefit from more horizontal extent.
AXES:             OK — (a) linear kernel axis begins at 0, correct. (b) dB-relative-to-peak necessarily tops at 0 and is negative-going; this is field-standard for spectral leakage and needs no defense. Two minor points: (a) has no tick at the plateau value (~0.125), so the reader cannot read the main-lobe height without interpolating; and the −100 dB floor truncates the Bohman skirt (the light-blue curve exits the axis) without any indication that it continues below.
ERROR BARS:       Not present — appropriate; these are deterministic kernels, not estimates. (The ν values quoted in the caption are the statistical content and are absent from the graphic.)
PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — bold "(a)"/"(b)", both top-left. (a) sits noticeably further from its axes than (b) sits from its own; tighten to equal offsets.
ASPECT RATIO:     Needs adjustment — individual panels are near-square, which is defensible, but the composite 3.5:1 canvas is driven entirely by dead space. Collapsing the gap would let each panel grow ~50 % in width, resolving the fine multitaper ripple in (b) that is currently near the ink-width limit.
ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — no callouts or text boxes; the ±W markers are thin dotted reference lines, exactly the Tufte-preferred substitute. Consider a small inline "±W" tick label in (b) so the lines are self-explaining.
LEGEND STYLE:     Box — detached 5-entry legend below and between the panels, far from both sets of curves. It does not overlap data (not blocking), but it imposes the full lookup cost on every curve in both panels.
ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Detached legend on a multi-line chart with only five series; all five are directly labelable at their right-hand endpoints in (b), where the curves are well separated (untapered ≈ −27 dB, multitaper ≈ −43 dB, Hann ≈ −88 dB at x = +24).
  - [ADVISORY] Excessive interior white space forces a long horizontal jump between panels and shrinks both plots.
  - [ADVISORY] Two blue hues — "multitaper" (medium blue) and "Bohman" (light blue) — are hard to separate in panel (a), where both hug the plateau edge; this is the one place the reader most needs to tell them apart.
  - [ADVISORY] Bohman curve runs off the bottom of (b) with no clipping indicator, implying the trace simply ends.
  - [ADVISORY] Main-lobe plateau height in (a) (~0.125 = 2W in units of N) has no corresponding y tick.
  - [ADVISORY] Differing x-ranges between (a) and (b) are not visually cued; a reader may assume a shared abscissa.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Delete the legend and label the four computed kernels inline at their right endpoints in panel (b), each in its own series color, with short thin leader lines where the curve is steep; label the dotted trace directly as "ideal box" beside one of the ±W verticals. This removes the lookup entirely and reclaims the bottom strip.
  - Close the inter-panel gap to ~5–8 % of figure width and let both panels expand; target an overall canvas nearer 2:1 rather than 3.5:1.
  - Recolor Bohman to a non-blue hue (or make multitaper a distinctly darker/thinner stroke) so the two blues are separable in (a); verify in grayscale.
  - Add a y tick at the plateau value in (a), or annotate it inline as "2W" at the plateau.
  - Either lower the (b) floor to −110 dB or add a small break/arrow marker where the Bohman curve leaves the axis.
  - Add a light shaded or bracketed cue in (a) indicating the ±10/N extent as a subset of (b)'s ±24/N range — or simply annotate (b) with "same kernels, wider range".
  - Put the ν values from the caption into the graphic as small inline text next to each endpoint label (e.g. "Hann, ν = 8.9"); this merges the caption's statistical payload with the visual comparison.
  - Equalize the (a) and (b) panel-label offsets.

### efigure2.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Good — no gridlines, no bounding boxes, no fills or shadows; spines reduced to left/bottom only; tick density is reasonable. Minor redundancy: the "frequency (cycles per sample)" label is repeated on both panels (defensible here since the panels are far apart, but it would be unnecessary if the panels were adjacent). The legend key swatches are the only non-data ink of consequence.

**READING FLOW:** Problematic — the reader must scan (a) → (b) left-to-right, then drop to a bottom legend that is read *down columns* (periodogram → untapered; then K=7 → K=6; then Hann → true PSD) rather than across, which is the opposite of the expected left-to-right sweep. Every series identification requires a jump to the bottom of the figure and back.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- None decorative. The only avoidable element is the detached 6-entry legend block.

**WHITE SPACE:** Excessive and unevenly distributed — there is a ~700 px dead gap between panel (a)'s right edge and panel (b)'s left edge (roughly a third of the figure width carries no ink). Panel widths are also unequal ((a) ≈ 700 px of plotting width vs (b) ≈ 480 px), so the two x-axes share the same 0–0.5 domain at different scales, which impedes direct visual comparison. Panel (b) additionally wastes vertical space: the axis runs to +30 dB while the data top out near +18.5 dB.

**AXES:** Mostly OK — dB (log) units justify the non-zero origins in both panels; panel (b) is a difference quantity with an explicit black zero reference line, which is correct practice. Issues: (i) unequal horizontal scaling of the identical 0–0.5 frequency domain across panels; (ii) panel (b) upper limit (+30) is ~11 dB above the largest datum; (iii) the zero reference line in (b) is drawn in the same heavy black used for "true PSD" in (a), inviting misreading as a data series.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — equivalent degrees of freedom (ν) are quoted in the caption for every estimator but no uncertainty is rendered in the figure. Since the entire argument is about bias vs. variance, this is a substantive omission rather than a formatting one.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — bold (a)/(b), both at top-left, matched size and weight.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate for (a) (wide, suits a spectrum); (b) is unnecessarily narrow-and-tall relative to (a) given it plots the same abscissa. Harmonize.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — none present.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box — detached, below both panels, six entries, column-major reading order. It does not overlap data, but it imposes a full lookup cost for every curve. With six series, four of which are mutually overlapping noisy traces, full inline labeling is not realistic; partial inline labeling is (see fixes).

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The central claim of the figure — "dropping the seventh taper halves the floor" (K=6, dashed blue) — is not visually recoverable. In both panels the dashed blue K=6 trace is drawn essentially coincident with and beneath the solid blue K=7 trace, and both are buried inside a dense ±5 dB noise band. A reader cannot see the difference the caption asserts. The comparison must be made legible (see fixes) or the claim must be softened.
- [BLOCKING] Undefined uncertainty: ν values appear in the caption but no interval, band, or note in the figure states what spread the reader is seeing. The visible vertical thickness of the blue/orange traces is realization noise, not an interval, and nothing in the figure says so. Many journals will require this to be stated explicitly.
- [ADVISORY] ~700 px of unused horizontal space between panels, with unequal panel widths and therefore mismatched x-scales for an identical abscissa.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) y-axis extends 11 dB beyond the data.
- [ADVISORY] Legend reads column-wise, against the natural left-to-right flow; grouping is also not semantic (the two "then box" smoothed estimators are split across columns 1 and 3).
- [ADVISORY] The zero reference line in (b) uses the same heavy black stroke as the "true PSD" series in (a), creating a color/weight collision across panels.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) is heavily overplotted in the 0.25–0.5 band: grey periodogram, blue multitaper, and orange Hann traces all occupy the same ~25 dB envelope, so the ordering of their noise floors — the point of the panel — is hard to extract.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Resolve the K=7 vs K=6 comparison. Either (i) add a third panel (c) plotting *only* K=7 and K=6 (or their difference) over the 0.2–0.5 band where they diverge; or (ii) in panel (b), replace the raw noisy traces with band-smoothed medians so the two blue curves separate cleanly, and show the raw traces at low alpha behind them. As drawn, the dashed curve is invisible and the caption is unsupported.
- State the uncertainty convention in the figure or caption explicitly, e.g. "traces are a single realization; vertical spread is realization variance, not a confidence interval," and consider adding a small ν-scaled 95% interval bracket for one or two estimators as a visual reference.
- Close the inter-panel gap and set both panels to the same width and the same x-scale. Reclaiming the dead middle third lets both panels grow, which will also help separate the overlapping traces.
- Tighten panel (b) to roughly −10 to +20 dB so the data fill the frame.
- Reorder the legend to read row-major and group semantically: row 1 = unsmoothed reference (periodogram, true PSD); row 2 = smoothed estimators (untapered+box, Hann+box, multitaper K=7, K=6). Better still, move the three visually separable series — green (untapered+box), orange (Hann+box), black (true PSD) — to inline labels at their right-hand endpoints in matching colors, and retain a two-entry legend only for the grey periodogram and the blue multitaper pair. In panel (b) the green, blue, and orange curves are well separated at x≈0.45, so endpoint labels there will not clip the traces; add thin same-color leader lines if any label sits within a few points of its curve.
- Draw the zero reference line in (b) as a thin grey rule rather than heavy black, reserving black for "true PSD."
