- In panel (a), drop the grey periodogram to lower alpha or a thinner stroke so the three smoothed estimators read as the foreground data.

### efigure3.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Good — No frames beyond the two-sided L-spines, no fills, no gridlines, no 3D or shadow effects. Markers + lines are justified (they disambiguate eight overlapping series and mark the actual sampled bandwidth parameters). The only redundant ink is the triplicated x-axis label and, arguably, the triplicated tick labels 5/10/15/20/25 on three identical axes.

**READING FLOW:** Problematic — Panels read left→right correctly and the shared legend sits below, which is fine. Two flow defects: (1) the y-axis labels of panels (b) and (c) sit so far left that they collide with the neighbouring panel, forcing the eye to disentangle label from plot; (2) the legend is laid out column-major (multitaper pair, then periodogram pair, …) so the reader must scan *down* then *across*, against the natural left→right order, while trying to match eight glyphs back into three panels.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- None identified in the strict sense — no ornament, no decorative shading. The dotted 8/N reference line is data-bearing and appropriately subordinate in weight.
- Borderline: three copies of the identical x-axis title where one shared title (or one under the middle panel) would do.

**WHITE SPACE:** Crowded between panels; Excessive inside panels (b) and (c). The inter-panel gutter is too narrow for the long two-line-worth y-axis titles, while ~35 % of the horizontal plot area in panel (b) is empty (all series stop at width ≈ 16; only the Welch curve in (a)/(c) reaches ≈ 25). The band of blank space between the panel row and the legend is also larger than needed.

**AXES:** Issues —
- (a) linear y begins at 0 ✓; x begins at ~1 rather than 0, which is defensible (half-power width cannot be 0) but is undocumented.
- (b) log y — appropriate for a quantity spanning 10⁻⁹–10⁻², and self-evidently justified; no zero-baseline violation.
- (c) dB is already a log measure; negative values are intrinsic. Fine.
- All three x-axes are forced to the same 1–25 range for comparability (good), but the empty right third of (b) is a cost of that choice and should be acknowledged or trimmed.
- Panel (b) y-axis shows only decade labels with minor ticks unlabelled — correct and clean.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — and explicitly justified in the caption ("exact kernel and degrees-of-freedom computations (no sampling variability)"). No action needed; this justification should stay in the caption.

**PANEL LABELS:** Consistent — (a), (b), (c) all present, bold, parenthesised, top-left, same size. Good.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — three near-square panels for what are effectively parametric scatter/line relations, not time series. The overall 1888×916 canvas is reasonable, though the figure would tighten if the panels were slightly narrower (removing the dead right margin) and the legend moved to two rows of four read left→right.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — no callouts, no text boxes, no bracketed labels. The single dotted vertical rule at 8/N is the minimal correct implement for that annotation; it is unlabelled in-plot, which is acceptable only because the caption defines it. Consider a small inline "8/N" at the top of the rule in the same grey so the reader does not have to consult the caption.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box (borderless, shared) — With eight series a single shared key is defensible, so this is not flagged under the 2–5-series rule. However, panel (a) is a strong candidate for partial inline labelling: at the right edge the endpoints separate cleanly (Welch ≈ 60, untapered ≈ 33, multitaper cluster ≈ 28–30, Hann ≈ 17, Bohman ≈ 14), so short colour-matched endpoint labels would remove most of the lookup cost. The legend itself does not overlap any data (it sits below the axes) — no blocking issue. The two multitaper Slepian entries (solid vs dashed circles/squares, same blue) and the blue sine-taper dotted triangle are hard to separate at a glance and nearly impossible to separate inside the dense (a) mid-range.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The y-axis titles of panels (b) and (c) are clipped at their left edge — "kernel" renders as "ernel" and "peak" as "eak". Truncated axis text will be rejected at copy-edit.
- [BLOCKING] Those same y-axis titles overlap the plot region / x-axis extent of the panel to their left (the (b) title intrudes into panel (a)'s right-hand area near x≈25; the (c) title intrudes into panel (b)'s). Annotation/label text overlapping a neighbouring panel's data area is a hard fail.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (b) wastes roughly a third of its width: no series extends past half-power width ≈ 16, yet the axis runs to 25.
- [ADVISORY] The x-axis title "half-power width (units of 1/N)" is repeated three times identically — redundant non-data ink.
- [ADVISORY] Legend is ordered column-major, forcing a down-then-across scan contrary to natural reading order.
- [ADVISORY] Three blue series (multitaper K=2NW−1 solid circle, K=2NW−2 dashed square, sine tapers dotted triangle) are nearly indistinguishable in hue; in panel (a) they overplot between widths 5 and 16 and the reader cannot resolve them.
- [ADVISORY] The dotted 8/N reference line carries no in-plot label; its meaning is caption-only.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (c)'s caption caveat (Gaussian and Welch kernels have no flat top, so the value is a main-lobe skirt, not a side lobe) is a substantive interpretive warning buried in prose — the two affected curves are not distinguished graphically in any way.
- [ADVISORY] The vertical gap between the panel row and the legend is larger than the gap needed, while the inter-panel gutters are too small — white space is allocated in the wrong place.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Increase the inter-panel horizontal spacing (e.g. `subplots_adjust(wspace≈0.45)` or `constrained_layout=True`) and re-render so the (b) and (c) y-axis titles are fully drawn and fully inside their own panel's margin. Verify at final publication width that no glyph is clipped.
- Better still, shorten the y-axis titles and let the caption carry the detail: "kernel power beyond 2×HPHW" and "peak beyond 1.5×HPHW (dB)", defining HPHW once in the caption. This solves the clipping and the overlap simultaneously and buys back plot width.
- Replace the three identical x-axis titles with one centred title beneath the panel row (or place it under panel (b) only), keeping tick labels on all three axes for readability.
- Either clip panel (b)'s x-limit to ~17 (with a note that no method reaches wider values there) or, if identical x-limits across panels are required for comparison, keep 25 but accept and state that choice — do not leave it unexplained.
- Add inline colour-matched endpoint labels in panel (a) for the well-separated curves (Welch, untapered, Hann, Bohman) with short thin leader lines where needed; retain the shared key only for the three blue multitaper variants. This cuts the legend from eight entries to three and removes most lookup cost.
- Differentiate the three multitaper series more strongly — e.g. dark navy / mid blue / light blue, or keep one blue and shift sine tapers to a distinct hue — so they remain separable in the dense mid-range of panel (a) and at reduced print size.
- Label the reference line in-plot: a small grey "8/N" rotated or set just above the axis at x=8 in panel (a) only, so the rule is self-describing without repeating the annotation three times.
- Reflow the legend to row-major order across four columns so it reads left→right, and reduce the gap between the panels and the legend by ~40 %, redistributing that space into the inter-panel gutters.
- In panel (c), mark the two curves whose values are main-lobe skirts rather than side lobes (Hann→Gaussian, Welch) with open rather than filled markers, or a lighter stroke, and state the encoding in the caption — so the caveat is visible in the graphic rather than only in prose.
- Optional: since all three panels share one x variable and one reference line, consider a vertical (stacked) arrangement with a single shared x-axis. That eliminates two x-axis titles and two sets of tick labels outright, and lets each panel be wider than tall — a better data-ink ratio for the same information.

### efigure4.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — the grid is largely honest (no 3D, no shadows, no gridlines, no panel frames beyond the two-spine style), but there is substantial *redundant* ink: the x-axis title is repeated 4× per row ("sample t" ×8, "taper index k" ×4, "frequency offset (units of 1/N)" ×4) where one label per row (or a single bottom-row label per column-independent scale) suffices. Tick labels on (j)–(l) duplicate the identical 0.0–0.4 scale already shown in (i); likewise the 0/100/200 ticks are repeated eight times. The title "eigen-tapers of $Q$, $k=0..3$" appears three times in row 2 and "eigen-weight ladder:" four times in row 3 — in both cases only the trailing value ($\nu$) or the exception (h) is information. Convert the repeated text into a single left-hand row label.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — small-multiple matrix reads left→right (methods) and top→bottom (properties), which is the correct arrangement. Weakness: the row semantics exist only in the repeated per-panel titles; an explicit row label in the left margin ("bank", "eigen-tapers", "weights", "kernel") would remove the need to re-read four titles per row.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Repeated identical panel titles and axis titles (see above) — text chartjunk.
- Two legend blocks ((b), (d), (e)) reproducing a colour code that is otherwise stable across all of rows 1–2; the $k=0..3$ legend in (d) duplicates the one in (e).
- The light-blue $|f|\le W$ band in (m)–(p) *does* encode information (defined in caption) so it is not junk, but it is heavier than needed; two thin vertical rules at $\pm W$ would use less ink.
- Nothing else decorative — no borders, fills, shadows, or 3D.

**WHITE SPACE:** Crowded at the top row (three-line titles collide with panel letters); adequate to slightly excessive elsewhere — the right half of (i) and (l) is empty, but that emptiness is itself informative (fewer tapers), so it should be kept.

**AXES:** Issues —
- Bars (i)–(l) correctly start at zero; shared x/y limits across the row enable comparison. Good.
- (m)–(p) dB-relative-to-peak axis ending at −80 is field-standard and acceptable; state "clipped at −80 dB" if any sidelobe falls below.
- Rows 1–2 y-axes are unlabeled/untick-marked "amplitude (curves offset)". This is defensible, but the spine then carries no information; either drop the y-spine entirely or add a faint zero baseline per offset curve so the reader can judge sign and amplitude.
- No inverted axes; no unjustified truncation.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — appropriate, these are deterministic constructions. (The degrees of freedom $\nu$ in the row-3 titles substitutes for a variance statement; consider stating in the caption that $\nu$ is the equivalent d.o.f. of the weight ladder.)

**PANEL LABELS:** Inconsistent — all sixteen letters are present, bold, parenthesized, and top-left, which is correct in style. However, in the top row (a)–(d) the letters sit at the vertical level of the third title line and collide with it: "(b)" runs into "bank: one taper modulated to f′", "(c)" into "bank: a sinc window at every offset", and "(d)" into "bank: Slepian tapers k = 0..3". Rows 2–4 are clean. The vertical offset of the label relative to the axes therefore differs by row.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — panels are near-square, acceptable for a 4×4 matrix. The two waveform rows would individually read better wider-than-tall, but matrix alignment across rows is the more valuable constraint; leave as is.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Clean — no callout boxes or bracketed annotations; the numeric annotations (width, % beyond 2W, $\nu$) are carried in titles rather than floating boxes, which is the right choice.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box (frameless) — overlapping. In (d) the legend entry "k = 1" sits directly on the purple $k=0$ trace near the top of the panel; in (b) the legend's first row crowds the $f'=0/N$ curve. In (e) the legend sits in the whitespace above the $k=0$ curve and is clean. All three are removable: the four traces are vertically stacked and separated, so each can be labeled inline at its right endpoint in its own colour.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel letters (b), (c), (d) overlap the third line of their panel titles — text-on-text collision that will be unreadable at publication width.
- [BLOCKING] Legend text in (d) overlaps the plotted $k=0$ curve; legend in (b) is within a few pixels of the top curve and will clip when scaled down.
- [BLOCKING] Data appear clipped at the panel boundary in (c): the light-green trace rises out of the top of the axes near $t\approx230$, and in (d) the top two traces run into the upper frame edge. Either expand the y-limits or reduce the offset spacing so no curve leaves its panel.
- [ADVISORY] Detached legends on line panels; four series only, vertically stacked — direct endpoint labeling would eliminate the lookup cost entirely.
- [ADVISORY] Redundant axis titles (16 where 3 are needed) and redundant y-tick labels in (j)–(l).
- [ADVISORY] Row-2 titles repeat identical text three times; row-3 titles repeat "eigen-weight ladder:" four times.
- [ADVISORY] Rows 1–2 y-axis carries a spine but no ticks or units; the "curves offset" convention leaves the reader without a zero reference for each trace.
- [ADVISORY] The $|f|\le W$ shading is defined in the caption but not on the figure; a reader scanning row 4 has no in-panel cue.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Raise the top-row titles (or lower the axes) by ~1 line height, or move the panel letters into the upper-left *inside* corner of the axes for all sixteen panels, so letters never touch title text. Keep one convention across all rows.
- Delete the legends in (b), (d), (e). Label each trace inline at its right endpoint, in the trace's own colour ($f'=0/N \ldots 3/N$ in b; $k=0\ldots3$ in d and e), staggered to match each curve's terminal height. Since rows 1–2 share the $k$ colour code, one set of endpoint labels in column (d)/(e) can serve the remaining panels.
- Expand y-limits or shrink the inter-curve offset in (c) and (d) so all traces are fully contained; clipped curves misrepresent the construction.
- Keep x-axis titles only on the bottom panel of each column-independent scale: one "sample t" under row 2 (rows 1–2 share the scale — note this in the caption), one "taper index k" under row 3, one "frequency offset (1/N)" under row 4. Remove y-tick labels from (j)–(l).
- Replace the repeated row-2 titles with a single left-margin row label "eigen-tapers of $Q$ ($k=0..3$)", and set row-3 titles to just "$\nu = 13.4$" etc. with a left-margin label "eigen-weight ladder". Panel (h)'s distinct title ("the Slepians") is genuine information — keep it.
- Replace the blue $|f|\le W$ fill with two thin vertical rules at $\pm W$ (or keep the fill but lighten it further and annotate "$\pm W$" once, in (m) only, in small inline type).
- Add a faint zero baseline (hairline, light grey) beneath each offset trace in rows 1–2, or drop the y-spine and state the offset convention in the caption only.
- Caption addition: define $\nu$ explicitly (equivalent degrees of freedom) and state that rows 1–2 share the $t$ axis and that (m)–(p) share both axes, so the reader trusts the cross-panel comparison.

### figure1.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Good — spines limited to left/bottom, no gridlines, no frames, no fills, no 3D or shadows. The thick grey halo in (a) is data ink (it encodes the reference multitaper estimate and permits the two coincident curves to be seen), so it is justified. Minor redundancy: in (c) the legend entry "max |difference|" restates the y-axis label; in (b) the y-axis tick ladder runs 10⁰…10⁻¹⁵ with six labeled decades where three or four would suffice.

READING FLOW:     Problematic (mildly) — panels read left→right correctly, but each panel forces a downward jump to its own legend block and then a jump back up to the traces. Three separate legends means three lookup detours in a single row of panels. The very long rotated y-label in (c) also pushes the eye upward into the panel-label zone before the data are reached.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Three separate detached legend blocks, two of which (b, c) label only two well-separated elements and are unnecessary.
  - Legend entry "max |difference|" in (c) duplicates the axis label.
  - Parentheses on panel letters "(a)(b)(c)" are decorative; bold letters alone are conventional and lighter.

WHITE SPACE:      Excessive below panels (a)–(c): the legend strips create a wide empty band across the bottom third of the figure that could be reclaimed by inline labeling, allowing the plots themselves to grow. Panel (c) is simultaneously crowded horizontally — its long y-axis label consumes more width than the plot box.

AXES:             Issues —
  - (a) y-axis starts near 18 dB rather than zero. Acceptable and defensible (dB is a logarithmic unit; zero is not a meaningful baseline), but the caption should state this rather than leaving it implicit.
  - (c) The L=16 marker sits essentially on the lower axis boundary and risks clipping at publication scale; extend the y-limit downward one tick.
  - (a) and (b) share an identical x-axis (frequency, 0–40 Hz) yet each carries a full label and tick set — a candidate for a shared axis or at least abbreviated repetition.
  - (c) x-axis ticks 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 are log-spaced but the axis is not annotated as logarithmic; state "log₂ scale" or show the axis as log explicitly.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — panel (c) plots the maximum absolute difference from a single 8 s epoch of one channel. No variability is shown and none is claimed, but a single-realization maximum is a noisy statistic. Not blocking, since the quantity is deterministic given the record; recommend the caption say explicitly that (c) is from the one epoch in (a) and that no averaging over epochs was performed.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a), (b), (c) all present, bold, same size, top-left of each panel. One caveat: in (c) the label sits immediately adjacent to the top of the tall y-axis label and reads as crowded; nudge the label left or shorten the y-label.

ASPECT RATIO:     Needs adjustment — (a) and (b) are spectra (frequency series) and are appropriately wider than tall. (c) is a short, monotonic 5-point trend rendered in a narrow near-square panel; it is the visually weakest element and is squeezed by its own axis label. Either widen (c) slightly or accept a smaller panel with a much shorter y-label. Overall 2094×606 is a reasonable single-row strip once the legend band is removed.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — no callout boxes, no bracketed labels, nothing overlapping data. The "1/L" guide line in (c) is a good Tufte-style reference element; it should be labeled inline at its right end rather than through a legend.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box (three of them) —
  - (a): the legend is defensible, since the three curves are deliberately coincident and cannot be labeled inline. Keep it, but move it into the empty upper-right region inside the axes (the spectrum falls off to the right, leaving open space) so it occupies dead plot area instead of adding a band below the figure. Verify it does not touch the 2.5 Hz peak.
  - (b): ADVISORY — two traces separated by twelve orders of magnitude. Replace the legend with inline labels "smooth minus multitaper" (green, beside the lower trace) and "average (16N) minus multitaper" (pink, beside or above the upper trace), placed at the right end where both traces are flat.
  - (c): ADVISORY — replace with "max |difference|" removed entirely (it is the y-axis) and "1/L" written in grey at the right end of the dotted guide, offset below the solid line so the two do not collide near L=16 where they converge.

ISSUES:
  - [ADVISORY] Detached legend boxes on all three panels; (b) and (c) label only two elements each and impose an avoidable lookup cost. Legend band consumes roughly a quarter of figure height.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption describes the third curve as "dashed purple," but the rendered colour is a pink/mauve; the legend calls it "average." Align caption colour word, legend text, and rendered colour exactly, or the reader cannot verify which curve is which.
  - [ADVISORY] Green/pink pairing is the classic red–green confusion axis. Under deuteranopia the green and pink traces in (b) may be hard to separate; they are separated by position here, which mitigates it, but in (a) the coincident curves rely entirely on colour plus dash pattern. The dash pattern carries the load — state this in the caption ("dashed" is doing the real work).
  - [ADVISORY] L=16 data point in (c) sits on the axis line and may clip when scaled to column width.
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant legend entry in (c) duplicating the y-axis label.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c) y-axis label is long enough to dominate the panel and crowd the panel letter.
  - [ADVISORY] (a) and (b) duplicate an identical x-axis label and tick set without sharing.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Delete the legends in (b) and (c); label traces inline, in series colour, at the right-hand flat end of each trace. Label the 1/L guide inline in grey at its right end, offset downward to avoid the solid line where the two converge.
  - Move the (a) legend inside the axes to the upper-right dead space, with no frame and no background fill; check it clears the 2.5 Hz shoulder and the 8–11 Hz peaks.
  - Reclaim the freed bottom band by making all three plot boxes taller; this is the single largest improvement available.
  - Shorten (c) y-axis label to "max |difference| (dB)" and put the full definition in the caption; this un-crowds the panel and frees width.
  - Thin (b) y-axis tick labels to 10⁰, 10⁻⁵, 10⁻¹⁰, 10⁻¹⁵ (or 10⁻³, 10⁻⁹, 10⁻¹⁵ plus minor unlabeled ticks).
  - Extend (c) y-limits one tick below the L=16 point so no marker touches the axis.
  - Add to the caption: that dB is a logarithmic unit and (a)'s y-axis therefore does not begin at zero; that (c)'s x-axis is log₂; and that (c) derives from the same single epoch as (a) with no epoch averaging.
  - Fix the colour word in the caption to match the rendered hue, and consider a colourblind-safe pair (e.g. teal and orange) since (a) relies on colour to distinguish coincident curves.
  - Drop the parentheses from panel letters; bold **a**, **b**, **c** is lighter and equally clear.

### figure2.png

---
**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — The core content is admirably lean (thin curves, light grey fills, no 3-D, no gridlines, no bounding boxes beyond the two-spine frame), but there is avoidable redundant ink:
- The x-axis label is printed four times per row (`frequency (cycles per sample)` ×4, `frequency offset (units of 1/N)` ×4) even though all four panels in a row share identical ranges and ticks. One label per row (or one centred under each row) would recover ~8% of the figure area.
- Panel (a) carries **two** separate legends (curve identities + bar meanings), four entries of non-data ink inside the data rectangle.
- Panel (e)'s Fejér kernel is drawn as a solid filled region rather than a line, so it deposits a very large amount of ink for what is the same kind of object drawn as a thin line in (f)–(h); it also competes with the grey "true spectrum" fill behind it.

**READING FLOW:** Natural — Left→right ordering (no taper → taper → right smoothing → over-smoothing) with each kernel directly beneath its estimator is exactly the right arrangement; the reader never backtracks. Only local friction is that the meaning of the blue bar in (c)/(d) and the blue shading in (g)/(h) must be fetched from the caption.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Duplicate legend blocks in (a) (one could be eliminated entirely; see below).
- Legend swatches standing in for annotations that should be drawn on the data itself ("peak width 7/N", "separation 30/N").
- Two-line titles in the top row vs. one-line in the bottom row create a ragged, slightly ornamental header band.

**WHITE SPACE:** Adequate — Panels are well separated; the bottom row's −80 dB floor gives the kernels room without crowding. Minor waste from the quadruplicated axis labels.

**AXES:** OK — dB (log) ordinates legitimately do not start at zero; frequency abscissa starts at 0 (top) and is symmetric about 0 (bottom). No inverted axes. Tick density is appropriate. Y-tick labels correctly appear only on column 1 — good discipline.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — Acceptable in principle (ν is quoted as the variance surrogate), but see advisory: with ν given, a single vertical 95% χ²-based CI bar per panel would let the reader *see* the variance rather than compute it from ν.

**PANEL LABELS:** Inconsistent — All eight labels are present, bold, parenthesized, top-left: good. However their horizontal placement collides with the centred titles in the bottom row: in (g) the label and title touch outright ("(g)kernel: < 0.001% beyond 2W"), and (f) and (h) are within a character-width. In the top row the labels sit below the title line, in the bottom row they sit on the title baseline — two different conventions in one figure.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate — Each panel is near-square, suitable for these single-decade spectral shapes; the 4×2 grid at 2115×1140 px will reduce cleanly to a two-column width.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Overlapping / Replaceable —
- The blue half-power-width bar in (c) is so short it reads as a stray marker rather than a width annotation; in (d) it reads correctly. Neither is labelled in-figure.
- The blue shading in (g)/(h) (|f| ≤ W) is unlabelled in-figure.
- The "peak width 7/N" and "separation 30/N" grey marks promised by the caption for panel (a) are **not visible on the data** — only their legend swatches appear. This is a figure/caption mismatch.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box (two of them), one overlapping data —
- Panel (a) upper legend: the entry "estimate (one realization)" sits at roughly y ≈ 37 dB spanning x ≈ 0.06–0.33, i.e. directly across the region where the grey periodogram trace excurses to +45 dB. The grey trace passes through/behind the legend text. **Overlap with data.**
- Panel (a) lower legend sits in a genuinely empty region — acceptable placement, but its content belongs on the curve, not in a box.
- Panel (e) legend ("true spectrum (shape only)" / "kernel") sits at ≈ −8 to −15 dB over x ≈ −40 to −18, where the grey true-spectrum fill rises from −27 to −13 dB; the lower entry is within a few pixels of the fill boundary and will clip at reduced publication width.
- Colour inconsistency: the "estimate" is grey in (a) but orange in (b)–(d); the "kernel" is grey in (e) but orange in (f)–(h). The legend in (a)/(e) therefore teaches a colour mapping that the other six panels contradict.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel (a) legend entry "estimate (one realization)" overlaps the grey periodogram trace in the 0.06–0.20 cycles/sample band.
- [BLOCKING] Panel label (g) collides with its own title text (no separating space); (f) and (h) are within one character width and will touch after downscaling.
- [BLOCKING] Caption states "the grey bars in (a) mark the peak width and separation," but no such bars are visible in panel (a) — only legend swatches. Either the bars are missing/clipped or the caption is wrong.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (e) legend sits within a few pixels of the grey true-spectrum fill boundary; will clip at final width.
- [ADVISORY] Detached legend boxes on curve plots with only 2–3 series; both are removable by direct labelling.
- [ADVISORY] Series colours are not consistent between the leftmost panel and the remaining panels of each row (grey in a/e, orange in b–d/f–h), so the legend's colour key is misleading.
- [ADVISORY] X-axis label repeated four times per row; ticks and ranges are identical.
- [ADVISORY] The blue half-power bar in (c) is too short to read as a width; the blue |f| ≤ W shading in (g)/(h) is unlabelled in-figure.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (e) kernel drawn as a solid fill rather than a line, inconsistent with (f)–(h) and visually confusable with the grey true-spectrum fill behind it.
- [ADVISORY] ν is given but its consequence (estimator variance) is not shown graphically.
- [ADVISORY] Two-line titles (top row) vs one-line (bottom row) make the header band ragged.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Delete both legend boxes. In panel (a), write `true PSD` in black directly beside the black curve where it is smooth and isolated (e.g. at f ≈ 0.40, just above the black line) and `one realization` in the series colour just below the noisy trace at f ≈ 0.35; use a short thin leader line of matching colour if separation is tight. Do the same in (e): `kernel` beside the kernel at its −50 dB skirt, `true spectrum (shape only)` in grey inside the grey fill near the upper left.
- Use one colour for "estimate" across (a)–(d) and one for "kernel" across (e)–(h). If grey in (a)/(e) is deliberate (no taper), state that in the caption; otherwise unify to orange so the direct labels only need to appear once.
- Draw the peak-width and peak-separation marks *on* panel (a) — a short horizontal bar of length 7/N spanning one peak at its half-power level, and a bar of length 30/N between the two peak centres, each with a tiny inline label `7/N` and `30/N`. Then the second legend disappears and the caption claim becomes true.
- Give the blue half-power bar a visible minimum length (or replace the bar in (c) with a tick pair) and add a one-word inline label `2W` (or `half-power width`) beside the bar in (c) only; the reader will transfer it to (d).
- Label the blue shading inline in (g) with a small `|f| ≤ W` in the shading colour, once, rather than relying on the caption.
- Redraw the Fejér kernel in (e) as a line (same weight and colour as (f)–(h)) instead of a filled region; this both fixes the colour inconsistency and removes a large block of non-data ink obscuring the grey true spectrum.
- Fix panel-label placement: put all eight labels in the outer top-left *corner of the axes*, above the title line, with a fixed pad (e.g. left-aligned to the y-axis spine, one line above the title), identical in both rows. Guarantee at least one em of clearance from the title.
- Keep x-tick labels on all panels if desired, but print the x-axis title once per row (centred beneath the row) instead of four times. Move `ν = 2.0`, `ν = 8.9`, `ν = 50.0` out of the titles and into the upper-right interior of each top-row panel as inline text; titles then become single-line and the header band is even.
- Optionally add, in the upper right of each top-row panel, a short vertical bar giving the 95% χ²_ν interval for that estimator — one mark that makes ν visible and pays for itself in ink.

### figure3.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Needs work — The core plotting is admirably lean (no gridlines, despined axes, no 3D/shadows, reference lines drawn in light grey so they sit behind the data). The non-data ink that remains is concentrated in three places: (1) two detached legend boxes occupying roughly the bottom 25–30 % of the figure height; (2) two panel titles whose text duplicates the caption almost verbatim ("N = 1024 samples of the AR(4) process", "300 realizations"); (3) the orange callout circles, which are drawn heavier than the curves they annotate and reuse the bias-series colour.

**READING FLOW:** Problematic — Left→right across panels is natural, but each panel forces a downward jump to a legend block and then a jump back up to the curves. In panel (b) with four series and two dash patterns this lookup happens repeatedly.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Panel titles that restate caption content (redundant with the caption text; Tufte would delete both titles or reduce them to two or three words).
- Legend swatch lines in both legend blocks — redundant data ink once the series are labelled inline.
- Callout circles drawn in the same orange as the "bias" series, implying series membership they do not have.
- Legend text "peak region 0.09−0.16" uses a Unicode minus as a range dash; should be an en dash (0.09–0.16) or "0.09 to 0.16".

**WHITE SPACE:** Crowded in the annotation band at the top of panel (b) (three orange callouts plus two grey reference-line labels compete in the upper third), and simultaneously **excessive** below both panels where the legend blocks sit. Net effect: the data region of panel (b) is compressed while a quarter of the canvas carries text that could be inline.

**AXES:** OK with one item to verify — Panel (a): y spans ±50 about a true zero, appropriate for a signed record. Panel (b): y begins at 0, correct for an RMS magnitude. However, the x-axis tick spacing in panel (b) is not visibly uniform (0→5 occupies noticeably more horizontal space than 5→10, 10→15, …). If the axis is linear this is a rendering/tick-placement artefact that should be fixed; if it is a nonlinear (e.g. square-root) scale, that must be stated in the caption and the axis label. Unlabelled nonlinearity in a bias–variance trade-off figure would be misleading, since the location of the "broad minimum" depends on the scale.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — and explicitly justified in the caption ("no error bars are shown because the curves are ensemble averages"). That justification is acceptable but incomplete: an ensemble average of 300 realizations still has a standard error, and readers will want to know whether the difference between the whole-band minimum and the peak-region minimum exceeds it. A light shaded band (±1 SE of the mean over 300 realizations) on the two black curves would settle the question without adding clutter.

**PANEL LABELS:** Inconsistent in placement — (a) and (b) are both bold, parenthesized, same size (good), but (a) sits in clear margin space while (b) is pushed up against the rotated y-axis label "RMS error vs true PSD (dB)", so the two glyph groups collide or very nearly touch. Positioning relative to each panel's axes is not matched.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Appropriate for (a), needs adjustment for (b) — a 1024-sample time series correctly gets a wide panel. Panel (b) is a smooth two-variable trade-off curve and would read better closer to square; reclaiming the legend band and narrowing (b) slightly would achieve this without shrinking the data.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable / partly overlapping — No boxes, fills or shadows, which is good. But: the "too noisy / ν = 2" callout circle at W = 0 is bisected by the y-axis spine, so half the marker is lost and it reads as an axis decoration rather than a data point. The accompanying text sits in the steepest part of the two black curves and crowds them. The "too smooth / ν = 50" circle at W ≈ 24 lands exactly where the black total curve and the orange bias curve converge, and because the circle is orange it is ambiguous which series is being marked.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box (both panels) — Panel (b) has four series, panel (a) three; both are within the 2–5 range where direct labelling eliminates the lookup cost entirely. Neither legend overlaps data (not blocking), but both are detached and both cost vertical real estate.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel label "(b)" collides with the rotated y-axis label "RMS error vs true PSD (dB)". Panel labels must be legible and separated from axis text at final publication width.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (b) x-axis tick spacing appears non-uniform while the tick labels (0, 5, 10, …, 30) imply a linear scale. Either fix the tick placement or document the nonlinear scale in the axis label and caption — an undocumented nonlinear abscissa distorts the apparent location and breadth of the error minimum.
- [ADVISORY] The W = 0 callout circle is clipped by the left spine; the marker and its "too noisy / ν = 2" label crowd the steeply rising black curves.
- [ADVISORY] Callout circles and callout text use the same orange as the "bias" series, creating a false colour association; the W ≈ 24 circle sits where the black and orange curves converge, compounding the ambiguity.
- [ADVISORY] Two detached legend boxes consume ~25–30 % of figure height on a figure whose series are individually labellable at their endpoints.
- [ADVISORY] Panel titles duplicate caption text (sample count, process order, number of realizations).
- [ADVISORY] Ensemble-average curves are shown with no indication of the uncertainty of the mean; the reader cannot judge whether the whole-band vs peak-region shift in the optimum is resolved.
- [ADVISORY] Range written with a Unicode minus ("0.09−0.16") rather than an en dash.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Delete both legend boxes and label inline, each label in its series colour: in (b), "total, whole band" beside the solid black curve near W ≈ 28, "peak region 0.09–0.16" beside the dashed curve near W ≈ 30 (stagger vertically — the two black curves run apart there, so no leader lines are needed), "noise" at the right end of the blue curve, "bias" at the right end of the orange curve just below it. In (a), place "record x_t" in grey over a quiet early segment, "Hann-tapered record" in orange near the centre, and "Hann taper" in black beside the upper dashed envelope around t ≈ 850.
- Recover the freed vertical space by enlarging both data regions; this also lets panel (b) approach a square aspect.
- Move "(b)" further left and up, clear of the y-axis label, matching the offset used for "(a)".
- Nudge the W = 0 callout circle inward (or draw it open with a thin leader) so it is not clipped by the spine, and move "too noisy / ν = 2" up and left into the empty corner with a short thin leader line to the marker.
- Recolour all three callout circles and their text to a neutral dark grey or black so they read as cross-references to Fig. motif rather than as members of the orange bias series.
- Delete or drastically shorten both panel titles; the caption already carries this information. If a title is kept in (b), reduce it to "error vs box width".
- Add a light ±1 SE band (n = 300) around the two black curves, or state the SE magnitude numerically in the caption, and amend the caption sentence from "no error bars are shown because the curves are ensemble averages" to state the actual standard error of the mean.
- Verify the abscissa scale in (b); if linear, correct the tick placement so spacing is uniform.
- Replace the Unicode minus in the range with an en dash.

### figure4.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — the underlying line work is clean (no gridlines, no 3D, no shadows), but non-data ink is spent on four separate legend boxes, a gray background patch in (b), and a large unused vertical band between the two rows. Panel (a)'s y-axis spine carries no ticks or values and encodes nothing beyond "offset"; it could be erased entirely. The gray fill in (b) marks $|f|\le W$ — information, but it could be conveyed by two thin vertical rules at $\pm 4$ at a fraction of the ink, and it currently sits *behind* the curves at a value heavier than several of the low-amplitude sidelobes it overlays.

READING FLOW:     Problematic — the a→b→c→d order is correct, but each panel's legend is parked below its x-axis label, so the eye must drop far past the data (and past a wide empty gap) to decode the series, then jump back up. In (c) the legend sits at the very bottom-left of the figure, roughly 100 px below the axis, while (d)'s legend sits *inside* its axes: two different conventions in the same figure force the reader to re-learn where to look.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Gray background patch in (b) doing the work of two reference lines.
  - Four legend boxes for series that are ordered (k = 0…7, "first 1/3/5/7/9 tapers") and could be labeled directly at their curve endpoints or by a single colorbar-style k ramp.
  - Legend keys in (a)/(b) are redundant with each other — identical viridis k-mapping is re-declared in both panels.
  - Panel (d)'s legend restates two vertical rules that could be annotated in place.

WHITE SPACE:      Excessive — roughly a full panel-height of empty space between row 1 and row 2, created by the below-axis legends. Simultaneously, (a) and (b) are *crowded* at the axis-label line where legend text collides with axis text (see ISSUES).

AXES:             Issues —
  - (a) y-axis is unlabeled/untickd offset space; acceptable for stacked tapers but the spine should be dropped or explicitly marked "arbitrary offset."
  - (b) and (c) y-axes begin at zero. Good.
  - (d) log y with decade ticks $10^{-16}$–$10^{-1}$: zero baseline correctly not required; documented by the quantity being a fraction. Fine.
  - (d) x starts at 0 and is integer-indexed. Good.
  - (b)/(c) share identical x-axis ("frequency offset, units of 1/N") and identical $-8..8$ range but are in different rows and columns, so the shared scale is not exploited; consider stacking them to allow one x-axis.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — appropriate; these are deterministic quantities.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a)–(d) all present, bold, parenthesized, upper-left. Minor: (a)/(b) sit at the very top of the axes block while (c)/(d) sit level with their titles; nudge to a uniform offset from each axes' top-left corner.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — the ~2:1 landscape panels suit spectral traces and the k-index series. (d) could be slightly narrower since it spans 16 integer points; the extra width is not carrying data.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Replaceable — (d)'s two dashed vertical rules ("2NW", "K = 2NW−1") are explained only in a boxed legend. Replace with inline text set vertically beside each rule (or as secondary x-tick labels at k = 7 and k = 8). The dotted gray rule at k = 7 is lighter than the data line but runs very close to the orange rule at k = 8; at publication width the two will read as one feature.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — and two of the boxes collide with axis text:
  - (a) The legend's first row ("k = 0 / k = 2 / k = 4") is drawn directly over the x-axis label "sample t"; the words are superimposed and both are degraded.
  - (b) Identically, the first legend row ("k = 0 / k = 2 / k = 4 / k = 6") overprints "frequency offset (units of 1/N)".
  - (b) The rotated y-axis label "$|U_k(f)|^2$ (unit-norm tapers)" runs up past the axes top and collides with the title line / panel-(b) label region.
  - (c) Six-entry legend is detached and far below the axes; the "all N tapers" and "ideal box" keys in particular are the two most important curves and are hardest to find.
  - (d) Two-entry legend box sits inside the axes upper-right; it does not currently overlap the plateau of the data curve but is within ~1 line-height of it and will collide if the panel is scaled down.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Legend text overprints the x-axis label in panel (a) ("sample t") and panel (b) ("frequency offset (units of 1/N)"). Both strings are unreadable where they intersect.
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (b) y-axis label overlaps the title/panel-label band at the top of the axes.
  - [ADVISORY] Detached legend boxes on all four line panels; with 2–8 ordered series, direct endpoint labeling would remove the lookup cost entirely.
  - [ADVISORY] Inconsistent legend placement (below-axes in a/b/c, inside-axes in d).
  - [ADVISORY] Gray shading in (b) is heavier than the sidelobe data it sits behind; it competes with the low-amplitude structure that the panel is meant to show ("filling the gaps").
  - [ADVISORY] ~1 panel-height of dead vertical space between rows; figure real estate is wasted while the data panels are compressed.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (d) uses a single blue that is outside the viridis k-mapping used in (a)–(c), breaking the color↔k association the rest of the figure establishes.
  - [ADVISORY] The k = 7 (dotted) and k = 8 (dashed) rules in (d) are one x-unit apart and will merge visually at column width.
  - [ADVISORY] (c) and (b) share an identical x-scale but are not aligned, so the reader cannot register the running sum against the individual windows.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Remove all four legends. In (a)/(b), label the k = 0 and the highest-k curve inline in their own colors at the right-hand end (in (a), beside each trace's flat right edge; in (b), above the outermost lobe at f ≈ ±4), and add a short note in the caption that color runs dark→light with increasing k — or add one thin viridis colorbar labeled "taper index k" shared by (a)–(c).
  - In (c), place "first 1", "first 5", "first 9" directly above their respective plateaus near f = 0 to −2 in matching colors, and label the black "all N tapers" curve on its flat top and the dashed "ideal box" on its vertical riser at f = −4 with a short thin leader line. Stagger these vertically so no label sits on a curve.
  - In (d), delete the legend box and set "2NW" and "K = 2NW−1" as rotated inline text immediately left/right of their rules near the bottom of the axes, where the data curve is absent; or mark k = 7 and k = 8 as emphasized x-ticks and drop one of the two rules if it is redundant.
  - Move each x-axis label back to its own line (increase bottom margin per axes, or use `constrained_layout`) so no text overprinting remains; shorten (b)'s y-label to "$|U_k(f)|^2$" and move "unit-norm tapers" into the caption.
  - Replace the gray band in (b) with two thin vertical rules at f = ±4 (or a very light band at ≤10% alpha drawn below the lines with `zorder` set behind), and state "$|f|\le W$" once as inline text at the top of the band rather than in the title.
  - Reclaim the inter-row gap: reduce figure height by ~20–25% after the legends are removed, letting each panel grow.
  - Consider stacking (b) above (c) in the same column with a single shared x-axis, so the running sum registers frequency-for-frequency against the individual spectral windows; move (d) to the right column with (a).
  - Recolor (d)'s markers using the same viridis ramp indexed by k, so color carries the same meaning throughout the figure.
  - Drop (a)'s y-spine and ticks; annotate the axis simply as "taper (arbitrary offset)".

### figure5.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — the core content (curves, bars) is drawn cleanly with no 3D, shadows, gridlines, or fills, and the top-row y-axis wisely omits meaningless ticks. But there is substantial redundant ink: "sample t" is printed four times, "taper index k" four times, the identical 0.0–0.4 tick ladder is repeated in all four bottom panels, and the words "eigen-weight ladder:" are repeated in four titles when only ν differs. Three detached legend boxes (b, c, d) duplicate information that could sit directly on the curves. Bottom-row bar colors (pink/orange/teal/blue) encode nothing — they do not correspond to the viridis series colors directly above them, so color is decorative here rather than data-bearing.

READING FLOW:     Problematic — the column logic (a→e, b→f, …) is sound and reads left→right, but the panel letters are placed *inside/over* the multi-line titles, so the eye must disentangle letter from caption text at the top of columns 3 and 4. The four-line stacked titles in the top row also force a long read before any data is seen.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Three legend boxes (b, c, d) with frames, each duplicable by inline labels.
  - Repeated axis titles ("sample t" ×4, "taper index k" ×4) and repeated y-tick ladders in the bottom row.
  - Repeated title stem "eigen-weight ladder:" in (e)–(h).
  - Non-informative, non-matching fill colors in the bottom row.

WHITE SPACE:      Crowded at the top (title blocks collide with panel letters and legends sit on the data); Excessive at the bottom right of (e), (g), (h), where the x-axis runs to ~12 but bars stop at k=6 or k=9. The common axis range is defensible for cross-panel comparison, but it should be stated in the caption so the blank region is read as information ("no further tapers") rather than as wasted space.

AXES:             Mostly OK — bottom-row bar charts correctly begin at zero and share identical limits, enabling honest comparison. Top-row y-axes are unlabeled/unticked with arbitrary vertical offsets; this is acceptable for taper shape but the offsets should be flagged in-axis (e.g., a faint zero baseline per curve or the note "curves offset" is already present — keep it). No inverted or log axes. The top-row x-axes omit the final tick near 256; consider labeling 0 and 255/256 explicitly since N=256 is the key parameter.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — appropriate, these are deterministic window functions and weight ladders.

PANEL LABELS:     Inconsistent — (a), (b), (e), (f), (g), (h) are legible, but (c) and (d) are typeset directly on top of the title text ("(c)" collides with "bank: a sinc window…" and "(d)" collides with "bank: Slepian tapers k = 0..3"). Horizontal offsets of the letters relative to their axes also differ between the top and bottom rows.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — wide panels suit the sample-index traces; the bar panels are near-square and read well. Overall 2090×1141 for a 4×2 grid is sensible.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Overlapping — the legend frames in (b) and (d) sit on top of the topmost curves; in (b) the legend's first row overlaps the purple f′=0/N trace, and in (d) the "k = 1" entry sits directly over the k = 0 and k = 1 curves near t≈150–200. The ν values in the bottom titles are fine as text but would be more Tufte-like placed inline at the top-right of each bar panel, in the empty region, where the eye already is.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — three framed legends, two of which overlap data. Panels (b), (c), (d) each carry only four labeled series and are ideal candidates for direct endpoint labeling in the matching series color.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panel letters (c) and (d) overlap the adjacent panel titles; letters and title text are superimposed and partially illegible.
  - [BLOCKING] Legend box in (b) overlaps the f′=0/N curve; legend box in (d) overlaps the k=0 and k=1 curves.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend box in (c) is detached from the data and duplicates information that could be labeled inline.
  - [ADVISORY] Bottom-row bar colors carry no information and do not match the curve colors of the corresponding top-row panel, breaking the column pairing the layout is trying to assert.
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant ink: four copies of each axis title, four copies of the identical y-tick ladder, four copies of "eigen-weight ladder:".
  - [ADVISORY] Empty x-range in (e), (g), (h) reads as wasted space unless the caption explains that the common axis is deliberate.
  - [ADVISORY] Four-line stacked titles in the top row consume vertical space that could go to the data; the parenthetical parameter lists belong in the caption (most are already there).

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Move all panel letters outside the axes, flush left above each panel's y-axis, at a uniform offset and weight; reserve a dedicated title line so no letter ever overlaps text.
  - Delete the three legend boxes. Label each trace inline at its right-hand endpoint in its own color (f′ = 0/N, 1/N, 2/N, 3/N in b; k = 0…3 in d; same in c), with a short thin leader line in the series color where a curve is steep near the right edge. In (c) the blue and teal traces run close at t≈255 — stagger those two labels vertically or shift them to t≈150 where the traces separate.
  - Recolor the bottom-row bars to match the dominant color of the taper family above them (or use one neutral gray for all four, since color adds nothing), so column identity is reinforced rather than contradicted.
  - Share axes: keep tick labels and the y-axis title "weight c_k (normalized)" only on (e), and a single centered "taper index k" beneath the bottom row; likewise one "sample t" beneath the top row and "amplitude (curves offset)" only on (a).
  - Compress the bottom-row titles to "ν = 13.4", "ν = 11.1", etc., and state "eigen-weight ladder" once in the caption; better still, print ν as inline text in the empty upper-right of each bar panel.
  - Trim the top-row titles to a single short label ("Welch", "smooth one periodogram", "sliding sinc", "Thomson multitaper (reference)") and let the caption carry N, L, σ, K — it already does.
  - Add a sentence to the caption noting that all four ladder panels share the axis 0 ≤ k ≤ 12 and 0 ≤ c_k ≤ 0.45, so the differing bar counts and heights are directly comparable.
  - Consider a faint zero baseline under each offset trace in (b)–(d) so the reader can judge sign and amplitude; keep it lighter than the curves.

### figure6.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — no gridlines, no 3D, no shadows, no panel frames beyond a clean two-spine box: the core is disciplined. The waste is in repetition: (i) the y-tick labels (40/20/0/−20/−40 and 0/−20/…/−80) are repeated in all four columns of both rows despite identical shared scales; (ii) the x-axis title is printed four times per row ("frequency (cycles per sample)" ×4, "frequency offset (units of 1/N)" ×4). Eight axis titles where two would do. The gray "true spectrum (shape only)" fill is also very heavy ink for a purely contextual reference — in (e) and (h) it visually outweighs the kernel it is meant to contextualize.

READING FLOW:     Natural — left→right across estimators, top→bottom from estimate to kernel, with column-consistent colors (green/blue/orange/pink) tying (a)↔(e), (b)↔(f), etc. No backtracking. Titles carry the ν and kernel-leakage numbers at the point of use, which is good Tufte practice.

CHARTJUNK:
  - Small blue square marker above the spectral peak in (a)–(d): entirely undefined in both the legend and the caption. In (a) it sits immediately left of the "true PSD" legend text, so it reads as if it were part of that entry.
  - Heavy gray area fill in (e)–(h) repeated four times; an outline or a much lighter tint would serve the same reference role with a fraction of the ink.
  - Redundant per-panel axis titles and tick labels (see data-ink).

WHITE SPACE:      Crowded in (e) (legend sits on top of the fill and near the kernel), Excessive in (a)–(d) — the y-axis runs to −45 dB while no curve in any top panel goes below about −25 dB, so the bottom ~25% of each top panel is empty. Conversely (g) and (h) are clipped hard at the −80 dB floor.

AXES:             OK — dB (log) and dB-relative-to-peak scales legitimately need no zero origin; frequency begins at 0; bottom-row offset axes are symmetric about 0. No inverted axes. Two issues: the top-row y-limit (−45) is unmotivated by the data, and the −80 dB floor truncates the (g) and (h) kernels without any indication that the curve continues below the frame.

ERROR BARS:       Not present — one realization is plotted, which is the stated intent, but the figure asserts ν = 17.2/14.0/8.9/17.6 in the titles without rendering the variability those numbers describe. The median absolute errors live only in the caption.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a)–(h) all present, bold, parenthesized, top-left, outside the axes, same size. Note only that in the top row the label sits beside a two-line title and in the bottom row beside a one-line title, so the label baselines differ slightly between rows.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — roughly square panels suit these single-peak spectral shapes; the 4×2 grid at ~2094×1140 gives a sensible publication proportion. Trimming the dead space at the bottom of the top row would let the panels grow without changing the overall footprint.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Replaceable — the light-blue |f| ≤ W band is a good, quiet annotation and should stay. The undefined blue square in (a)–(d) should either be defined or replaced with a labeled horizontal bar of width 2W drawn once, with the inline text "2W" beside it. The kernel-leakage percentages are already inline in the titles — correct choice.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box (frameless) — two detached legends for a total of five entries, both in the first column. The (a) legend labels only two series (black true PSD, colored estimate) and could be eliminated by writing "true PSD" in black beside the black curve on the falling flank and "estimate" in the series color beside the colored trace. The (e) legend is worse: all three entries are drawn on top of the gray "true spectrum" fill, and the "kernel" entry sits close to where the green kernel curve rises through the left half of the panel — a legend overlapping plotted data.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Legend in (e) overlays the gray "true spectrum (shape only)" fill and crowds the green kernel curve; a legend must not sit on data.
  - [BLOCKING] The blue square marker above the peak in (a)–(d) is undefined in the legend, axis annotation, and caption. A reader cannot decode it.
  - [ADVISORY] Detached legend boxes on line charts in (a) and (e); inline labeling would remove the lookup cost for all five entries.
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant y-tick labels in columns 2–4 of both rows and four copies of each x-axis title.
  - [ADVISORY] Top-row y-axis extends to −45 dB with no data below ≈ −25 dB: ~25% dead space in four panels.
  - [ADVISORY] Kernels in (g) and (h) are clipped at the −80 dB frame with no indication of continuation.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption/figure color mismatch: Welch is described as "purple" but is drawn in pink/magenta.
  - [ADVISORY] ν values are asserted in titles but no variability is depicted; the reader must take the stability claim on faith from a single realization.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Define or replace the blue square: draw a single horizontal bar spanning 2W at the peak height with the inline label "2W" in (a) only, and note in the caption that it marks the half-power resolution.
  - Delete both legend boxes. In (a), write "true PSD" in black on the black curve's falling flank near f ≈ 0.35 and "estimate" in green beside the green trace near f ≈ 0.45 where the two are well separated. In (e), label the gray region "true spectrum (shape only)" in dark gray inside the fill at upper right where the kernel is absent, label the light-blue band "|f| ≤ W" just above the band, and write "kernel" in the series color beside the curve near f ≈ −30 (use a short thin leader line in the series color if the text would come within ~1.5 text heights of the curve).
  - Share y-axes formally: keep tick labels only in column 1 of each row. Keep one centered x-axis title per row instead of four.
  - Set the top-row y-limits to about [−30, 50] to recover the empty band, and let the panels expand accordingly.
  - Lighten the gray fill (or replace with a thin gray outline plus a very pale tint) so the kernel curves clearly dominate in (e)–(h).
  - In (g) and (h), either extend the y-axis or add a small downward arrow/break mark where the curve exits the frame.
  - Optionally add a pale chi-square-based confidence band around the true PSD in one top panel, or a small inset showing ν, so the titled ν values are visually cashed out; at minimum move the median-absolute-error numbers from the caption into each title alongside ν.
  - Align the panel-label baselines between rows (pad the one-line bottom-row titles) so (a)–(d) and (e)–(h) labels sit at matching heights.
  - Correct "purple" to "pink" in the caption, or recolor the Welch series to match the caption.

### figure7.png

---

**DATA-INK RATIO:** Good — no gridlines, no panel borders, no fills or shadows; spines are trimmed to the data range and the zero reference line in (b) is hairline-thin and genuinely informative. The only non-data ink of consequence is the two detached legend blocks, which duplicate information that could be carried inline or in the caption.

**READING FLOW:** Problematic — the left→right panel order is natural, but each panel forces a downward jump to a legend block, then a jump back up to match color to curve. In (a) three long legend entries must be cross-referenced against a dense, highly overlapping curve bundle, which is the most expensive lookup in the figure. The (b) panel label is displaced from the (a) label position, breaking the expected top-left rhythm.

**CHARTJUNK:**
- Two separate legend blocks carrying parameter text (ν values, taper counts, segment lengths) that properly belongs in the caption, not in the graphic.
- Legend entries in (b) duplicate the series identities already established in (a); only the "minus multitaper" relation is new.
- The median |diff| values embedded in the (b) legend are summary statistics masquerading as legend labels.

**WHITE SPACE:** Crowded in the plot panels, excessive below them — panel (a)'s three curves are packed into roughly one-third of the figure height while the lower third is consumed by legend text, and there is a wide empty gutter between the (a) and (b) legend blocks and to the right of the (b) block.

**AXES:** Issues —
- dB is a log/ratio scale, so non-zero origins are defensible; (a) happens to include 0 and (b) is centered on 0, both fine.
- **Data clipping:** the orange trace in (b) plunges below the bottom axis limit near 0.8 Hz and is cut off; the same low-frequency excursion is truncated or nearly so in (a). Truncated data without an explicit break marker misrepresents the extreme.
- The y-axis label in (b), "difference from multitaper (dB)", is clipped at the top of the canvas — the "(dB" is cut by the figure boundary.
- x-axis label "frequency (Hz)" and identical 0–40 tick sets are repeated in both panels; a shared axis treatment would recover ink and height.

**ERROR BARS:** Not present — the caption quantifies the estimators' own noise (1.6, 2.1, 1.5 dB) but the figure shows no visual reference for it, so the reader cannot see that the differences fall within noise. This is the figure's central claim and it is stated only in prose.

**PANEL LABELS:** Inconsistent — both are bold and parenthesized, but (a) sits clear of the axes at top-left while (b) is pushed right and upward into the y-axis label, colliding with it and getting clipped at the canvas edge.

**ASPECT RATIO:** Needs adjustment — the 3.6:1 overall width is right for spectra, but the usable plot height is too small for (a)'s ~55 dB range plus twenty-odd harmonic peaks; peaks and valleys compress until the three curves are hard to separate. Reclaiming the legend band would raise panel height by roughly 40% at no cost in width.

**ANNOTATION BOXES:** Replaceable — no callout boxes are present, but the parenthetical ν annotations inside the legend text act as annotations and would read better as a small table or caption text. The median |diff| figures in (b) would be far more effective as two thin horizontal reference lines at ±1.6 and ±0.9 dB, drawn in the matching series colors.

**LEGEND STYLE:** Box (frameless, detached, below axes) — with three series in (a) and two in (b), both legends are prime candidates for elimination. The curves in (a) are heavily interleaved, but each has a segment where it separates cleanly: the orange trace is isolated in the valleys around 7–13 Hz, the pink trace rides above the others through the same valleys, and the blue trace tracks the peaks; short colored labels ("multitaper", "smooth", "average") placed at those separations with thin leader lines would remove the lookup entirely. In (b) the two curves are cleanly separated by sign over most of the range — pink almost entirely above zero, orange almost entirely below — so inline labels at, say, 32–36 Hz above and below the zero line would suffice with no leader lines at all.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] The y-axis label in panel (b) is clipped by the top figure boundary and collides with the "(b)" panel label; the text "(dB)" is partially cut off.
- [BLOCKING] Data are truncated without notice: the orange difference trace in (b) extends below the bottom axis limit near 0.8 Hz, and the corresponding orange dip in (a) is at or past the lower limit. Either extend the limits, mark an axis break, or state the excursion's true magnitude.
- [BLOCKING] Undefined uncertainty: the caption asserts the differences are "the size of the estimators' own noise" but no interval, band, or reference line encodes that noise in the figure, and the nature of the quoted 95th percentiles is not graphically defined.
- [ADVISORY] Detached legend blocks on two multi-series line charts; both should be replaced by direct inline labeling.
- [ADVISORY] Panel labels inconsistently positioned — (a) clear at top-left, (b) shifted right and into the y-axis label.
- [ADVISORY] Legends carry method parameters and summary statistics that belong in the caption or as reference lines.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (a) is vertically compressed; the three curves overlap so tightly through 5–20 Hz that discrimination depends entirely on color.
- [ADVISORY] Redundant x-axis label and tick set repeated across panels.
- [ADVISORY] Caption calls the third series "purple" while the rendered color reads as pink/magenta; align the caption wording with the plotted hue.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Delete both legend blocks. In (a), label the three curves inline in their own colors where they separate most cleanly (orange in the 7–13 Hz valleys, pink just above it there, blue on a peak shoulder near 25–30 Hz), each with a short thin leader line in the series color. In (b), place "smooth minus multitaper" below the zero line and "average minus multitaper" above it near 33–37 Hz, no leaders needed. Move all ν, taper-count, and segment-length detail to the caption.
- Redistribute the reclaimed bottom third to panel height. This alone will resolve most of the curve-crowding in (a).
- Fix the (b) y-axis label clipping and move both panel labels to a common position — flush top-left of each panel's axes bounding box, outside the y-axis label, same bold weight and size.
- Extend the (b) y-limits to contain the full low-frequency orange excursion, or insert an explicit axis break with the peak value annotated inline. Do the same for (a) if the orange minimum is being cut.
- Show the noise the caption claims: add a light gray band spanning ±1.6 dB (or ±4.3√(2/ν) for the relevant pair) across panel (b), drawn behind the curves, and state in the caption what it represents. This converts the figure's key argument from prose into visual evidence and replaces the median |diff| legend text.
- Consider thin horizontal reference lines at the two median |diff| values, in the matching series colors, as a lighter-weight alternative to the shaded band.
- Share the x-axis between panels — one "frequency (Hz)" label and one tick set if the panels are vertically stacked, or keep side-by-side but drop the duplicated axis title from the right panel if the range is identical and stated once.
- Consider slightly increasing the line weight distinction between the three curves in (a) (e.g., multitaper marginally heavier as the reference), so identity does not rest on color alone.

### figure8.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Good — the three spectrograms and the two spectral overlays are genuinely data-dense; no gridlines, 3D effects, shadows, or background fills that fail to encode information. Only spines that appear to be drawn are left/bottom on the line panels, which is correct. Two minor redundancies: (i) x tick labels are repeated on (c), (e) and (g) although they share an identical time axis and a single x label on (g) — the interior tick labels could be dropped; (ii) the colour scale (10–45 dB) is saturated over large ictal regions, so a lot of yellow ink carries no gradation.

READING FLOW:     Problematic — two specific breaks:
  1. Panel (d) plots four series with no key; the key lives inside panel (f), one row *below*. The reader must jump forward, then back. 
  2. The vertical colorbar sits in the gutter between (e) and (f), vertically aligned with (e) only, so it reads as belonging to (e) alone even though it serves (c), (e) and (g). It also visually separates the two columns at mid-height, interrupting the top→bottom scan of the right column.

CHARTJUNK:        
  - None of the classic ornamentation (no shadows, no 3D, no decorative fills).
  - The seizure shading in (a) and the dashed instant markers do encode information and should stay.
  - The four-entry legend box in (f) is the only element that functions as removable overhead.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate overall, but unevenly distributed — the right column has generous inter-row gaps while panel (h)'s x label is being squeezed off the canvas; reallocating a few millimetres from the gutters to the right margin would fix the clipping.

AXES:             Issues — 
  - Panel (h) x-axis label is truncated at the figure boundary: "multitaper minus Hann-then-box (d" — the "(dB)" is cut off. BLOCKING.
  - Spectrograms (c), (e), (g) have no tick label at t = 0 (first label is 100 s) while panel (a) does; since (a) and (c/e/g) are meant to be read as vertically registered time axes, add the 0 tick (or explicitly start the spectrogram axis at the first window centre and say so).
  - Non-zero / negative baselines in (d), (f) are fine — decibel is a log ratio scale; (h) and (b) are correctly centred/zeroed. No inverted axes.
  - Panel (b) y label is bare "μV"; give it the same treatment as (a) ("C4 (μV)" or "amplitude (μV)").

ERROR BARS:       Not present — acceptable here, but note that (d) and (f) are single-realisation spectra whose smoothness differs only by ν; a jackknife or χ²-based confidence band on at least the multitaper trace would make the degrees-of-freedom argument visible rather than asserted.

PANEL LABELS:     Mostly consistent — all eight present, bold, parenthesised, upper-left. Two small inconsistencies: the right-column labels (b), (d), (f), (h) sit on the same baseline as the panel titles and crowd them, whereas (a), (c), (e), (g) sit slightly above/left of the title; and (c), (e), (g) labels drift inward over the y-axis region. Align all eight to a common offset from each panel's top-left axes corner.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — wide-and-short for the time series and spectrograms, near-square for the spectra, wide for the histogram. Panel (b) is proportionally a little tall for a 6-s trace; widening it (or trimming its height) would let the 6–8 Hz ictal rhythm be counted by eye.

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — the dashed instant markers in (a) and the white dashed markers in (c/e/g) are the minimal correct annotation and do not overlap text. The "median |diff| 1.5 dB" statistic in (h) is carried as a title; it would read better as inline text beside the distribution's mode or as a short tick/reference mark at the median, rather than as a heading.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box — detached four-entry legend inside panel (f), occupying roughly the upper third of that panel's plotting area. It does not appear to overlap the plotted curves (curves peak near 60 dB, legend sits above), so this is ADVISORY rather than blocking, but it imposes a lookup cost on *two* panels from one location.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (h) x-axis label is clipped by the figure edge ("...Hann-then-box (d"). Journals will reject or rasterise this; it must be fully visible.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (d) has no key at all; its series identities are only recoverable from the legend in (f), forcing a forward-then-back jump.
  - [ADVISORY] Detached legend box in (f) can be replaced by direct end-of-curve labelling.
  - [ADVISORY] Colorbar placement in the inter-column gutter, aligned only with (e), misattributes a shared colour scale to a single panel and interrupts the right-column reading flow.
  - [ADVISORY] Colour scale saturates (large uniform yellow bands in the ictal window and in the <3 Hz band of all three spectrograms); the reader cannot see how much the three estimators differ where it matters most.
  - [ADVISORY] Redundant x tick labels on (c) and (e); missing t = 0 tick on all three spectrograms, breaking registration with (a).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel-label vertical offset differs between left and right columns.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) y-axis label is a bare unit.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Increase the right margin (or reduce right-column width by ~3%) so "multitaper minus Hann-then-box (dB)" fits; verify at final single-column/double-column width.
  - Delete the legend box in (f) and label the four traces inline at the right edge of panel (d), in the matching series colour: place "Hann (ν=2)" on the grey trace near 26–28 Hz where it runs lowest, "multitaper (ν=6)" and "Hann+box (ν=4.8)" staggered vertically at 29–30 Hz, and "Welch (ν=7.7)" on the pink trace near 24 Hz. The four traces converge at high frequency, so stagger the labels vertically by at least one text height and use thin same-colour leader lines for the two closest pairs (multitaper/Hann-then-box) rather than letting the text sit on the curves. Labelling in (d) rather than (f) puts the key at first encounter and serves both panels.
  - Move the colorbar to a single horizontal bar spanning the full width of the left column beneath (g), or a vertical bar spanning the full height of (c)–(g), so it unambiguously applies to all three spectrograms. Use `extend='both'` (arrowheads) to declare that values beyond 10 and 45 dB are clipped.
  - Consider widening the colour range or switching (g)→(e) comparison to a signed difference map; alternatively add faint contour lines at 40 and 45 dB so structure survives inside the saturated region.
  - Suppress x tick labels on (c) and (e), keep them only on (g), and add the t = 0 tick so the four time axes register vertically with (a).
  - Set all eight panel labels with an identical transform offset from the axes bounding box (e.g. x=-0.12, y=1.05 in axes coordinates) and keep titles on a separate line so labels never sit beside title text.
  - Rename (b)'s y label to "C4 (μV)" for consistency with (a).
  - Optional: move the "median |diff| = 1.5 dB" figure out of (h)'s title into inline text at the distribution's peak, with a short vertical reference tick at the median on the x-axis.

### figure9.png

---
DATA-INK RATIO:   Needs work — the core plotting is lean (no gridlines, no 3D, no shadows, perceptually uniform viridis, single shared colorbar), but there is redundant ink: "frequency (Hz)" is repeated three times for three identically scaled axes, and full four-sided frames enclose the spectrograms (only the left/bottom are informative; the image extent itself defines the panel). Panel (a) carries unlabelled x-ticks that do nothing because the axis is meant to be shared.

READING FLOW:     Problematic — the top-to-bottom stack is correct, but panel (a) is drawn to a **wider x-extent** than panels (b)–(d) (its right edge runs ~150 px past theirs, into the colorbar column). The single time axis at the bottom therefore does not apply to (a), and the two shaded seizure intervals do not sit vertically above the harmonic stacks they mark. The reader must estimate a horizontal rescaling to connect (a) to (b)–(d) — exactly the backtracking a stacked time-aligned figure exists to prevent. Secondarily, the colorbar at mid-right pulls the eye out of the vertical column between panels (b) and (c).

CHARTJUNK:
  - Four-sided frames on the spectrogram panels (top/right spines carry no information).
  - Unlabelled tick marks on panel (a)'s x-axis (implies a shared axis that is not actually shared).
  - Triplicated y-axis label text on identical 0–30 Hz axes.

WHITE SPACE:      Adequate overall, with two local problems: an unusually large vertical gap between (a) and (b) relative to the (b)/(c)/(d) gaps, and a dead column between the spectrogram right edge and the colorbar that exists only because panel (a) was allowed to overrun it.

AXES:             OK — panel (a) includes zero and the ±500 µV clip is documented in the caption; frequency axes begin at 0 Hz; time begins at 0 s. No inversions. The colorbar's 10–45 range not starting at zero is justified (decibel/log scale), though the caption should state the dB reference (dB re 1 µV²/Hz, presumably).

ERROR BARS:       Not present — appropriate for spectrograms; the degrees of freedom ν stated in each panel title serve as the variability annotation, which is a good Tufte-consistent substitution.

PANEL LABELS:     Consistent — (a)–(d) are bold, same size, top-left of each panel, outside the data region.

ASPECT RATIO:     Appropriate — wide-and-short panels suit a 600 s record; the 4-row stack preserves time alignment (once the x-limit bug is fixed).

ANNOTATION BOXES: Clean — the seizure shading in (a) is data ink (interval encoding), not a box, and the per-panel titles are unboxed inline text. No callouts, no borders, no overlap.

LEGEND STYLE:     Box (colorbar) — unavoidable for pseudocolor and correctly shared across (b)–(d) with a single label; it does not overlap data. However, its vertical extent is centred arbitrarily rather than spanning the top of (b) to the bottom of (d), so it reads as floating rather than as belonging to the three panels.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Panel (a) x-limits do not match panels (b)–(d). The time axis is shared in appearance only; the shaded seizure intervals are horizontally offset from the corresponding harmonic stacks in the spectrograms, which invites misreading of seizure onset/offset times.
  - [ADVISORY] Seizure intervals are marked only in (a). The caption's central claim ("each seizure is a harmonic stack descending from ~20 Hz to 3 Hz") must be verified by eye across a 150-px-misaligned gap with no fiducial in (b)–(d).
  - [ADVISORY] Colorbar height is not keyed to the panels it serves; it should span exactly from the top of (b) to the bottom of (d).
  - [ADVISORY] Unlabelled x-ticks on (a) plus top/right spines on (b)–(d) are non-data ink.
  - [ADVISORY] "frequency (Hz)" repeated three times; "power (dB)" lacks a stated reference level.
  - [ADVISORY] Vertical gap between (a) and (b) is larger than the inter-spectrogram gaps, weakening the perceptual grouping of the four panels as one time-aligned object.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Share the x-axis across all four panels (e.g. `sharex=True` or set identical `xlim=(0, 600)`), and reserve the colorbar column with a gridspec so panel (a) ends at the same pixel as (b)–(d). This is the one change that makes the figure read correctly.
  - Carry the seizure markers down: either repeat the translucent shading as thin vertical spans in (b)–(d), or place two short tick bars / a slim ribbon strip immediately under panel (a) that runs the full width, so onset and offset are readable in every panel without a lookup.
  - Set the colorbar axes to span the full height of (b)–(d) and drop its surrounding frame to a single thin outline.
  - Remove x-tick marks from (a) (keep the bottom spine) and strip the top/right spines from the spectrograms.
  - Keep the y-label on the middle spectrogram only, or place one rotated "frequency (Hz)" label centred across (b)–(d); this recovers a few percent of width for the data.
  - Tighten the (a)→(b) gap to match the other inter-panel gaps so the stack reads as a single aligned column.
  - In the caption, state the dB reference for the power scale and the colour-scale limits (10–45 dB) explicitly, since "same colour scale" is doing comparative work across three panels.
