## Color & Accessibility Auditor

---
PER-FIGURE:

**efigure1.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — each hue = one kernel/method (blue = multitaper, green = untapered+box, gold = Hann+box, light blue = Bohman+box); black dotted = ideal box reference. No decorative fill.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Borderline — 4 hues + black on a 5-series plot. Acceptable because every series is a distinct method, but the two blues push the count of *hue families* to 3 (blue, green/gold, light blue).
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk (minor) — dark blue (multitaper) vs. light blue (Bohman) differ mainly in lightness, which survives grayscale but reads as "same method, two variants" and is hard to separate where the curves overlap at the shoulders (a). Green(teal)/gold pair is Okabe-Ito-like and is safe under deuteranopia/protanopia.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good — the set (teal-green ≈ #009E73, gold ≈ #E69F00, blue ≈ #0072B2, sky ≈ #56B4E9) is essentially Okabe-Ito. Recommend giving Bohman a non-blue Okabe-Ito slot (e.g. reddish-purple #CC79A7) or a dashed line style, since it is the accent that matters most in (b).

**efigure2.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — grey = raw periodogram (noise reference), black = truth, green/blue/gold = methods; solid vs. dashed blue = K=7 vs. K=6.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 3 accent hues plus two neutrals (grey, black), the neutral-for-reference convention is exactly right.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk (minor) — in panel (b) the dashed blue (K=6) and the gold Hann curve lie almost on top of each other in the 0–0.2 band; distinguishing them depends on hue alone at 1-px width. Under protanopia the gold darkens and the overlap becomes ambiguous. K=7 vs. K=6 is encoded by dash only, which is fine, but the dashes are barely visible at this line width.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good — same Okabe-Ito-like set as efigure1. Increase dash length/line width for K=6, or plot K=6 in a distinct hue.

**efigure3.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — 8 series, each a method; colour is doubled with marker shape (circle/square/triangle/diamond) and line style, which is the right redundant encoding.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Excessive by the raw count (5 hue families: blue, green, gold, vermilion, pink, plus a second blue tint) but justified by 8 methods and mitigated by marker redundancy. Consider dropping to grey all "context" methods and colouring only the 2–3 you argue about.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk — the critical problem pair is **gold (Hann → box) vs. vermilion/dark-orange (Hann → Gaussian)**. Under deuteranopia and protanopia both collapse to similar dull yellow-brown, and in grayscale they have nearly identical lightness. They are also conceptually adjacent (both "Hann then smooth"), so confusion is likely. Marker shape (circle vs. triangle) is the only surviving cue. Secondary risk: mid-blue vs. sky-blue (K=2NW−1 vs. Bohman) and the three blue multitaper variants, which rely on dash + marker.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good in provenance (Okabe-Ito), weak in assignment. Suggest: reassign Hann→Gaussian to reddish-purple (#CC79A7) and move Welch to grey, or keep both oranges but make one clearly darker *and* dashed.
- (Non-colour, noted in passing: the y-axis labels of panels (b) and (c) are clipped/overlapping the neighbouring axes.)

**efigure4.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful and well-stratified — viridis samples encode the *ordered* taper index k in rows 1–2; method identity colours (pink/orange/green/blue) encode the column's method in rows 3–4; light-blue shading encodes |f|≤W.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good given the size; the ordered/categorical split is handled correctly.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes, largely — viridis is safe; the four method colours appear one per panel, so no within-panel discrimination is required. The only issue is cross-panel: the orange of column 2 (Hann+Gaussian) vs. the gold used for Hann+box elsewhere in the paper.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good — viridis for ordered index, Okabe-Ito-like for categories, consistent with figure5.

**figure1.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — thick grey = reference estimate, green = smoothed route, dashed pink = sliding-window route; the neutral-reference-plus-two-accents design is exemplary.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 2 accents + grey. Best-in-set.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes — green(teal)/pink separate under all three simulations and in grayscale (different lightness).
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good. **But the caption calls the pink curve "dashed purple"** — the rendered colour is reddish-pink (#CC79A7-like), not purple. Same mismatch recurs in figure6 and figure7.

**figure2.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — grey = periodogram/neutral, black = truth, gold = Hann-based estimate, blue bar/blue shading = kernel half-power width and |f|≤W band. Grey fill = true-spectrum shape (explicitly "shape only"), correctly de-emphasised.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — one accent hue (gold) plus a blue annotation accent; everything else neutral.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes — gold/blue/grey/black is robust under deuteranopia, protanopia and grayscale.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good. Minor: in (e) the kernel is drawn grey on grey fill, so the kernel/true-spectrum distinction depends on lightness only; a darker outline would help.

**figure3.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful within the figure — (a) grey = record, gold = tapered record, black dashed = taper; (b) black = total, blue = noise, orange = bias, dark-orange circles = highlighted operating points.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 2 accents per panel.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes — blue/orange is the single most colourblind-robust categorical pair available.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good, but the *semantics* break the paper's convention: here blue = "noise/variance" and orange = "bias", whereas in figures 1, 2, 5–9 blue = multitaper and gold = Hann. A reader who has learned "blue = multitaper" will mis-read panel (b). Also the vermilion highlight circles reuse the Hann→Gaussian colour from efigure3/figure5.

**figure4.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — viridis encodes ordered taper index (a, b) and ordered number-of-tapers (c); black dashed = ideal box; blue = 1−λ_k; orange dashed / grey dotted = 2NW and K markers.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes — viridis plus blue/orange/black.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Needs work on *mapping consistency*: viridis is re-normalised per panel, so the same label means different colours across panels — k=5 is green-teal in (a) but a different sample in (b) (6 vs. 8 tapers spanning the ramp), and in (c) the colours index "first N tapers", not k. Fix the colour→k mapping once (e.g. always sample viridis over k = 0…9) and reuse it in (a), (b), (c). Also: the |f|≤W band is shaded **grey** here but **light blue** in figure2(e–h), figure6(e–h) and efigure4(m–p) — same concept, two colours. Legends in (a), (b), (c) overlap the axis labels.

**figure5.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — viridis for the ordered bank index; method colour for the weight-ladder bars.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes within-panel (one colour per panel). Cross-panel, the pink/orange/green/blue set is safe.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good. One risk: panel (f) bars are vermilion (Hann+Gaussian) while the paper's Hann+box is gold; because the column header says "smooth one periodogram (Hann, Gaussian)", the colour is technically right, but readers will likely read vermilion and gold as the same "Hann" family. Consider a single Hann hue with a line-style/hatch distinction for box vs. Gaussian.

**figure6.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — green/blue/gold/pink = the four estimators, carried consistently from the legends of figures 1, 5 and 7; grey fill = true spectrum shape; blue shading = |f|≤W.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Borderline-Good — 4 hues for 4 estimators, one per panel, so no within-panel clutter.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes — green/blue/gold/pink is discriminable under deuteranopia and protanopia; in grayscale the four differ in lightness, and panel separation removes any residual ambiguity.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good. Caption again says Welch is "purple" when it is drawn pink.

**figure7.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — blue = multitaper, gold = Hann+box, pink = Welch; all consistent with figure6.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — 3 accents, no decoration.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes — blue/gold/pink is safe. Grayscale: gold and pink have similar lightness and both are thin lines in (a); consider a dash for Welch.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good. Caption says "purple" for the pink Welch curve.

**figure8.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Mostly meaningful — viridis spectrograms on one shared 10–45 dB scale (excellent); peach shading = seizure interval; blue and orange dashed verticals = the two time instants; (b) trace orange = ictal rhythm; (d, f) line colours = estimators (grey/blue/gold/pink), consistent with figures 6–7; (h) grey histogram neutral.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Excessive by a small margin — the panel-(a) annotations introduce blue and orange as *time markers*, colliding with blue = multitaper and gold = Hann used two panels later; the (b) trace orange is a third role for orange.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: At risk (minor) — in (d) and (f) four curves share one axis: grey, blue, gold, pink. Under achromatopsia gold and pink converge, and the grey periodogram is light enough to be confused with them where curves cross. Viridis panels are fully safe.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good — viridis, shared colourbar, single colour scale across figures 8 and 9 is exactly right. Recommend making the time-marker verticals both neutral (black/grey dashed, differentiated by label) so blue/orange retain their estimator meaning; recolour the (b) trace to match its marker or leave it black.

**figure9.png**
- COLOR PURPOSE: Meaningful — viridis spectrograms, shared scale with figure8; peach shading = seizure intervals; black traces.
- COLOR ECONOMY: Good — one sequential map + one annotation tint.
- COLORBLIND SAFE: Yes — viridis; the peach shading is distinguishable from white by lightness alone.
- PALETTE QUALITY: Good — cleanest colour design in the set.

CROSS-FIGURE:
- **Caption/figure colour-name mismatch (3 figures).** figure1 ("dashed purple"), figure6 ("purple") and figure7 ("purple") all describe a curve that is rendered reddish-pink. Either rename to "pink" or change the ink to an actual purple.
- **Multitaper is blue everywhere except figure1**, where the reference multitaper estimate is thick grey. Defensible (grey = "the exact reference"), but it means the paper's flagship identity colour is absent from its opening figure. Consider a thick light-blue reference band, or state the grey convention in the caption.
- **Blue and gold are re-used for non-method meanings** in figure3(b) (blue = noise, orange = bias), figure4(d) (blue = 1−λ_k, orange dashed = 2NW), figure8(a,b) (blue/orange dashed = time markers, orange = EEG trace). Each is locally clear but globally erodes the "blue = multitaper, gold = Hann" mapping the paper otherwise maintains very well.
- **Two oranges for two Hann variants.** Gold = Hann→box (efig1, efig2, efig3, fig2, fig3, fig6, fig7, fig8); vermilion = Hann→Gaussian (efig3, fig5f, efig4-col2) and also the highlight circles in figure3(b). Internally consistent but the two are the least separable pair in the palette for red-green deficiencies.
- **|f| ≤ W shading changes colour**: light blue in figure2(e–h), figure6(e–h), efigure4(m–p); grey in figure4(b). Same quantity, two encodings.
- **Ordered-index colours (viridis) are re-normalised per panel** in figure4(a) vs. (b) vs. (c), so identical k labels get different colours inside one figure.
- PALETTE HARMONY: Strong. The whole set reads as one paper: an Okabe-Ito-style categorical palette for methods, viridis for ordered indices and images, grey/black for references, with a shared 10–45 dB colourbar across figures 8 and 9. Saturation and line weights are consistent. No jarring mismatch.

ISSUES:
- [BLOCKING] figure1, figure6, figure7 captions call a pink curve "purple". Colour names in captions must match the ink, or colourblind and print readers cannot resolve the legend at all.
- [BLOCKING] figure4: viridis is sampled independently in panels (a), (b) and (c), so the same taper index k is drawn in different colours within a single figure; the (c) mapping additionally encodes a different variable (number of tapers) with the same ramp.
- [BLOCKING] efigure3: gold (Hann → box) vs. vermilion (Hann → Gaussian) is the primary categorical contrast for two closely related methods and collapses under deuteranopia, protanopia and grayscale. Currently rescued only by marker shape.
- [ADVISORY] Semantic colour collisions: blue = multitaper vs. blue = "noise" (figure3b) / "1−λ_k" (figure4d) / pre-ictal time marker (figure8a); gold = Hann vs. bias (figure3b) / 2NW marker (figure4d) / ictal trace and marker (figure8a,b).
- [ADVISORY] Multitaper drawn grey in figure1 but blue in figures 6–8.
- [ADVISORY] |f|≤W shading is light blue in figure2/figure6/efigure4 but grey in figure4(b).
- [ADVISORY] efigure1/efigure3: dark blue vs. sky blue (multitaper vs. Bohman) are the same hue family for unrelated methods.
- [ADVISORY] efigure2(b): dashed blue (K=6) and gold (Hann) overlap for half the axis with dashes too fine to resolve.
- [ADVISORY] figure8(d,f) and figure7(a): gold and pink have near-identical grayscale lightness in multi-curve panels.
- [ADVISORY] Non-colour but affecting legibility of the colour legends: clipped y-axis labels in efigure3(b,c) and figure8(h); legends overlapping axis labels in figure4(a–c), figure5 and efigure4 headers.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
- Publish a single palette table in the methods/SI and use it everywhere: multitaper = #0072B2 (blue), untapered→box = #009E73 (green), Hann→box = #E69F00 (gold), Welch/average = #CC79A7 (pink), Bohman→box = #56B4E9 (sky), Hann→Gaussian = #CC79A7-variant or hatched gold, periodogram/record/reference = grey, truth = black. This is Okabe-Ito, which the figures already approximate.
- Reserve blue and gold exclusively for multitaper and Hann. Recolour figure3(b) noise/bias to black-solid vs. black-dashed, or to grey vs. vermilion; recolour figure4(d) 2NW/K markers to grey/black; make figure8(a) time markers both black dashed with text labels, and leave the figure8(b) trace black.
- Replace vermilion for Hann→Gaussian with a distinct hue (e.g. #CC79A7 if Welch moves to grey) or keep gold and distinguish box vs. Gaussian by dashed vs. solid — then the two "Hann then smooth" curves read as one family with two treatments, which is the actual claim.
- Fix the three captions to say "pink", or switch the ink to a true purple (#7B3294) if "purple" is preferred.
- Standardise the |f|≤W band to a single light blue (#DEEBF7) in every kernel panel, including figure4(b).
- In figure4, define one viridis→k mapping over the full k range used anywhere in the figure and reuse it in (a) and (b); give panel (c) a separate, clearly different encoding (e.g. a single hue in increasing lightness, or grey-to-black) since it indexes cumulative taper count, not k.
- Give sky-blue (Bohman) a non-blue slot, or add a dash pattern, so it is not read as a multitaper variant in efigure1/efigure3.
- In efigure2, increase dash length and line width for K=6, and in efigure3 consider greying out Welch and the untapered curves so only the 2–3 compared methods carry colour.
- Run the final set through a deuteranopia/protanopia/grayscale simulator, checking specifically: efigure3 gold-vs-vermilion, efigure1 blue-vs-sky, figure7(a) and figure8(d,f) gold-vs-pink-vs-grey.
- Keep as-is: the shared 10–45 dB viridis colourbar across figures 8 and 9, the grey-reference convention, and the neutral "true spectrum, shape only" fills — these are the strongest colour decisions in the manuscript.

    AXIS LABELS:        Complete in content, but **the y-axis titles of panels (b) and (c) are clipped/overlapping the adjacent panel** ("kernel power beyond 2× half-power half-width" runs into panel (b); "peak beyond 1.5× half-power half-width (dB)" is truncated at the left edge, losing the leading character). Unit-less quantity in (b) ("kernel power beyond 2×…") should state it is a fraction of total kernel power.

  **efigure4.png** (2097×2191 px)

    AXIS LABELS:        Missing units/ambiguous: "amplitude (curves offset)" — parenthetical is a display note, not a unit; use "amplitude (a.u.)" with the offset noted in the caption. "weight c_k (normalized)" acceptable. "sample t" and "taper index k" are dimensionless (fine).

    TITLE PLACEMENT:    Incorrect (borderline) — descriptive titles inside all four panels, including the figure-level statement in (a).

CROSS-FIGURE:
  - Font family is uniform across all 13 figures (one sans-serif + matching italic mathtext); no mixed-typeface problems detected. Confirm the mathtext family is set to the same face as the text (e.g. `mathtext.fontset` matched to Helvetica/Arial) rather than the default.
  - Font *sizes* are not uniform in effective (printed) terms. All figures were drawn at a similar point size in the source, but the pixel widths differ (1832–2172 px) and figure8 in particular is ~13% lower resolution than efigure1, so at a common 6.875 in reproduction width the same nominal element prints ~6.5 pt in figure8 vs ~7.3 pt in efigure1. figure5 and efigure4 share identical panels but at different scales.
  - In-panel legend text is systematically smaller than axis/tick text (≈4–6 pt vs ≈7 pt) in efigure4, figure2, figure4, figure5, figure6 and figure8 — an inconsistent hierarchy; legends should be at least as large as tick labels (8–9 pt recommended).
  - Panel-letter style is consistent: bold, lower-case, parenthesised "(a)". However panel letters collide with panel titles or y-axis titles in efigure3, efigure4, figure1, figure2, figure3, figure4, figure5, figure6, figure7, figure8 — i.e. the layout margin convention is not applied consistently anywhere.
  - Capitalization is consistently lower-case/sentence style for axis titles, legends and panel titles (proper nouns Hann, Welch, Slepian, Bohman, Gaussian, Thomson correctly capitalised). Minor deviations: "vs" (figure3 title) vs "versus" (efigure3 caption); "2-s windows" (figure8/9) vs "0.70-s segments" vs "1.5 s" hyphenation of units differs.
  - Unit/normalisation conventions differ for equivalent quantities: "kernel, linear (units of N)" (efigure1) vs "kernel (unit area)" (figure4) vs "|U_k(f)|² (unit-norm tapers)" (figure4); "window length L / N" (figure1) vs "half-power width (units of 1/N)" (efigure3).
  - Amplitude axes are labelled three different ways for the same kind of quantity: "amplitude (curves offset)" (figure5, efigure4), "taper (offset)" (figure4), "x_t" (figure3), "µV" (figure8b), "Fp1 (µV)" / "C4 (µV)" (figure9a, figure8a).

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] figure8 panel (h): x-axis title truncated — "multitaper minus Hann-then-box (d"; the unit "(dB)" is lost.
  - [BLOCKING] efigure3 panels (b) and (c): y-axis titles clipped at the left edge and overlapping the neighbouring panel, rendering them partly unreadable.
  - [BLOCKING] Legend/annotation text below the 6 pt floor at double-column width: efigure4 (rows 1–2 in-panel legends, ~4.2–5.1 pt), figure5 (~4.6–5.5 pt), figure8 panel (f) legend (~4.3–4.9 pt), figure4 (b)/(d) legends (~5.0–6.0 pt).
  - [BLOCKING] figure4 panels (a) and (b): legends overlap the x-axis titles, obscuring "sample t" and "frequency offset (units of 1/N)".
  - [BLOCKING] Bold panel letters overlap adjacent text in efigure4 ((c),(d)), figure2 ((c),(g)), figure3 ((b)), figure4 ((b)), figure5 ((c),(d)), figure6 ((g)), figure7 ((b)), figure8 ((c),(e),(g),(h)) — text collisions are a reproduction defect regardless of size.
  - [BLOCKING] "EEG" and "ELROND" are used in figure8 and figure9 (titles and captions) without ever being expanded/defined; electrode labels C4 and Fp1 are not attributed to the 10–20 system.
  - [ADVISORY] Missing or ambiguous units: figure3(a) y-axis "x_t"; figure8(b) y-axis "µV" with no quantity name; figure4(a) "taper (offset)"; efigure4/figure5 "amplitude (curves offset)"; efigure3(b) ordinate is a dimensionless fraction but not stated as such.
  - [ADVISORY] Abbreviations used inside figures without in-figure or in-caption definition: "dof" (efigure3a), "re" as in "dB re peak" (efigure1, efigure4, figure2, figure6), "|diff|" (figure7b, figure8h), "RMS" (figure3b), "Q" (efigure4), "PSD" (efigure2, figure2, figure6 legends — expanded only in figure1's caption).
  - [ADVISORY] Figure/panel titles set inside the artwork: efigure4, figure2, figure3, figure4, figure5, figure6, figure8, figure9 all carry sentence-length descriptive or interpretive titles in the plot area ("no taper: leaky and noisy", "the record: N = 1024 samples of the AR(4) process", "scalp EEG, channel C4, common-average reference; seizure shaded"). Most journals require this material in the bold caption/legend, not in the figure.
  - [ADVISORY] Effective font size varies across the set because figure pixel widths differ (1832–2172 px); equivalent elements will not print at equal pt size.
  - [ADVISORY] Legend text is smaller than tick-label text in six figures, inverting the recommended hierarchy (legends 8–9 pt, ticks 7–8 pt).
  - [ADVISORY] Minor style inconsistencies: "vs" vs "versus"; unit hyphenation ("2-s windows" / "1.5 s" / "0.70-s segments"); three different normalisation phrasings for kernel ordinates; "window length L / N" vs "(units of 1/N)".
  - [ADVISORY] No statistical significance markers appear anywhere, so no undefined markers; if any are added later (e.g. to figure3 or figure8h) they must be defined in the caption.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Regenerate all figures with a single rcParams block: `font.family` = Helvetica/Arial (and a matched `mathtext` set), tick labels 7–8 pt, axis titles 8–9 pt, legend text 8–9 pt, panel letters 9–10 pt; render every figure at exactly the intended publication width (3.25 in or 6.875 in) at ≥600 dpi so printed sizes are identical across the set.
  - Apply `constrained_layout`/`tight_layout` with explicit padding and re-export; then verify no clipping (figure8h, efigure3b–c, figure7b) and no overlap of legends with axis titles (figure4a–b).
  - Move panel letters to a reserved margin position (e.g. x = 0.0 in axes-fraction coordinates, above the axes box, with `pad`) so they never collide with titles or rotated y-axis labels.
  - Move in-panel legends outside the axes (below the panel or in a shared figure-level legend, as already done in efigure1–efigure3 and figure7) and raise their size to ≥8 pt; delete the 4–5 pt legends in efigure4, figure5 and figure8f.
  - Delete the descriptive/interpretive in-figure titles and transfer that text to the bold caption; if short labels are needed inside the artwork, reduce them to neutral tags (e.g. "no taper", "Hann + box, W = 4/N", "ν = 8.9").
  - Complete axis labels with quantity + unit: "amplitude (a.u.)" for taper/record panels (figure3a, figure4a, figure5, efigure4), "amplitude (µV)" for figure8b, "(dB)" restored on figure8h, "fraction of kernel power beyond 2× half-power half-width (dimensionless)" for efigure3b, and a single consistent kernel-ordinate convention across efigure1, figure2, figure4, figure6.
  - Expand on first use in every caption: electroencephalogram (EEG), power spectral density (PSD), autoregressive (AR), root-mean-square (RMS), degrees of freedom (replace "dof" on the axis), "dB re peak" → "dB relative to peak", "|diff|" → "absolute difference"; add a one-line provenance/definition and citation for the ELROND annotation set and note that C4/Fp1 follow the 10–20 system.
  - Harmonise wording and unit style set-wide: use "versus" everywhere, one hyphenation rule for units of time, and "(units of 1/N)" consistently (including figure1c).

## Technical Format Checker

### efigure1.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2172 × 616 px (aspect 3.53:1) — recommended **double column** — 316 DPI at 6.875 in (303 DPI at the full 181 mm / 7.16 in IEEE double-column width); 668 DPI if forced to single column (3.25 in), but the 3.53:1 aspect ratio makes single-column placement impractical (panel height would fall to ~25 mm and tick labels would be ~3 pt).
RESOLUTION:     Adequate for the 300 DPI color-line-art minimum, **below the 600 DPI preferred level** — figure type: line art (vector-style curves, text, no photographic content); actual 303–316 DPI at double-column width.
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG (review-acceptable only) → PDF or EPS (vector, preferred for this figure) or 600 DPI TIFF/LZW for final submission.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.14 MB (limit 10 MB); there is ample headroom to re-export at 600 DPI or as vector.
TEXT RENDERING: Rasterized — all axis labels, tick labels, panel letters and legend entries are bitmap glyphs at 303–316 effective DPI.
COLOR MODE:     OK (RGB) as submitted — Potential shift if the production workflow converts to CMYK: the saturated teal/green ("untapered"), orange ("Hann"), medium blue ("multitaper") and light blue ("Bohman") are all in the at-risk gamut region; the two blues are the pair most likely to lose separation after conversion.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. Axis/tick/legend text is pixel-based at ~310 DPI, which will show visible stair-stepping in print and at reviewer zoom. IEEE requires vector or ≥600 DPI rendering for line art with text.
  - [BLOCKING] File format: PNG is accepted for review only; final submission must be TIFF, EPS, PDF or PS.
  - [ADVISORY] Resolution 303–316 DPI meets the color line-art minimum but is half the 600 DPI preferred value for graphs; fine curve detail (the multitaper sidelobe ripple in (b), the stair-steps on the Bohman skirt) is the first thing to soften.
  - [ADVISORY] Effective font size at final width is marginal: label text scales to roughly 6.5–7 pt and tick labels to ~6 pt at 181 mm reproduction — at or just above the 6 pt floor, with no margin if an editor reduces the figure.
  - [ADVISORY] Large dead space in the lower-left/center of the canvas (the legend block sits under panel (b)'s left half while the region under panel (a) is empty). This wastes ~25% of the allotted area and forces the panels to be smaller than necessary.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) light-blue (Bohman) trace is clipped at the −100 dB axis floor; the reader cannot tell whether the curve continues or terminates.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption begins with a bold title-style phrase ("\textbf{Kernels at the same design bandwidth}"). IEEE TBME captions run as plain sentences beginning "Fig. 1."; there is no in-figure title, which is correct, but the bold caption lead-in should be de-emphasized.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting: the caption correctly reports estimator degrees of freedom (ν = 14.0, 17.2, 8.9, 7.3) for the noise-level comparison, but it does not state that these are deterministic/analytic kernels rather than averages over realizations. A reader may look for an unstated window count behind the curves.
  - [ADVISORY] Two blues (multitaper vs. Bohman) differ mainly in lightness; in grayscale reproduction or after CMYK conversion they may be confusable, especially where they overlap in (a)'s passband.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting library as vector PDF or EPS with fonts embedded (Type 1/TrueType, no Type 3), sized to a 181 mm canvas; this resolves both BLOCKING items at once. If a raster is required, render at 600 DPI for the final width (≈4280 × 1215 px) and save as LZW-compressed TIFF, RGB.
  - Set the figure width to exactly 181 mm (7.16 in) at creation time and specify font sizes in points (8 pt axis labels, 7–8 pt tick labels, 8 pt legend, 9–10 pt bold panel letters) so the on-page sizes are deterministic rather than a by-product of scaling.
  - Recover the wasted canvas: move the legend into a single row spanning the full width directly beneath both panels, or into panel (a)'s empty upper-right corner, then crop the excess whitespace and enlarge both axes. Reducing overall height to ~40–45 mm while enlarging the plot boxes improves readability at the same column width.
  - Extend panel (b)'s y-axis to −110 or −120 dB, or annotate that the Bohman skirt falls below the displayed floor, so no trace is silently clipped.
  - Differentiate the two blue curves by line style (e.g., dashed for Bohman) or swap one to a hue further from the other, and verify legibility in a grayscale and a CMYK soft-proof before submission.
  - Rewrite the caption opening as plain text, e.g. "Fig. 1. Kernels at the same design bandwidth (N = 256, W = 4/N; Table II). (a) Linear scale near the main lobe. (b) Decibels relative to the peak. ...", and add a clause such as "Curves are analytic expected kernels (no averaging over realizations); ν denotes the equivalent degrees of freedom per spectral estimate."

### efigure2.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2121 × 699 px (aspect 3.03:1) — recommended **double column** — 308 DPI at 6.875 in (296 DPI at the full 181 mm / 7.16 in trim width)
RESOLUTION:     Below threshold (marginal) — line art (2-panel PSD line plots, no gradients/photographic content); 308 DPI at 6.875 in, 296 DPI at 181 mm, 653 DPI only if squeezed to 3.25 in single column (illegible at that size)
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG (acceptable for review only) → PDF or EPS (vector) preferred; TIFF at ≥600 DPI acceptable
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.16 MB (limit 10 MB)
TEXT RENDERING: Rasterized — all tick labels, axis labels, and legend entries are bitmap glyphs; pixel edges will be visible in print at ~300 DPI
COLOR MODE:     OK for submission (RGB required) — but the saturated teal-green (untapered-box curve) and the mid-blue multitaper curve are the two most at-risk hues if the publisher converts to CMYK; the orange will also dull slightly
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. At the recommended double-column width the effective resolution is only ~300 DPI, well below the 600 DPI preferred for line art, so axis numerals and legend text will show stair-stepping.
  - [BLOCKING] Resolution falls *below* 300 DPI (296) if the figure is placed at the full 181 mm double-column width. As supplied there is no headroom: any scaling up puts the figure out of spec.
  - [ADVISORY] Aspect ratio 3.03:1 is very wide/short. At 181 mm the panel height is only ~57 mm, which is what forces the small type; consider a taller layout or moving the legend to a two-line block beneath a slightly taller panel pair.
  - [ADVISORY] Estimated final type size ~7 pt for tick labels at double column — inside the 6 pt floor but below the 8–10 pt preferred range. Unusable at single column (≈3.4 pt).
  - [ADVISORY] Font appears to be a serif face for axis/legend text while the panel labels (a)/(b) are bold sans-serif. Times is acceptable, but mixing families within one figure is inconsistent; IEEE prefers a single sans-serif family.
  - [ADVISORY] The dashed blue "multitaper, K = 6" trace is almost entirely occluded by the solid blue "K = 7" trace in both panels, so the key comparison claimed in the caption ("dropping the seventh taper halves that") is not visually verifiable. The grey periodogram also visually swamps the coloured smoothed estimates in (a).
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is largely compliant — equivalent degrees of freedom ν are given for every estimator and the figure is explicitly "one realization." However, the caption should state that n = 1 realization means no error bars/distributions are shown, and specify the box-smoother half-bandwidth/number of averaged frequency bins that yields ν = 17.2 and ν = 8.9 so the df values are reproducible.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption begins with a bold sentence-style title ("Dropping the leakiest taper."); IEEE style is a brief descriptive caption without a bolded title. Panel labels themselves are correctly lowercase (a), (b).
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting library as vector PDF or EPS at exactly 181 mm width with all fonts embedded (matplotlib: `savefig('efigure2.pdf', bbox_inches='tight')` with `pdf.fonttype=42`). This eliminates both the resolution and rasterized-text blockers in one step.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandated, re-render at ≥600 DPI for a 181 mm canvas (≈4276 px wide) and save as LZW-compressed RGB TIFF.
  - Increase tick-label and legend font size to 8 pt at final printed size; set all text in one sans-serif family (Arial/Helvetica) including the (a)/(b) labels.
  - Reduce the aspect ratio to roughly 2:1 (e.g., 181 × 85–90 mm) so panels are taller and text can be enlarged without crowding; keep the 3-column legend but allow two rows beneath both panels.
  - Make the K = 6 trace distinguishable: use a distinct hue or a lighter/heavier line weight rather than a dashed variant of the same blue, and/or plot it on top with reduced alpha on the grey periodogram (or move the raw periodogram to a background light grey at lower z-order).
  - Trim the caption to a descriptive sentence without the bold title, add "single realization (n = 1); no error bars shown," and state the box-smoother bandwidth (number of averaged DFT bins) used for each ν quoted.
  - Verify final RGB values of the teal, blue, and orange curves after a soft-proof CMYK conversion; if the teal and blue converge, shift the green toward a darker blue-green or differentiate by line style as well as colour.

### efigure3.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     1888 x 916 px — recommended **double** column (181 mm / 7.16 in) — **264 DPI** at that width
                (For reference: 539 DPI at single-column 88 mm / 3.5 in; using the generic 6.875 in / 3.25 in widths gives 275 / 581 DPI.)
                Aspect ratio 2.06:1 → final size ≈ 181 × 88 mm at double column; height is far below the 230 mm limit. Three side-by-side panels plus a 4-column legend make single-column placement unusable (axis numerals would fall to ~4–5 pt).
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (vector-style XY line/marker plots, no photographic content); 264 DPI at double-column width vs. 300 DPI minimum and 600 DPI preferred for color line art
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG (review-acceptable only) → PDF or EPS (vector) preferred; TIFF at ≥600 DPI acceptable as a raster fallback
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.21 MB (limit 10 MB)
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all labels, tick numerals and legend text are pixel-based; anti-aliased edges visible on stroke ends and glyph stems
COLOR MODE:     OK (assumed RGB for PNG; no color_mode field supplied — confirm). IEEE requires RGB, so no conversion needed. Note that the saturated teal/green, orange and magenta series would shift noticeably if any downstream tool converts to CMYK; the two blues (dark blue vs. light blue) plus the dotted-vs-dashed distinction are also the most at-risk pair for print discrimination.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Resolution insufficient: 264 DPI at the required double-column width, below the 300 DPI floor and well below the 600 DPI preference for color line art.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure; tick labels and legend entries will not remain crisp at print size.
  - [BLOCKING] Clipped y-axis labels in panels (b) and (c): the label for (b) ("kernel power beyond 2× half-power half-width") is cut at the left edge of the panel and collides with panel (a)'s x-axis region, and panel (c)'s label renders as "eak beyond 1.5× half-power half-width (dB)" — the leading "p" is truncated. Both must be fully visible and clear of the neighbouring axes.
  - [ADVISORY] Panels (b) and (c) x-axes extend to 25 while data stop near 16; the empty right third widens the figure unnecessarily and pushes the y-labels into the adjacent panel. Consider matching x-limits to the plotted range (or keeping 25 only in panel (a) where Welch reaches it).
  - [ADVISORY] Caption begins with a bold title-style phrase ("**Variance, leakage and side-lobe level versus measured resolution**"). IEEE TBME captions are plain descriptive sentences; remove the bold run-in heading.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting: the caption correctly states the curves are exact computations with no sampling variability, and equivalent dof ν is the plotted quantity in (a) — this satisfies the dof/realization requirement. Add N and the number of swept bandwidth settings per method (marker count) so readers know the curves are interpolated between discrete parameter values, not continuous.
  - [ADVISORY] Color-only differentiation between the two blue multitaper variants relies partly on marker shape (circle vs. triangle vs. square) — verify legibility in grayscale print; the dotted/dashed/solid line styles are present and should be preserved in the vector export.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel labels (a)/(b)/(c) are lowercase in parentheses — compliant; keep this style in the caption cross-references as written.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting source as **PDF or EPS** at a physical canvas of 181 mm × ~88 mm with fonts embedded as vectors; this simultaneously resolves the resolution and rasterized-text blockers.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandated, re-render at ≥600 DPI for 181 mm width (≥4280 px wide) and save as TIFF with LZW compression; expected size remains well under 10 MB.
  - Increase left padding / use constrained or tight layout so panel (b) and (c) y-axis labels are fully rendered and do not overlap the neighbouring panel; alternatively shorten the labels (e.g. "power beyond 2× HPHW", "peak beyond 1.5× HPHW (dB)") and define HPHW in the caption.
  - Set x-limits of (b) and (c) to the plotted data range (≈1–17) or share x-limits across all three panels with a common tick set, then reclaim the freed horizontal space for the axis labels.
  - Verify all text is 8–10 pt at final 181 mm width (currently ≈9 pt for tick numerals, ≈10 pt for axis/legend text — acceptable); do not scale the figure below double-column width.
  - Rewrite the caption opening as a plain sentence, e.g. "Variance, leakage, and side-lobe level versus measured resolution for N = 256. All curves are exact kernel and degrees-of-freedom computations (no sampling variability; M bandwidth settings per method, shown as markers)."
  - Confirm and preserve RGB color mode on export; do not allow CMYK conversion of the magenta/green/orange series.

### efigure4.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2097 × 2191 px — recommended **double** column (16-panel 4 × 4 grid; single column would render each panel ~20 mm wide) — **293 DPI** at 181 mm (7.16 in). For reference: 305 DPI at 6.875 in, 599 DPI at 88 mm single column, 645 DPI at 3.25 in.
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (curve plots, bar charts, dB kernels; no photographic content) — 293 DPI at the recommended double-column width vs. 300 DPI minimum and 600 DPI preferred. Rendered height at full width = 7.48 in ≈ 190 mm, within the 230 mm limit.
FORMAT:         **Needs conversion** — PNG → TIFF (LZW), EPS, or PDF for final submission (PNG is review-stage only).
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.45 MB (limit 10 MB); ample headroom for a vector or 600-DPI raster replacement.
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick labels, panel letters and in-panel legends are pixel-bound (PNG has no text layer); stroke edges will alias at print size.
COLOR MODE:     **Cannot verify / assume RGB** — `color_mode` absent from metadata. Palette (viridis curves, saturated teal/orange/blue bars, light-blue shading bands) is RGB-safe; the saturated teal in (o)/(k) and orange in (j)/(n) are the entries most at risk if any downstream CMYK conversion occurs. Keep RGB and confirm on export.
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Resolution 293 DPI at 181 mm fails the ≥300 DPI floor and is less than half the 600 DPI preferred for line art. Fine structure — the sinc sidelobe nulls in (m), the ripple ridges in (p), thin bar outlines in (i)–(l) — will smear.
  - [BLOCKING] Text is rasterized in a pure line-art figure. Journal quality review routinely rejects this.
  - [BLOCKING] Smallest type falls below the 6 pt absolute floor. In-panel legend entries ("f' = 0/N", "k = 0" … in (b), (d), (e)) measure ≈24 px ≈ 5.9 pt at 181 mm width; axis tick labels ≈30 px ≈ 7.4 pt, i.e. legal but at the bottom of the 8–10 pt band.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel-label collisions in the top row: the three-line column headings of (b), (c) and (d) overrun their axes and overlap the "(c)" and "(d)" letters ("bank: a sinc window at every offset" runs into "(c)"; "(K = 7 Slepians, equal weights)" reaches the right figure edge and abuts "(d)").
  - [ADVISORY] Per-IEEE style, the three-line descriptive column headings above row 1 function as in-figure titles and should be moved to the caption; keep only short axis/panel annotations inside the artwork.
  - [ADVISORY] Legends in (b) and (d) sit on top of the k = 0 / f' = 0 traces, obscuring them.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting incomplete. The estimator degrees of freedom are displayed (ν = 13.4, 11.1, 17.2, 14.0) — good — but the caption never defines ν, never gives the record length N used to generate the banks, and never states how many windows/tapers each ν derives from (7 Hann offsets, the Gaussian-smoother equivalent count, the sinc bank, K = 7 Slepians). No error bars are present, so no realization count is required for dispersion, but the ν provenance must be stated.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption does not identify the panel-letter scheme as lowercase-in-parentheses consistent with the artwork, and the leading "Fig.~\ref{fig:banks} in full." reads as a title rather than a descriptive caption opening.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export from the plotting source as **vector PDF or EPS** at exactly 181 mm width with fonts embedded (matplotlib: `fig.set_size_inches(7.13, 7.45); fig.savefig('efigure4.pdf')`, `rcParams['pdf.fonttype']=42`). This resolves the resolution and rasterized-text blockers simultaneously and will stay far under 10 MB.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandated, re-render at ≥600 DPI for 181 mm width, i.e. ≥4280 px wide (`dpi=600`), and save as TIFF with LZW compression.
  - Set fonts explicitly to a sans-serif face (Arial/Helvetica) with tick labels and axis labels at 8 pt and legend text at no less than 7 pt at final size; do not rely on `tight_layout` scaling to shrink them.
  - Reserve header space (`fig.subplots_adjust(top=…)`) or shorten the row-1 column headings to one line each (e.g. "Welch", "smoothed periodogram", "sliding sinc", "Thomson multitaper") and relocate the parenthetical parameter details to the caption; place panel letters (a)–(p) in a consistent corner outside the axes to remove the collisions.
  - Move the (b) and (d) legends outside the axes or to a single shared legend so no trace is occluded.
  - Extend the caption with the missing quantitative provenance, e.g.: "All banks computed for N = 256 samples. ν denotes the equivalent chi-square degrees of freedom of the corresponding spectral estimate, ν = 2(Σc_k)²/Σc_k²; it is obtained from the eigen-weight ladders in (i)–(l), comprising 7 Hann segments (a), the Gaussian-smoother equivalent bank (b), the full sliding-sinc bank (c) and K = 7 equally weighted Slepian tapers (d). No averaging over noise realizations is involved; all curves are deterministic window/kernel functions."
  - Confirm RGB on export and embed an sRGB profile; avoid any CMYK conversion step for the saturated teal and orange fills.

### figure1.png

---
**DIMENSIONS:**     2094 × 606 px (aspect ratio 3.46:1) — recommended **double column** — **292 DPI at 181 mm (7.16 in)**; 305 DPI at the 6.875 in text-block width; 598 DPI if forced to single-column 88 mm (3.5 in), but a three-panel strip at 88 mm would leave each panel ~28 mm wide and is not viable. Printed height at double column ≈ 52 mm — well within the 230 mm limit.

**RESOLUTION:**     **Below threshold** — line art (vector-style plots, no photographic content, no gradients). Effective 292 DPI at full 181 mm width, i.e. below the 300 DPI floor for color line art and far below the 600 DPI preference. To reach 600 DPI at 181 mm the raster would need to be ≈ 4290 px wide.

**FORMAT:**     **Needs conversion for final submission** — PNG is accepted for review only; convert to **vector PDF/EPS** (preferred, since this is line art) or 600 DPI TIFF (LZW).

**FILE SIZE:**     OK — 0.19 MB (limit 10 MB); ample headroom for a 600 DPI TIFF or vector PDF.

**TEXT RENDERING:**     **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick labels, legend entries and panel letters are baked into the bitmap. At 292 DPI the stroke edges of the thin math italics ($\lambda_k$, the minus sign in panel c) and the dotted 1/L guide will show pixel stepping in print.

**COLOR MODE:**     **OK for IEEE (RGB required), but potential shift if the publisher rasterizes to CMYK** — the saturated teal/green trace and the mid-pink/magenta dashed trace are both in the high-risk category; the pink in particular can desaturate toward a muddy rose, reducing its separation from the light-grey multitaper band in panel (a).

**Font size check (informational):** at 181 mm width, axis-label glyphs measure ≈ 8–9 pt cap-equivalent and tick labels ≈ 8 pt, so nothing falls below the 6 pt floor provided the figure is **not** scaled down from 181 mm. Do not reduce this figure below ~150 mm width or the legend text drops under 7 pt.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Resolution 292 DPI at the intended 181 mm double-column width is below the 300 DPI minimum for color line art and below the 600 DPI preference.
- [BLOCKING] Text and all plot strokes are rasterized in a line-art figure; this is a quality-blocking issue for IEEE TBME and will be visible on the dotted 1/L guide and italic subscripts.
- [BLOCKING] PNG is not an accepted final-submission format (review only).
- [ADVISORY] In panel (c) the y-axis label "max |average − multitaper| (dB)" runs into the top edge of the canvas and is visually clipped/crowded against the panel letter (c); increase the top margin or shorten the label.
- [ADVISORY] The 1/L guide line in (c) and the grey multitaper band in (a) are both light grey; at 292 DPI with a dotted stroke the guide may partially disappear in print and is also hard to distinguish from the multitaper band's grey in a reader's mind across panels.
- [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is incomplete for panels (b) and (c): the caption states one epoch and ν = 17 for the multitaper estimate, but does not state **how many window positions** were averaged in the sliding-window estimate (for L = 16N slid one sample at a time this count should be given explicitly), nor whether the points in (c) are single realizations. If they are single realizations, say so and state that no error bars are shown; if they are averages, give the number of realizations and the dof of each estimator being compared.
- [ADVISORY] Because (b) compares noise/agreement floors between two estimators, the degrees of freedom of the smoothed-periodogram and sliding-window estimators (not only the multitaper ν = 17) should be stated so the reader can judge whether the comparison is at matched resolution/dof.
- [ADVISORY] Large horizontal white gutters between panels (a)/(b) and (b)/(c) waste roughly 10–12 % of the double-column width; panels could be enlarged at no cost in page area.
- [ADVISORY] Panel letters are bold "(a)", "(b)", "(c)" — lowercase and parenthesized as required — but verify they are not converted to uppercase by any downstream style file.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Re-export directly from the plotting library as **vector PDF or EPS** at a physical canvas of 181 × 52 mm with fonts embedded (Type 1/TrueType, no Type 3). This resolves the resolution, rasterized-text, and format issues in one step.
- If a raster is mandated, re-render at ≥ 4290 × 1241 px (600 DPI at 181 mm) and save as TIFF with LZW compression in RGB; do not upsample the existing 2094 px file.
- Keep the figure at full double-column width; do not allow the typesetter to scale it below ~150 mm, which would push legend text under 7 pt.
- Add ~4 mm of top padding to panel (c) (or move the y-label closer to the axis) so the label no longer touches the canvas edge, and align the three panel letters on a common baseline.
- Darken the 1/L guide (e.g. 40–50 % grey) and increase its dash period, or change it to a thin solid line with a distinct hue, so it survives print at 300 DPI and is not confused with the grey multitaper band in (a).
- Distinguish the teal and pink traces with line style as well as hue (already partly done via the dashed purple in (a)) and verify legibility after a soft-proof to CMYK in case the publisher converts; consider a slightly darker magenta to preserve contrast against the grey band.
- Reduce inter-panel gutters and redistribute the recovered width to the three axes, keeping the legends directly beneath their own panels.
- Extend the caption with: the number of window positions averaged for the sliding-window estimate at each L, the dof (or equivalent smoothing bandwidth) of the smoothed periodogram and sliding-window estimators alongside ν = 17, and an explicit statement that panel (c) shows single-realization maxima with no error bars (or the realization count if averaged).

### figure2.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2115 x 1140 px (aspect 1.86:1) — recommended **double column** — 295 DPI at 181 mm (7.16 in); 308 DPI at the 6.875 in text width; 604 DPI if forced to 88 mm single column
RESOLUTION:     **Below threshold** — line art (multi-panel line plots, shaded regions, text annotations); 295 DPI at full double-column width vs. 300 DPI minimum and 600 DPI preferred for line art. Printed height at 181 mm would be 97.6 mm — well inside the 230 mm limit, so height is not a constraint.
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG (accepted for review only) → PDF or EPS (vector) preferred; TIFF at ≥600 DPI acceptable as a raster fallback
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.22 MB (limit 10 MB); a vector PDF/EPS of this figure will also be well under the limit
TEXT RENDERING: **Rasterized** — all axis labels, tick labels, panel titles and legends are baked into the PNG bitmap; at 295 DPI stroke and glyph edges will show pixel stepping in print
COLOR MODE:     OK for submission (RGB) — advisory: the saturated orange (~#E69F00) traces and the mid-blue kernel-width bars/shading are the two hues most likely to shift (orange toward muddy brown, blue toward purple) if the publisher converts to CMYK
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Resolution: 295 DPI at 181 mm fails the 300 DPI floor and is less than half the 600 DPI preferred for line art. Thin grey periodogram traces in (a) and the 1-px kernel sidelobe structure in (e)–(f) are exactly the content that degrades first.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. Regeneration as vector (PDF/EPS) from the plotting script is required; upscaling the PNG will not recover glyph edges.
  - [ADVISORY] Format: PNG is review-only for IEEE TBME; a vector or ≥600 DPI TIFF must be supplied for production.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel titles are set inside the figure ("no taper: leaky and noisy", "Hann taper: still noisy", "kernel: 1.3% beyond 2W", etc.). TBME asks for no titles inside the artwork; this interpretive text belongs in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Label collision: in (g) the panel label "(g)" and the title "kernel: < 0.001% beyond 2W" overlap. In (a) the legend entry "estimate (one realization)" runs to/past the axes edge and its marker swatch collides with the text.
  - [ADVISORY] Small type: tick and axis labels measure ~8 pt at 181 mm, which is acceptable, but the in-axes legend entries in (a) and (e) scale to roughly 6.5–7 pt. Anything trimmed further (e.g., if the figure is placed at less than full column width) breaks the 6 pt floor.
  - [ADVISORY] Axis inconsistency: panels (a) and (b) end near f = 0.45–0.5 while (c) and (d) extend slightly past 0.5, so the four spectra are not on identical x-scales despite being presented as a direct comparison. Kernel panels (e)–(h) share limits correctly.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting is largely satisfied — the caption states n = 1 realization with N = 1024, and equivalent degrees of freedom ν are printed in each of (a)–(d). However, ν is the only noise-level descriptor given and it is stated only for the top row; state explicitly in the caption that no averaging over realizations was performed (so no error bars are shown) and, since noise levels are being compared across estimators, give the basis of the ν values (e.g., ν = 2 for the raw/tapered periodogram, ν from the smoothing bandwidth and taper for (c)–(d)).
  - [ADVISORY] Grey is used both for the periodogram trace in (a) and for the "true spectrum (shape only)" fill in (e)–(h); in grayscale printing the orange traces will also map to grey, reducing the distinction between estimator and reference curves.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting script as vector PDF or EPS sized to 181 mm x ~98 mm, with fonts embedded (matplotlib: `savefig('figure2.pdf', bbox_inches='tight')` with `rcParams['pdf.fonttype']=42`). This clears both BLOCKING items at once.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandated, re-render at ≥600 DPI for 181 mm width (≥4275 x 2304 px) as RGB TIFF with LZW compression — do not upsample the existing PNG.
  - Move all eight in-figure panel titles into the caption, keyed to the existing lowercase (a)–(h) labels, and keep only the ν values (or move those to the caption as well) inside the axes.
  - Nudge the (g) panel label left/up or shorten the kernel-leakage annotations to eliminate the label/title overlap; place the (a) and (e) legends outside the data area or in an unused corner so the swatches and text do not clip.
  - Set identical x-limits (0 to 0.5) on (a)–(d) so the four estimates are strictly comparable.
  - Raise all in-axes legend and annotation text to ≥8 pt at final size, and confirm a sans-serif family (Arial/Helvetica) is used throughout.
  - Add to the caption: the number of realizations (one) and the statement that ν is the equivalent degrees of freedom of each estimator, with the value for the periodogram (ν = 2) identified as the reference, so the noise-level comparison across panels is fully specified.
  - If the journal will convert to CMYK, soft-proof the orange and blue in a CMYK profile, or shift to a CMYK-safe palette (e.g., darker orange, deeper blue) to preserve the contrast between kernel curves and the blue |f| ≤ W shading.

### figure3.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2115 x 626 px — recommended **double column** — 308 DPI at 6.875 in (297 DPI if placed at the full 181 mm / 7.16 in trim width); 651 DPI if forced to single-column 3.25 in
RESOLUTION:     Below threshold — line art (two vector-style plots, no photographic content); effective 297–308 DPI at the recommended width vs. the 600 DPI preferred for line art
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG (review only) → TIFF (LZW), EPS, or PDF for final submission; vector PDF/EPS strongly preferred for this figure type
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.26 MB (limit 10 MB; ample headroom for a 600 DPI TIFF or vector export)
TEXT RENDERING: Rasterized — all labels are pixel-baked into the PNG; strokes and glyph edges will show stair-stepping at print zoom
COLOR MODE:     OK (assumed RGB for PNG; metadata color_mode not supplied — confirm RGB) — the saturated orange (bias curve, tapered record, circled annotations) and the mid blue (noise curve) are both at risk of visible desaturation if the production house converts to CMYK
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Raster line art at ~300 DPI, below the 600 DPI line-art expectation. The 3:1 panel aspect makes the figure double-column only; at 181 mm the DPI drops to 297, i.e. marginally under even the 300 DPI colour-line-art floor.
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text and rasterized curve strokes in a graph/diagram figure. Thin grey record trace and the 1-px dotted vertical guides will alias badly in print.
  - [ADVISORY] In-figure panel titles ("the record: N = 1024 samples of the AR(4) process" and "Hann taper, then box: error vs box width (300 realizations)") violate the no-titles-inside-the-figure rule. Move both to the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Tick-label and legend text measures roughly 8 pt at the recommended double-column width — inside spec, but with no margin. Any downscaling (e.g. to single column) drops text to ~4 pt, below the 6 pt floor.
  - [ADVISORY] Degrees-of-freedom reporting is partially present: ν = 2, 9, 50 are annotated on panel (b), and the 300-realization count is stated, but ν is never defined in the caption and the noise/bias comparison in panel (b) does not say how ν relates to W (e.g. ν ≈ 4WN for a box of half-width W applied to a Hann-tapered periodogram). Since the blue curve is explicitly a noise level being compared across W, the estimator's degrees of freedom should be stated in the caption, not only as inline annotations.
  - [ADVISORY] The caption does not state over what the RMS is taken (frequency bins only, or frequency and realizations jointly), nor the number of frequency bins entering the whole-band and peak-region scores. "No error bars are shown because the curves are ensemble averages" is not sufficient — the reader still needs the spread or the standard error of the 300-realization mean to judge whether the broad minimum is resolvable.
  - [ADVISORY] Two legends sit outside the axes, consuming ~25 % of figure height. Fine for width but it inflates the overall height; consider in-axes legends to recover space.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting library as vector PDF or EPS at exactly 181 mm width (7.126 in) x ~52 mm height. This removes the resolution and rasterized-text blockers in one step.
  - If a raster deliverable is required, re-render at 600 DPI for 181 mm, i.e. 4276 x 1266 px, as RGB TIFF with LZW compression (expect ~2–4 MB, well within limit).
  - Delete both in-figure panel titles; fold their content into the caption, e.g. "(a) the record of Fig. X …; (b) RMS error of the Hann-tapered, box-smoothed estimate versus box half-width, 300 independent realizations."
  - Set all text to a single sans-serif face (Arial/Helvetica) at 9–10 pt for axis labels and 8–9 pt for tick and legend text at final size, so there is margin above the 6 pt floor.
  - Increase the grey record trace to ≥0.5 pt and the dotted guides to ≥0.5 pt so they survive print reproduction; consider a slightly darker grey for the record trace.
  - Add to the caption: the number of frequency bins in each RMS average, the definition of ν and its relation to W, and either the inter-realization standard deviation of the RMS or the standard error of the 300-realization mean (e.g. "shaded band / ±1 SE, n = 300 realizations").
  - Confirm RGB export and, if the journal converts to CMYK, pre-check the orange (#D95F02-like) and blue (#1F78B4-like) curves in a CMYK soft proof; if they shift, add marker-shape or dash-pattern redundancy so the four curves remain distinguishable without colour.

### figure4.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2107 × 1188 px — recommended **double column** — ≈294 DPI at 181 mm (7.16 in); ≈306 DPI at the 6.875 in text-block width; ≈602 DPI if forced to single column (88 mm)
RESOLUTION:     Below threshold — line art (multi-panel vector-style plots, no photographic content); 294 DPI at full double-column width vs. 300 DPI minimum / 600 DPI preferred for colored line art
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG (acceptable for review only) → PDF or EPS (vector) for final submission; TIFF only if a raster route is unavoidable
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.32 MB (limit 10 MB); ample headroom to supply a 600-DPI raster or vector file
TEXT RENDERING: Rasterized — all axis labels, tick labels, titles and legends are pixel-based (PNG); edges will visibly soften at print size, especially the small math glyphs ($\lambda_k$, $|U_k(f)|^2$, subscripts)
COLOR MODE:     OK for RGB submission — but note the viridis ramp: the mid-teal/green traces (k = 4–6) and the yellow-green (k = 7) are the most at-risk hues if the publisher converts to CMYK; the light-green "first 7 tapers" vs. yellow-green "first 9 tapers" pair in (c) is already low-contrast and will worsen
ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution at 181 mm is ~294 DPI, below the 300 DPI floor and well below the 600 DPI preference for colored line art. Rasterized line art at this density will show stair-stepping on the thin curves and the dotted/dashed reference lines in (d).
  - [BLOCKING] Text and math are rasterized. For a graph-type figure this is a quality-blocking issue; IEEE expects vector text in EPS/PDF submissions.
  - [BLOCKING] Legend/axis-label collisions in panels (a) and (b): the legend block overprints the x-axis labels "sample t" and "frequency offset (units of 1/N)", making both partially unreadable. In (b) the y-axis label "(unit-norm tapers)" also collides with the panel title text.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel titles are placed inside the figure ("Slepian tapers, offset for display", "spectral windows …", "running sum …", "energy outside the band, 1−λ_k"). IEEE style asks for no titles inside the artwork; this information belongs in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Large unused vertical gap between the top and bottom rows (~150–200 px band) plus wide margins. This inflates the canvas and lowers effective DPI for the plotted content.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical/estimator reporting: the caption does not state that the panels are deterministic (analytic DPSS/spectral-window computations, no random realizations) nor the estimator degrees of freedom implied by K. Since panel (c)/(d) motivate the choice K = 2NW−1 = 7, the caption should state the corresponding multitaper dof (2K = 14) so readers comparing noise levels have the estimator's dof explicitly.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (c) legend: "first 7 tapers" (light green) and "first 9 tapers" (yellow-green) are adjacent viridis samples and are hard to separate in print or for color-deficient readers; the dashed black "ideal box" and solid black "all N tapers" are also distinguished only by dash pattern at similar line weight.
  - [ADVISORY] Axis tick labels measure ~7–7.5 pt at 181 mm — inside the ≥6 pt hard limit but below the 8–10 pt preferred band.
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (d) log y-axis spans 10⁻¹⁶ to 10⁻¹, i.e. values at machine-precision level for k = 0–1; these points are numerically meaningless and may invite reviewer objection unless the caption notes the floor is double-precision limited.
SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting library as vector PDF or EPS sized to 181 mm width (e.g. matplotlib `figsize=(7.16, 4.0)`, `savefig(..., format='pdf')`). This resolves both the resolution and rasterized-text issues at once.
  - If a raster file is required, re-render at ≥600 DPI for 181 mm width, i.e. ≥4290 px wide (600 DPI TIFF, LZW compression); the 0.32 MB current file leaves plenty of size budget.
  - Fix the legend collisions: move the (a) and (b) legends outside the axes (e.g. `bbox_to_anchor` to the right of each axis or below the x-label with added `subplots_adjust`), or place them inside the axes in empty regions (upper-left of (a), upper-right corners of (b)).
  - In (b), shorten the y-axis label to "$|U_k(f)|^2$" and move "unit-norm tapers" into the caption to clear the overlap with the title area.
  - Delete the four in-figure panel titles and fold their content into the caption, e.g. "(a) first six Slepian tapers, offset vertically; (b) spectral windows |U_k(f)|² for unit-norm tapers; (c) running sum …; (d) out-of-band energy 1−λ_k."
  - Tighten layout (`constrained_layout=True` or reduce `hspace`) to remove the dead band between rows; the reclaimed area lets you enlarge fonts to 8–9 pt at final size without changing overall dimensions.
  - Increase color separation in (c): keep viridis endpoints only (e.g. dark purple, blue, teal, green) and skip the near-yellow sample, or add distinct dash patterns/markers to the "first 7" and "first 9" traces; increase the "all N tapers" line weight relative to the dashed ideal box.
  - Add to the caption: the number of realizations (state "deterministic; computed analytically from the DPSS eigenbasis, no averaging over realizations"), and the estimator dof associated with K = 7 (2K = 14) to satisfy the noise-comparison reporting requirement.
  - Optionally clip panel (d) y-axis at ~10⁻¹² and note in the caption that lower λ-deficits are at double-precision roundoff.
  - Confirm the export is RGB (not CMYK or indexed-palette PNG); if the publisher converts to CMYK, request a proof check on the teal/green traces in (b) and (c).

### figure5.png

---
DIMENSIONS:     2090 × 1141 px — recommended **double column** — 304 DPI at 6.875 in (181 mm). (At single column 3.25 in it would be 643 DPI, but a 4 × 2 panel grid with 8 sets of axis labels cannot be read at 88 mm.)
RESOLUTION:     Marginally adequate — figure type: **line art (color)**; 304 DPI at double-column width meets the 300 DPI floor but is well below the 600 DPI preferred for line art. Aspect ratio 1.83:1 → 181 × 99 mm printed, comfortably under the 230 mm height cap.
FORMAT:         Needs conversion — PNG (review-acceptable only) → **EPS or PDF (vector)** preferred; TIFF at ≥600 DPI acceptable as a raster fallback.
FILE SIZE:      OK — 0.24 MB (limit 10 MB); a vector PDF/EPS of this content will also be well under the limit.
TEXT RENDERING: Rasterized — all labels, tick numbers, legends and panel titles are pixel-baked from the PNG raster.
COLOR MODE:     OK (assumed RGB for PNG; metadata field not supplied) — journal requires RGB, so no conversion needed. Note that the viridis teal/green line colors and the saturated orange/teal bars in (e–h) are the hues most prone to visible shift if a CMYK proof is ever generated.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Rasterized text in a line-art figure. All axis labels, tick labels, legend entries and panel titles are raster at ~304 DPI effective; glyph edges will be soft in print. Regenerate as vector.
  - [BLOCKING] Panel-label/title collisions. The "(c)" label overprints the word "bank:" in the third column title, and "(d)" overprints "bank: Slepian tapers k = 0..3" in the fourth. Characters are obscured — not acceptable at print size.
  - [BLOCKING] Legend/data collisions. In (b) the legend box sits on top of the f′ = 0/N and f′ = 2/N curves; in (c) the legend overlaps the top two traces; in (d) the "k = 1" entry sits directly on the k = 0 curve. At 181 mm these overlaps are unresolvable.
  - [ADVISORY] Titles inside the figure. IEEE TBME asks for no titles in the artwork. Each of the eight panels carries a 2–3 line title ("average segments (Welch: Hann, L = N/4, 50%)", "eigen-weight ladder: ν = 13.4", …). This material belongs in the caption.
  - [ADVISORY] Resolution only 304 DPI at the recommended width — 1.4% above the hard floor, with no margin if the figure is scaled or re-exported.
  - [ADVISORY] Smallest text (tick labels, "sample t", "taper index k") measures ≈7 pt at 181 mm — above the 6 pt minimum but below the 8–10 pt target.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting. ν is quoted to one decimal in each of (e–h) but the caption does not state that these are *equivalent/effective degrees of freedom* of the spectral estimator, nor that panels (a–h) are deterministic constructions (no Monte-Carlo realizations, hence no error bars). For (a) the number of averaged windows (7 segments) is implied by the text but should be stated numerically alongside ν; similarly the number of tapers K entering each ladder (7, 12, 10, 7 as drawn) should be given so the reader can verify ν = 2(Σc_k)²/Σc_k².
  - [ADVISORY] The y-axis label "amplitude (curves offset)" appears only on panel (a) and "weight c_k (normalized)" only on (e); shared axes are fine, but the caption should state that the vertical offsets in (a–d) are arbitrary and that curves are not to a common zero.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption opens with a bold sentence functioning as a title; convert to a plain descriptive lead-in if the journal's style checker rejects bold run-in heads.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting source as vector PDF or EPS sized to 181 mm width (e.g. figsize = (7.13, 3.89) in), with fonts embedded as Type 1/TrueType and no text converted to outlines. This removes both the rasterized-text and the 304 DPI issues at once. If a raster deliverable is mandated, export TIFF (LZW) at 600 DPI → 4275 × 2334 px, ~1–3 MB.
  - Set all text to a sans-serif face (Arial/Helvetica) at 8 pt minimum for tick labels and axis titles at final size.
  - Move panel labels (a)–(d) outside the axes (upper-left, above the frame, with reserved margin) so they cannot collide with column headings; keep them lowercase in parentheses as now.
  - Delete the in-figure panel titles and fold their content into the caption, e.g. "(a) Welch, Hann segments L = N/4 at 50% overlap, 7 segments, ν = 13.4; (b) Hann periodogram smoothed by a Gaussian, σ = 3/N, ν = 11.1; …". This frees vertical space and resolves the (c)/(d) overprints.
  - Move the legends in (b), (c), (d) out of the plot box — a single shared horizontal legend beneath the top row, or direct in-line curve annotations at the right edge of each trace with the y-limits expanded ~10% to create clear space.
  - Add to the caption: the number of tapers/windows K contributing to each ladder, an explicit statement that ν is the equivalent degrees of freedom of the estimator, and a note that all curves are deterministic taper/weight sequences so no error bars apply.
  - Confirm RGB on export and avoid any CMYK proofing step; if a print proof is required, check the teal (≈#1F9E89) and orange (≈#D45500) elements for saturation loss.

### figure6.png

---
**DIMENSIONS:** 2094 × 1140 px (aspect 1.84:1) — recommended **double column** — 2094/7.16 in = **293 DPI** at 181 mm (304 DPI if the narrower 6.875 in text block is used). At single column (88 mm) the same file would be 598 DPI, but an 8-panel 4 × 2 grid compressed to 88 mm would render axis text at ~3 pt and is not viable.

**RESOLUTION:** Below threshold — **line art** (vector-style plots, no photographic content or continuous gradients), actual **293 DPI** at the recommended double-column width. This misses the 300 DPI floor and is far below the 600 DPI preferred for line art.

**FORMAT:** Needs conversion — **PNG → PDF or EPS** (vector) for final submission; PNG is acceptable only for the review upload.

**FILE SIZE:** OK — 0.22 MB, well under the 10 MB limit.

**TEXT RENDERING:** **Rasterized** — all labels are baked into the PNG raster; at 293 DPI the tick-label strokes and the thin dotted grid of the multitaper trace (panel f) will show pixel edges in print.

**COLOR MODE:** RGB assumed (metadata does not state it — confirm). RGB is correct for IEEE. If the production house converts to CMYK, the saturated teal (panel a/e) and the mid-blue (b/f) are the highest-risk hues; the pale blue "|f| ≤ W" band over the light grey "true spectrum" fill is already low-contrast and will lose separation further.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Resolution 293 DPI at 181 mm is below the 300 DPI minimum and far below the 600 DPI line-art preference. Re-export, do not upsample.
- [BLOCKING] Rasterized text and curves in a line-art figure. The dense side-lobe structure in panel (f) (several hundred lobes across ~470 px of axis) is at or beyond the sampling limit of the raster and will alias; only a vector export preserves it.
- [ADVISORY] In-figure panel titles ("untapered, then box W = 4/N", "kernel: 1.4 % beyond 2W", etc.) conflict with the "no titles inside the figure" rule. They are informative and IEEE generally tolerates them as short in-panel annotations, but the safest reading is to demote them to small in-axes annotations or fold the method names into the caption.
- [ADVISORY] Text size at final width: measured glyph heights of ~25–30 px correspond to roughly **6–7.5 pt** at 181 mm. The y-tick numerals and the three-entry legend in panel (e) sit at the 6 pt floor. Any further reduction is non-compliant.
- [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting incomplete. The caption gives ν per panel (17.2, 14.0, 8.9, 17.6), which satisfies the degrees-of-freedom requirement, but it does not say how ν is defined (equivalent χ² DOF of the smoothed estimate?) nor how it was computed (kernel ‖·‖ ratio vs. empirical). The four median-absolute-error values (11.6, 4.6, 1.3, 0.8 dB) are quoted from **one realization** — state the number of frequency bins in the band over which each median is taken, and ideally replace with a median over M independent realizations with M stated.
- [ADVISORY] No error bars or distribution shading appear anywhere, yet the figure's purpose is variance/bias trade-off. A reader may mistake the single-realization scatter for an estimator property. Consider stating explicitly in the caption that no confidence intervals are drawn.
- [ADVISORY] Panel (c) x-axis shows the estimate terminating near f = 0.47 and panel (b) near 0.46 while the true PSD extends to 0.5 — verify this is intentional (band edge from smoothing) rather than a clipped series, and note it in the caption if intentional.
- [ADVISORY] Grayscale fallback: the four coloured traces (teal, blue, orange, pink) have near-identical lightness and identical line weights; they become indistinguishable if printed monochrome. Panels are separated spatially, so this is low-risk, but the legend in (e) relies on colour alone.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
1. Re-export from the plotting source as **PDF (vector)** sized exactly to 181 mm width, e.g. `fig.set_size_inches(7.16, 3.90)` then `savefig('figure6.pdf')`. Keep the aspect ratio; 1140/2094 × 181 mm = 98.5 mm height, well inside the 230 mm limit.
2. If a raster is required, export at **600 DPI for 181 mm** = 4296 × 2339 px as TIFF with LZW compression. Regenerate at that size — do not resample the existing 2094 px file.
3. Set all fonts in the source to a sans-serif family (Arial/Helvetica) at **8 pt for tick labels and axis labels, 9 pt for panel titles and (a)–(h) labels**, specified at the final 7.16 in width, then verify no glyph falls below 6 pt.
4. Embed all fonts in the PDF/EPS (`pdf.fonttype: 42` / `ps.fonttype: 42` in Matplotlib) so no text is converted to outlines or bitmaps.
5. For panel (f), either plot the multitaper kernel with a thinner line (0.5 pt) plus an envelope, or decimate/resample the kernel evaluation grid so the side-lobe comb is representable at print resolution instead of forming a solid band.
6. Increase the contrast of the "|f| ≤ W" band — use a hatched or outlined region rather than pale blue over light grey — so it survives both CMYK conversion and grayscale printing.
7. Extend the caption with: the definition and computation method for ν; the number of frequency bins entering each median absolute error; an explicit statement that curves are single realizations with no confidence intervals; and the Slepian/Hann normalization convention used for the kernels in (e)–(h).
8. Confirm and declare **RGB** color mode in the submission metadata.

COLOR MODE:     OK for submission (RGB required) — but note the orange (~#E8A33D) and magenta/pink (~#D96BA0) traces are the two most at-risk hues if the publisher converts to CMYK; the pink will desaturate toward a duller rose and may become harder to separate from the orange in panel (b), where those two are the only curves.

ISSUES:
  - [BLOCKING] Effective resolution 295 DPI at the recommended 181 mm double-column width is below the 300 DPI floor and far below the 600 DPI preference for line art. The figure cannot be submitted as-is at full width.
  - [BLOCKING] Text and lines are rasterized. For a graph-type figure this is a quality defect independent of DPI; thin 1-px strokes in panel (b)'s spiky difference traces will alias badly.
  - [BLOCKING] Format: PNG is not an accepted final-submission format for IEEE TBME.
  - [ADVISORY] Extreme aspect ratio (3.63:1). At 181 mm wide the panels are only ≈50 mm tall, so the 10–20 dB features in panel (a) and the ±5 dB structure in panel (b) are vertically compressed. Consider 2.6:1–3:1, or stack (a) over (b).
  - [ADVISORY] Panel (b) y-axis label "difference from multitaper (dB)" runs into the top edge of the canvas and visually collides with the "(b)" panel label; the closing parenthesis appears clipped.
  - [ADVISORY] Data clipping: the orange trace in panel (a) and both traces in panel (b) run off the bottom axis near 0.7–0.9 Hz. Either extend the y-limits or state in the caption that the sub-1 Hz excursion is truncated.
  - [ADVISORY] Caption/figure color mismatch: the third estimate is described as "purple" but renders as pink/magenta. Panel (b) legend entries also rely on color alone to distinguish two similar-luminance hues.
  - [ADVISORY] Statistical reporting incomplete. The degrees of freedom are stated well (ν = 14.0, 8.9, 17.6) and the Welch window count (9 segments) is given, but the caption does not state **how many frequency bins** the median |diff| and 95th-percentile values were computed over, nor that the figure is a **single** EEG epoch (n = 1 realization) rather than an ensemble. Error-bar-free difference curves still need the underlying count.
  - [ADVISORY] Legends are placed below each panel in a 3-line / 2-line block; once the figure is scaled to 181 mm this legend text sits at the small end of the acceptable range (estimated ≈8–9 pt at 295 DPI). Re-verify ≥6 pt, ideally 8 pt, after rescaling.

SUGGESTED FIXES:
  - Re-export directly from the plotting script as **vector PDF or EPS** sized to 181 mm × ≈60–70 mm (`figsize=(7.13, 2.4)` in matplotlib, `savefig(..., format='pdf')`). This eliminates the DPI, format and rasterized-text issues in one step.
  - If a raster deliverable is mandatory, render at **600 DPI for a 7.13 in width = 4278 px wide** (`savefig(..., dpi=600)`), save as TIFF with LZW compression, and keep color mode RGB.
  - Embed fonts (Arial or Helvetica) and set tick labels/axis labels to 8 pt and legend text to 8 pt **at final print size**; avoid post-hoc scaling of a figure drawn at a different size.
  - Add top padding or use `constrained_layout` so the panel (b) y-label no longer touches the canvas edge or the "(b)" label; move panel labels slightly left/down into the axes corner.
  - Extend panel (a) and (b) y-limits (or add a broken-axis marker) so the <1 Hz excursions are fully visible, or note the truncation in the caption.
  - Change "purple" to "pink" (or recolor that trace to a true purple) and add line-style differentiation (e.g., solid / dashed / dotted) so the three estimates remain separable in grayscale and after any CMYK conversion.
  - Append to the caption: the number of frequency bins over which the medians and 95th percentiles were evaluated, the analysis bandwidth/epoch length, and an explicit statement that this is one epoch (n = 1), e.g. "medians over the N = ___ bins from 0.5–40 Hz of a single 13.5 s epoch."
  - Keep the bold lead-in sentence in the caption only (it is correctly outside the image); confirm no title text is added inside the axes at final export.
