**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — No pairwise significance annotations. (If any quantitative claim about estimator differences is made in the text from the (h) distribution, note that a p-value computed over ~10⁵ correlated pixels would be invalid; see SAMPLE SIZE.)

**TIME SERIES:** Concern — (a) appears continuous, but there is a conspicuous near-flat segment at ≈ 365–400 s and a corresponding abrupt dark band in (c,e,g). If this is a dropout, disconnection, or clipped/interpolated artefact rather than genuine post-ictal suppression, the line in (a) interpolates across a gap and the spectrograms are computed on non-data. This must be stated. Also, the spectrogram x-axes (starting ≈ 0–1 s, ending ≈ 590 s) are offset from (a) by the window half-width; the panels are stacked as if co-registered, so the edge truncation should be made explicit or the axes aligned exactly.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel (h) x-axis label is cut off by the figure bounding box: "multitaper minus Hann-then-box (d" — the unit is unreadable. Rendering/layout error.
- [BLOCKING] Panel (h) title reads "median |diff| 1.5 dB" (median *absolute* difference) while the histogram plots the *signed* difference. The summary statistic shown does not correspond to the quantity on the axis; a reader will misread 1.5 dB as the distribution's centre, which is near 0.
- [BLOCKING] Colour scale saturation at 45 dB blanks out the ictal interval in all three spectrograms, i.e. the comparison the figure exists to make is censored in its most important region. Either extend the upper limit, add over-range colour ("extend=max" arrow on the colorbar), or add an explicit difference panel.
- [BLOCKING] n is not recoverable from the figure: number of subjects/seizures (apparently 1), number of pixels in (h), and the degree to which those pixels are non-independent (50 % window overlap, correlated frequency bins) are all unstated. The histogram's y-axis ("pixels", counts to ~5000) conveys a precision that the effective sample size does not support.
- [ADVISORY] Amplitude clipping in (a) versus unclipped (b) gives inconsistent amplitude impressions; the clip is not marked on the plot.
- [ADVISORY] The ≈365–400 s flat/blank interval is unexplained; possible dropout interpolated across in (a) and propagated into (c,e,g).
- [ADVISORY] Different y-ranges in (d) and (f) prevent cross-instant comparison of estimator spread.
- [ADVISORY] The seizure shading present in (a) is not replicated in (c,e,g) (only two dashed instant markers), so the ictal interval must be transferred by eye across panels.
- [ADVISORY] Line occlusion in (d,f); the legend appears only in (f), leaving (d) unlabelled.
- [ADVISORY] Panels (c,e,g) have no x-axis label except on (g), and tick labels start at 100 s while the axis begins near 0 — minor, but the first tick's absence makes the offset from (a) hard to see.
- [ADVISORY] Single-record, single-channel demonstration; the channel was *selected* as the one with the largest ictal 2–20 Hz rise (stated in caption). This is a selection on the outcome of interest and should be flagged as such in the figure, since it bounds any general claim about estimator behaviour.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Repair the layout so the (h) x-label renders in full, with units: "multitaper − Hann-then-box (dB)".
- Change the (h) annotation to match the plotted quantity: report median signed difference and IQR (e.g. "median −0.1 dB, IQR [−1.8, +1.6]"), draw a vertical line at 0 and at the median, and report the median |diff| = 1.5 dB separately if desired. State the pixel count and the effective (independent) count in the panel or caption.
- Add a fourth spectrogram panel showing the signed difference (e) − (g) on a diverging colormap centred at 0, with a symmetric scale; this replaces eyeball comparison of three near-identical colour fields and shows *where* the estimators differ.
- Raise the spectrogram colour limit above the ictal maximum, or mark over-range values explicitly (pointed colorbar end / hatch), and say in the caption what fraction of pixels saturate.
- Annotate the clip in (a) on the axis itself (e.g. "clipped at ±500 µV", or draw dotted lines at ±500 and show the unclipped trace in a smaller inset); alternatively plot (a) unclipped with a broken axis so (b) is consistent with it.
- Replicate the seizure shading (and the (b) excerpt window) on (c), (e) and (g); align the spectrogram x-axes exactly with (a), or mark the window-edge truncation.
- Identify the 365–400 s interval: label it as post-ictal suppression, or, if it is a dropout/artefact, break the line in (a) rather than interpolating and mask the corresponding spectrogram columns.
- Use a common y-range for (d) and (f), or add a shared secondary panel showing each estimator's deviation from a common reference so the variance reduction is directly comparable across the two instants.
- Repeat the legend (or use direct line labels) in (d); reduce the multitaper line width or add transparency so the Welch and Hann-then-box traces remain visible; put the grey periodogram on top with low alpha, or offset the traces vertically with a stated offset.
- State in the figure (not only the caption) that this is one channel from one seizure in one subject, chosen for maximal ictal 2–20 Hz rise, and — if the paper claims general estimator behaviour — add a companion panel summarising the (e)−(g) difference distribution across multiple records/channels.

### figure9.png

---
**PLOT TYPE:** Appropriate — This is a signal-processing exemplar figure (single-channel time trace + three spectrogram estimators), not a group-comparison figure. Bar/box/violin considerations do not apply. The viridis colour map is perceptually uniform and colour-vision-safe, and using one shared colour scale across (b–d) is the correct choice for an estimator comparison. Two rendering concerns rather than plot-type concerns: (i) the shared colourbar spans 10–45 dB and panels (c) and (d) appear saturated at the yellow ceiling over broad regions (low frequencies, ictal onsets), so the reader cannot see how much dynamic range is being clipped; (ii) the seizure shading appears only in (a) and is not carried into (b–d), making the visual claim about ictal spectral content harder to verify.

**SAMPLE SIZE:** Not visible (in the statistical sense) — n = 1 ten-minute clip, 1 channel, 1 subject. That is legitimate for a methods illustration, but the figure/caption should say explicitly that this is a single illustrative record. The estimator parameters (window length, step, ν, NW, K, W) are commendably reported in the panel titles, and 599 windows is stated in the caption; the effective degrees of freedom ν is the relevant "n" here and it is visible. Individual-point display is not applicable.

**COMPARISON:** Concern — The three estimators are computed from *the same* data record, i.e. this is a paired/within-record comparison, but it is displayed as three independent images. The caption's central claim ("the two smoothed estimators render it identically") is a paired statement that cannot be verified from three separately rendered heatmaps; small but systematic differences (multitaper vs. box-smoothed leakage, edge behaviour near 0 Hz) are invisible at this scale. A difference panel (c − d in dB, diverging map centred at zero) or a paired spectral slice at one or two representative times would make the paired comparison explicit.

**AXIS TRUNCATION:** Present (disclosed, but not annotated) — (a) is clipped at ±500 µV and the y-axis ends exactly at the clip level, so clipped samples become flat-topped artefacts that are indistinguishable from true signal maxima; nothing in the panel marks where clipping occurred. The colour scale is also truncated at both ends (10 and 45 dB) with no "extend" arrows. Frequency axes are truncated at 30 Hz, which is reasonable but should be stated as a display range rather than the analysis range (aliasing/leakage from >30 Hz content is relevant to the leakage argument being made). None of these truncations exaggerate a group difference, so this is not the classic misleading-bar-chart case, but they should be annotated.

**OVERPLOTTING:** Present — (a) renders ~600 s of EEG (presumably ≥256 Hz sampling, i.e. >150 000 samples) into roughly 1750 horizontal pixels. The trace is an ink-saturated black band whose apparent amplitude depends entirely on the decimation/rasterisation path, and thin high-amplitude transients may be either invented or lost. Report the sampling rate and whether the trace was decimated, and prefer an explicit per-pixel min/max envelope. In (b–d) the analogous risk is pixel-level: 61 frequency bins (0.5 Hz for a 2-s window) and 599 time steps are mapped onto a much larger pixel grid, so any bilinear image interpolation would smooth the display in exactly the direction the figure is trying to measure.

**MULTIPLE COMPARISONS:** No concern — no inferential annotations, p-values, or pairwise brackets are present. Nothing to correct.

**TIME SERIES:** Concern — Three points. (1) The record is presented as 600 s of continuous data; state explicitly whether any segments were dropped, interpolated, or artefact-rejected, since gaps would be invisible in both the trace and the spectrograms. (2) Windows are 2 s stepped by 1 s, i.e. 50 % overlap; adjacent spectrogram columns are statistically dependent, which makes ictal features look temporally smoother and more coherent than the independent-window resolution supports. This should be noted so readers do not read column-to-column continuity as evidence. (3) The time axes are not aligned across panels: (a) extends visibly further right than (b–d), so the shaded seizure intervals in (a) do not sit vertically above the corresponding spectrogram columns. This breaks the intended vertical reading of the figure.

**ISSUES:**
- [BLOCKING] Panel (a) time axis is wider than (b–d), so vertical correspondence between the shaded seizure intervals and the spectrogram features is broken. In a stacked time-aligned figure this is a genuine misreading hazard.
- [BLOCKING] Image interpolation in (b–d) is unspecified. If any smoothing interpolation (bilinear/gouraud) was used, it confounds the very comparison the figure makes between unsmoothed and smoothed estimators. Confirm nearest-neighbour / flat shading and state it.
- [BLOCKING] Colour-scale saturation is undisclosed. Broad regions of (c) and (d) sit at the 45 dB ceiling; the reader cannot tell whether "identical rendering" of the two smoothed estimators is a property of the estimators or an artefact of clipping both to the same ceiling.
- [ADVISORY] ±500 µV clipping in (a) is disclosed in the caption but not marked in the panel; clipped samples are visually indistinguishable from unclipped peaks.
- [ADVISORY] The paired (same-record) nature of the estimator comparison is not exploited; "render it identically" is asserted rather than shown.
- [ADVISORY] Seizure shading is absent from (b–d).
- [ADVISORY] 50 % window overlap induces temporal dependence between adjacent columns; not noted.
- [ADVISORY] n = 1 record/channel/subject is not stated as an illustrative example; the generalising phrasing of the caption slightly outruns the evidence shown.
- [ADVISORY] Sampling rate, filter settings, and any decimation used to draw (a) are not reported.

**SUGGESTED FIXES:**
- Force a single shared x-limit across all four panels (e.g. `ax.sharex` or explicit `set_xlim(0, 600)` on every axis) and verify alignment by dropping a faint vertical guide at 300 s through all panels.
- Set `pcolormesh(..., shading='flat')` or `imshow(..., interpolation='nearest')` and state the choice in the caption, so the 0.5 Hz × 1 s native resolution of the estimate is what the reader sees.
- Add `extend='both'` arrowheads to the colourbar, or raise the ceiling until saturation disappears, and report the actual data range in the caption (e.g. "values span 6–58 dB; scale clipped to 10–45 dB for display").
- Mark clipped samples in (a) with red tick marks or a shaded band at ±500 µV, and widen the y-limit slightly beyond the clip level so the flat tops are visibly flat by construction.
- Replicate the two seizure shadings as semi-transparent overlays or as top-edge bars on (b–d).
- Add a fifth panel showing (c) − (d) in dB on a diverging, zero-centred map, plus one or two vertical spectral slices (one ictal, one interictal) overlaying the three estimates with their ν-based confidence intervals. This converts the "identical" claim from visual assertion into a paired, quantitative comparison.
- Add to the caption: sampling rate, filter band, any decimation used for the trace, confirmation that the 600 s are continuous with no rejected or interpolated segments, the 50 % window overlap and its effect on column independence, and an explicit statement that this is one illustrative record (n = 1 clip, 1 channel).

---

## Cross-Figure Consistency Orchestrator

---
BLOCKING ISSUES (must resolve before submission):

1. **Legend placement convention is split roughly 50/50 across the set.** Outside-below-axes: efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, figure1 (three separate blocks), figure3, figure4a–c, figure7. Inside-axes: efigure4 (b, d, e), figure2 (a, e), figure4d, figure5 (b, c, d), figure6 (a, e), figure8 (f only, serving d as well). figure9 has none. A reader cannot form a habit about where to look, and the inside-axes instances are precisely the ones that overlap data. Pick one rule — recommend a single shared, frameless legend below each figure (the efigure1/efigure2/efigure3/figure7 pattern, which is already the cleanest in the set) — and apply it to all 13. — affects: [all figures]

2. **Panel-label placement is nominally uniform but geometrically inconsistent, and collides in 10 of 13 figures.** Style is correct and consistent everywhere (bold, lowercase, parenthesised, top-left), but the vertical/horizontal offset relative to the axes box differs *between rows of the same figure* (figure2 top row sits above the title line, bottom row sits on the title baseline; same in figure6) and *between figures* (efigure1 (a) is set noticeably further from its axes than (b); figure8 left-column labels drift inward over the y-axis while right-column labels sit on the title baseline). Define one offset in axes-fraction coordinates and apply it globally so no label can touch a title or a rotated y-label. — affects: [efigure3, efigure4, figure1, figure2, figure3, figure4, figure5, figure6, figure7, figure8]

3. **figure2 and figure6 are explicitly parallel figures (figure6's caption says "the kernels, as in Fig. motif") but use four different templates for the same row.** (i) Kernel rendering: figure2e is a solid grey *fill*, figure2f–h and figure6e–h are lines. (ii) The |f| ≤ W band appears in figure2g,h only but in all of figure6e–h. (iii) The blue half-power-width bar above the peak appears in figure2c,d only but in all of figure6a–d. (iv) Duplicate y-tick labels are correctly suppressed outside column 1 in figure2 but printed in all four columns of figure6. Two figures the reader is asked to read as the same object must be drawn by the same code path. — affects: [figure2, figure6]

4. **figure5 is a strict subset of efigure4 (rows 1 and 3 are the same panels) but is rendered at a different scale, so identical panels print at different type sizes and with different label collisions.** Decide which is canonical, generate both from one script at one nominal point size, and state the parent/child relationship in both captions. — affects: [figure5, efigure4]

5. **Equivalent typographic and numeric elements do not print at equal size or in equal style across the set.** Canvas widths range 1832 px (figure8) to 2172 px (efigure1), so the same nominal 8 pt label prints ≈6.5 pt in figure8 and ≈7.3 pt in efigure1 at one reproduction width. In parallel, ν is formatted inconsistently for the *same* estimator at the *same* operating point: "ν = 2.0 / 8.9 / 50.0" (figure2) vs "ν = 2 / 9 / 50" (figure3); "ν = 17" (figure1) vs "ν = 17.2" (figure6, efigure1, efigure2, figure5g); "ν = 14" (efigure2) vs "ν = 14.0" (efigure1, figure6, figure7). Fix the render width per figure and the decimal convention for ν set-wide. — affects: [all figures]

6. **The set has no uncertainty convention at all, yet ν-based noise claims are made in 10 figures.** No figure displays a confidence band, χ²_ν interval, or ensemble dispersion — including figure3, which is the one figure with an ensemble (300 realizations). This is internally consistent but leaves every quantitative claim uncalibrated. Adopt one convention (e.g. a χ²_ν 95 % interval drawn identically — same bar geometry, same stroke, same placement — in figure2, figure6, figure7, figure8, efigure2) and state in every caption either that interval or that the quantity is deterministic. Do not solve it differently in different figures. — affects: [figure1, figure2, figure3, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9, efigure2]

ADVISORY ISSUES (strongly recommended):

1. **Blue and gold are reserved for multitaper and Hann in eight figures, then reused for unrelated variables in three.** blue = noise / gold = bias (figure3b); blue = 1 − λ_k / orange dashed = 2NW (figure4d); blue and orange dashed = pre-ictal/ictal time markers, orange = ictal trace (figure8a,b). Each is locally legible but erodes a mapping the rest of the set maintains well. Recolour these three to neutrals (black solid/dashed, grey) and keep blue/gold exclusively for estimators. — affects: [figure3, figure4, figure8, and by implication figure1, figure2, figure5, figure6, figure7]

2. **Heavy strokes and greys carry different meanings in different figures.** Heavy black = true PSD (figure2, figure6, efigure2a) but also = zero reference line (efigure2b, figure7b) and = raw record (figure3a dashed taper, figure8a, figure9a). Thick grey = "the reference estimate" (figure1a) where thick black means "truth" elsewhere. Make zero-reference rules thin grey everywhere, and either give figure1's reference a colour or state the grey-reference convention in its caption. — affects: [figure1, figure2, figure3, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9, efigure2]

3. **Kernel-panel axis ranges and floors differ between figures showing the same object.** dB floor: −80 (figure2e–h, figure6e–h, efigure4m–p) vs −100 (efigure1b). Offset x-range: ±8/N (figure4b,c), ±24/N (efigure1b), ±25/N (efigure4m–p), ±40/N (figure2e–h, figure6e–h). Standardise the floor, and where a wider range is scientifically needed, add an explicit in-panel note so the reader does not assume a shared abscissa. — affects: [efigure1, figure2, figure4, figure6, efigure4]

4. **Axis styling diverges between line panels and image panels, and between the two spectrogram figures.** All line panels use the two-spine despined style (good, consistent); the spectrograms in figure8 and figure9 carry four-sided frames. figure9's time axis shows a 0 s tick, figure8's spectrograms start their labels at 100 s; figure9's colourbar spans only (b)–(c) while figure8's sits in the inter-column gutter aligned to (e) — yet both captions assert a shared 10–45 dB scale spanning all panels. Harmonise spines, tick sets, and colourbar extent between the two EEG figures. — affects: [figure8, figure9]

5. **Descriptive in-panel titles are present in eight figures and absent in five.** Present: figure2, figure3, figure4, figure5, figure6, figure8, figure9, efigure4. Absent (all information in legend/caption): efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, figure1, figure7. Whichever convention survives the journal's no-titles-inside-the-figure rule, apply it uniformly; at present the reader learns the method from a panel header in figure6 and from a legend entry in figure7 for the same three estimators. — affects: [all figures]

6. **Line weight for the same estimator is not constant.** The multitaper trace is drawn markedly thicker than its companions in figure8d,f but at equal weight in figure7a; the "average/Welch" trace is dashed in figure1a but solid in figure6d, figure7a, figure8d,f. Fix one stroke width and one dash pattern per estimator across the set. Markers appear only in efigure3 and figure1c — confirm identical marker size and edge weight in those two. — affects: [figure1, figure6, figure7, figure8, efigure3]

7. **The design bandwidth convention changes between figures without being expressed in common units.** W = 4/N in figure1–figure7 and efigure1–efigure4; figures 8 and 9 use W = 1.0 Hz with 2-s windows at 200 Hz, i.e. W = 2/N, and NW drops from 4 (K = 7) to 2 (K = 3). Both are consistent with the paper's K = 2NW − 1 rule, but a reader tracking "the box of half-width W" across the set will assume 4/N. State W in Rayleigh units (units of 1/N) in figures 8 and 9 as well. — affects: [figure7, figure8, figure9]

8. **Visual weight is unbalanced within the extended-data set.** efigure1 and efigure2 each carry ~35 % dead canvas between two small panels, while efigure4 is a dense 16-panel matrix and efigure3 is three tightly packed panels with clipped y-labels. The four extended figures do not read as one family, and efigure1/efigure2 look under-finished beside figure8/figure9. Close the inter-panel gutters in efigure1/efigure2, let the panels grow, and target a common panel height across the extended set. — affects: [efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, efigure4]

9. **Cross-reference self-sufficiency and extended-figure ordering.** figure8's caption forward-references figure9 for its own colour scale (state the 10–45 dB range in both). figure1's caption defers the definition of ν to a later section, and figure7 and figure6 both defer to "the record/epoch of Fig. X". Separately, verify that extended-data numbering matches order of first citation: efigure4 is the companion to figure5 (early in the narrative) while efigure1–efigure3 support figure2/figure6 and Table tab:dof; as numbered, the extended set is not in citation order. The main-figure sequence itself (identity → kernel → bandwidth → box → banks → synthetic comparison → EEG agreement → seizures) is logical and needs no reordering. — affects: [figure1, figure5, figure6, figure7, figure8, figure9, efigure1, efigure2, efigure3, efigure4]

10. **Minor wording/capitalisation drift.** Sentence-case is used consistently for axis titles, legends and panel headers throughout (good), with correct capitalisation of Hann, Welch, Slepian, Bohman, Gaussian, Thomson. Remaining deviations: "vs" (figure3 panel header) vs "versus" (efigure3 caption); unit hyphenation "2-s windows" (figure8, figure9) vs "1.5 s" (figure7) vs "0.70-s segments" (figure8); and three phrasings for the kernel ordinate normalisation — "kernel, linear (units of N)" (efigure1), "kernel (unit area)" (figure4c), "|U_k(f)|² (unit-norm tapers)" (figure4b). — affects: [efigure1, efigure3, figure3, figure4, figure7, figure8, figure9]

OVERALL ASSESSMENT:
  Publication readiness: **Major revisions**

  This is a genuinely coherent figure set: one sans-serif family throughout, a consistent Okabe-Ito-style estimator palette (blue = multitaper, green = untapered + box, gold = Hann + box, pink = Welch), viridis for all ordered indices and images, a shared 10–45 dB colourbar across figures 8 and 9, harmonised axis wording ("frequency offset (units of 1/N)", "kernel (dB re peak)"), consistent lowercase panel-label style, and internally consistent ν values for identical estimators (17.2, 14.0, 8.9) across figure6, figure7, efigure1 and efigure2. The narrative order is logical and needs no resequencing.

  What blocks submission is convention discipline rather than science: legend placement and panel-label offsets are decided figure-by-figure rather than set-wide, the two explicitly parallel figures (figure2 and figure6) are drawn from different templates, figure5 and efigure4 duplicate panels at different render scales, ν is formatted three different ways for the same operating point, and no figure anywhere displays uncertainty despite ν-based claims in ten of them. Combined with the per-figure clipping and collision defects and the required PNG→vector re-export already flagged upstream, the practical path is to regenerate the entire set from one shared style module — fixed canvas widths, one rcParams block, one palette table, one legend/panel-label placement rule, one kernel-panel template, one uncertainty convention — rather than patching figures individually.
